## Summary

The HUD & Suit Layer is the player's own screen: a few small opaque plates at the edges of the picture that show what the suit knows about its wearer (the air in the room, the reserve breath, the suit's soundness, warmth and lamp charge), what the hands hold, and, only over a lit object, one key and one verb with a thin bar that fills while a long job runs. It exists because the suit, the interaction rules and the Perception Filter are all headless, and because the game's governing rule (the HUD is a suit, the ship is an instrument) needs one owner so that no other system invents its own readouts. It draws, decides nothing, and keeps two registers apart: a frame that never lies, and six readings that can lie only once Vertical Slice A switches the Filter on.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: Presentation · Priority: MVP · Key deps: Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Suit & Equipment, Perception Filter, Player Controller & Input (the four index dependencies, all Hard); Interaction System (Hard, prompts and bar; missing from the index row, reported to the producer, never edited here); Command & Simulation Layer (Hard, `PRESENTATION` subscription); Save/Load & Versioned Format (Soft, `save_status` only)

## Overview

HUD & Suit Layer is the presentation system that owns **layer 2** of the render pipeline: the player's own screen. In one sentence, it is a scene-tree-side, `PRESENTATION`-class drawing system in the root viewport that, every render frame, draws what five upstream systems publish about the local seat — the critical strip (the breath dial: the ambient O2 arc and numeral, or the reserve's while the suit breathes from it, HS-12), the suit panel (reserve, integrity, warmth, charge and the lamp switch), the hand rail (three tools, the glowstick count, the carried module, the scanner reading slot), the warning marks, the interaction prompt plate with its linear channel bar, the failed-write notice and the first-frame ship-log line — and owns the Godot `Theme`, the shared component set and the lying-reading drawing slots (gleam, motion signature, sound request) that the Perception Filter parameterises. The rail holds three tools at MVP in a four-slot geometry (the suppressant leaves the MVP kit, X6), the warning marks are six slots (five suit rungs and the one-bit ship alarm, D89), and the first-frame line also fires once after a death restore (D15). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* It creates **no simulation state**: no block, hook, command, mutator, reading key, RNG stream or `INPUT_SURFACE`. Everything it decides is *how* a value looks; every *when* — a rung and its hold, whether a prompt exists, how long a bar is, whether a reading lies, how many writes failed — is cited from the system that owns it.

**How the player meets it.** Not as a menu and not as a dashboard but as **a visor**: a few small opaque plates at the edges of the picture that never move, scale, tint or flash, with a few thin readings moving inside them. At the bottom centre sits a strip with a dial arc and a three-digit number — the suit's own reading of the air in the compartment the wearer stands in, and, once the suit is breathing its reserve, the seconds of reserve left (HS-12). At the bottom left sits a small panel for the things the suit carries about itself: how many seconds of reserve breath remain, how sound the suit is, how warm, how much charge the chest lamp has and whether it is switched on. At the bottom right sits the hand rail: the three tools, how many glowsticks are left, and what is held in the hands. Over a lit object in the world, and only there, a small plate shows one key and one verb; when the player commits to a long job, that plate grows a thin linear bar that fills one whole pixel at a time. Once in a rare while a small red-and-white mark appears beside a reading (or, for a ship alarm, in its own slot on the panel), pulsing in step with the ship's beacon (held solid at the first-launch default, HS-23). There is nothing else: no minimap, no objective, no room name, no health bar, no sanity or Attention indicator under any setting. The numbers are a **second opinion** — the body (breath, a click at the collar, a fogging visor) and the room (a leaking pipe, a frosted wall) speak first. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**What the game loses without it.** Suit & Equipment is headless: it "has no screen of its own", and its five readings, its tool roster and its counts reach no eye. Interaction is headless: it draws none of its prompts or its bar. The concept's single hypothesis — a 30-minute solo boarding-then-breach session that is tense and absorbing — needs the player to read their own body and to be told what a press will do. Pillar 4 loses its only cheap falsifier at MVP: the honest `suit.o2` numeral is the "second instrument" that exposes a lying wall gauge, and it is the HUD that shows it. And the art bible's governing rule — *the HUD is a suit, the ship is an instrument* — has no owner, so every other presentation system would invent its own readouts.

**Two registers, kept apart (HS-3).** The **frame register** — plates, bezels, rivets, keycaps, labels, prompts, the channel bar, rail glyphs and counts, the failed-write notice, the first-frame line — is fed nothing by the Filter, is bit-identical with the Filter on or off, and can never gleam, glide, tick-lie or go stale. The **reading register** is exactly six node families: the five suit readings (`suit.o2`, `suit.reserve`, `suit.integrity`, `suit.warmth`, `suit.charge`) and the scanner reading slot. Their glass, needle, fill and numeral pixels are the only lie-capable pixels in screen space, reached only through `perceived(SUIT_KEY(k), PlayerId)` and never through `true_value`. At MVP all six are honest (`READING_LIE_STAGE` is "never" for every key, and the scanner slot is an honest anchor whose own lie gate is `SCANNER_LIE_STAGE`, default never, stage 3 only); they are built with their dormant lie slots so Vertical Slice A can enable a lie without a redraw.

**Index wording, reconciled.** The index (rationale bullet 21) reads: "HUD & Suit Layer (UI, MVP, explicit). Centre-bottom critical strip, bottom-left suit panel, hand rail, warning mark, tri-modal lying-reading signatures, HR-1..15. *Why this tier:* How the player reads their own body; Section 7 of the art bible specifies it." This GDD honours every noun of it and refines three: (1) "warning mark" is realised as one asset instantiated in per-reading slots beside the reading it concerns, not as a single object that "lives on the critical strip" (HS-21; the art bible 7.2 wording amendment is owed, Cross-References); (2) "tri-modal lying-reading signatures" is built at MVP as dormant, tested slots, with the charge cell's motion signature still open (Open Questions); (3) the reserve reading is added to the screen-space set (the art bible's HR-3 amendment is owed). **Numbering, once and for all:** this system is **Systems Enumeration row 21** and **Recommended Design Order 24**. The row marked `| 24 |` in the Systems Enumeration is Adaptive Audio & Tells, a different system.

**The MVP slice and what waits.** **MVP (this GDD, playable solo):** the critical strip with the breath dial arc (ambient O2, or the reserve while it is engaged), pivot cap, three-digit numeral and one reserved mark slot; the suit panel with reserve (whole seconds), integrity (zone-band dial, no numeral), warmth (zone-band dial, no numeral), charge (cell fill) and the lamp-switch state; the hand rail (three tool slots in a four-slot geometry, glowstick 0-4, carried-module glyph, scanner reading slot, MVP-live and honest: an anchor per Sanity SN-19); the warning marks: five suit-rung slots plus the one-bit ship-alarm mark `suit.alarm` on its own panel slot (six slots, D89); the prompt plate (ENABLED and NEEDS_TOOL), the press acknowledgement, the linear bar at three length classes, hold-to-confirm; the failed-write notice (MVP-live on Save/Load's published `fail_escalated` read, HS-46) and the first-frame ship-log line (UI-7 after a fallback load; one plain record line after a `DEATH_STUB` restore, D15); the glyph-device swap; the `Theme` and shared components; the `HudSettings` hooks at their defaults; the dormant lie slots driven only by a stub Filter in tests. **Specified but dormant at MVP:** the gleam, glide, tick/stale and sound-request slots on all six reading families. **Vertical Slice A:** the suppressant tool returns to the fourth rail slot, with its charge pips, in the slice that adds its effect verb (X6); reading lies enabled through `READING_LIE_STAGE` (warmth first, then O2 and charge, then integrity and reserve), the scanner reading lying only behind `SCANNER_LIE_STAGE` (Sanity's, registered, default never, stage 3 only), the charge-cell motion signature, and the mark inheriting a lying reading's class. **Vertical Slice B:** the crew strip, chat and caption anchors (reserved, drawn by other systems), the own-identity glyph on the suit panel, the one-bit harm prompt on the ordinary plate, the name-reveal component. **Alpha:** the accessibility settings screens, HUD opacity (a hook at 100 % until then), pattern strength, captions. Nothing in this list adds a progression, an inventory screen, a weapon or a meter. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**What this GDD does not own** (scope guard, enforced as rules): the vitals ladder, rungs and hold times (Suit); whether, when and for whom a reading lies (Filter, Sanity); which verb a press means, when a prompt exists and how long a channel runs (Interaction, Controller); the layers, `H`, `world_to_hud`, `present_time`, pause rendering and the picture rect (Render Pipeline); the pause panel, web map and menus (Chart Table); every sound (Adaptive Audio); the failure count that raises the save notice (Save/Load); alarm eligibility and band tables (Life-Support Graph).

*Implementation questions raised while designing and deferred to ADRs (-> `/architecture-decision`): (A) the widget approach — a `Control` scene tree versus one custom-draw surface per plate, and the `Theme` structure; (B) the two-clock policy for layer 2 (`present_time` for animation, the committed tick `T` for the mark, process time for layer 3); (C) the update model (a per-frame `HudModel` snapshot versus per-widget polling versus dirty-key subscription); (D) the bitmap instrument-face import pipeline and tabular-numeral enforcement; (E) the render-time clock source for the hold bar under pause; (F) the explicit per-frame presenter order (camera solver, Avatar Presenter, Filter, HUD, Lighting) so that `world_to_hud` and `perceived` are read after their producers; (G) the HUD maths as pure static functions so a headless test run can assert them. No ADR exists yet (`docs/architecture/` holds only `tr-registry.yaml`). The pinned engine reference (`docs/engine-reference/godot/`; domain file `modules/ui.md`, with `input.md`, `rendering.md` and `breaking-changes.md`) has **no `scripting.md` and no `core.md`**: GDScript typing, `_process` ordering, signal timing, canvas batching, text-measurement calls, cursor modes and bitmap-font import are NOT SOURCEABLE from it and are asserted nowhere in this GDD.*

## Player Fantasy

> **Design test first (P1):** *A hidden stat vs. a visibly leaking pipe → the pipe.* On a screen this reads: **if a vital can reach the player as a body cue or as a number, the body cue comes first and the number is its second opinion.** The HUD is the number.

**Framing: direct.** The player experiences this system directly: it is the glass in front of their own eyes. *(creative-director consulted; three framings weighed and logged: A "The Visor" — a still frame you wear — is the headline, because it is the art bible's own governing rule, 7.0 "the HUD is a suit, the ship is an instrument", turned into a feeling; B "The Second Opinion" — the free rung of the falsifier ladder — is adopted as the HUD's role and cost layer, not its headline, because a headline of "the HUD checks the world" would make the numeral the fantasy and the numeral is the one surface architected to lie; C "The Survival Dashboard" — every vital as a bar or icon in a corner — is rejected: the art bible rejected it by name for violating Principle 2, the Suit rejected the moodle strip, and the user chose the centre-bottom strip over a corner cluster. A fully diegetic Dead-Space-style readout was rejected upstream and is not reopened.)*

> It is the only light that is not in the world. A strip of dark glass at the bottom of your vision with your breath on it; a small panel at your left hip; your hands at your right. It does not glow, it does not shake, it does not tell you where you are or what is coming. It tells you about you.
>
> In the dark of a dead ship it is the one thing that stays exactly where it was. That is what it is for. When the hatch blows and the beam swings and your breath goes ragged, the frame has not moved a pixel, and the numeral on it is falling one step at a time. You read it without looking away from the cone.
>
> You trust it the way you trust a gauge you have watched agree with a pipe. Which is to say: you check.

**The emotion named: held composure.** Not comfort and not power. The HUD's pleasure is *steadiness under fear* — the art bible's hardest test, "frightened, still reading", made into the player's experience. Its dread is quieter and arrives later (Vertical Slice A): the steadiest thing on screen is also the closest thing to you that can lie. The Suit names the body's emotion *competent fragility*; the HUD is the composure that lets the player act on it. The two fantasies are laid side by side, not stacked: the Suit owns the body ("The Body Is the Last Instrument"), the HUD is the screen side of the same moments, and this section restates none of the Suit's.

**MDA mapping (concept ranking).** *Challenge* (2): the HUD serves reading competence — learning the three motion signatures (settle = honest change, tick = honest digit, glide / continuous tick = lie) is a mastery the game never explains in text. *Discovery* (1): served thinly and negatively — the HUD never shows what the world should reveal (no minimap, no room name, no ship state), so discovery stays in the beam. *Fantasy* (4): "we are the only ones awake" is helped by an interface that says nothing about the web. *Sensation* (6): restraint is the sensation — a still frame against a moving beam. *Self-determination:* Competence (the zone bands and the numeral are learnable and honest at MVP); Autonomy (the HUD never instructs: no objective, no tutorial overlay); Relatedness (Vertical Slice B: the crew strip carries no vitals, so checking on a friend means lighting them, not reading a bar).

### Pillar table

| Pillar | Role | How this system serves it | Design test it carries |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P1 The World Never Lies** | **PRIMARY** | The frame is an anchor, fed nothing by the Filter. The five readings are the only lie-capable surface in screen space. Everything else — prompts, bar, rail glyphs and counts, notices — is meta-register truth. No ship state, no minimap, no banner ("COOLANT LOW" is the banner Principle 2 rejected). The honest cross-check for a suit reading is the body and the room, never a second HUD element. | The pipe over the stat; the body cue first, the number its second opinion. |
| **P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality** | **PRIMARY (co-primary)** | Hosts the lie slots on all five readings and the scanner slot: violet gleam on the glass edge, one motion signature, a sound request — dormant at MVP. Carries no sanity meter, bar, ring, number, icon or colour anywhere, under any setting. The honest `suit.o2` numeral is the MVP's second instrument. | No lie is unfalsifiable; at least one channel of truth remains (on the visor: the body and the room). |
| **P2 Dread Before Chaos** | Serves | The mark fires only at CRITICAL after the hold (or at `CELL_WARN_S`), never at STRAINED, never first; one alarm rhythm shared with the beacon. Prompts and hold-to-confirm never shake, flash or announce. | The HUD is never the first channel of a failure. |
| **P3 The Web Watches** | Serves (negatively) | No Attention, noise, light-level, illumination, exposure, horror, proximity or direction indicator under any setting. Prompts exist only where Interaction says the object is lit, so "reach for it" stays a free falsifier. | A meter vs a world tell: the tell. |
| **P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total** | Serves (through loss) | No death screen, no score, no restore beat beyond one plain ship-log line. A restore draws the readings at their restored values; the single record line (no death wording, HS-46) is the only trace *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. | Failure costs ship and body; the HUD adds no punishment text. |

### Three design tests the section must be able to defend

| # | Design test | Pillar served | Applied to the HUD |
|---|---|---|---|
| **HS-DT-1** | **The body first.** *A vital that reaches the player only as a reading vs one that reaches them first as a body cue → the body cue first; the reading is its second opinion.* | P1: "Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth. Only instruments and perception can be wrong." Inherits the Suit's design test 1 by citation. | The HUD never carries a vital the body does not also carry (the one deliberate exclusion is integrity STRAINED, which the Suit carries by the zone band alone). No HUD element is the first channel of anything that can kill. |
| **HS-DT-2** | **Who produced the number decides where it is drawn.** *A value the ship produced vs one the suit produced vs one nobody in the fiction produced → world / visor / plate.* | P1 + P3: "Attention is shown in the world ... never as a hidden meter." | Ship state goes to the world, lit to be read. Suit telemetry goes to the visor, in the reading register (lie-capable). Prompts, keycaps, the bar and the notices are meta, in the plate register (never lie-capable). A proposed HUD element that cannot name its producer is cut. |
| **HS-DT-3** | **Still frame, moving reading.** *A HUD that moves, flashes, tints or rescales with the game's state vs a frame that never moves and readings that move inside it → the still frame.* | P4: the lie leaks *on a reading*, never in the frame. P2: the mark has one rhythm. | Anything that moves the frame (shake, jolt, scale, blur, vignette, tint) is a defect, not a style choice. This is what makes a gliding needle readable rather than merely unsettling. |

*Validation proposal (playtest, not a constant):* HS-DT-1 holds if, in the MVP door-breach playtest, at least 6 of 8 testers asked "what told you the air was going?" answer with the breath or the valve click before the numeral (sound arm) and at least 4 of 8 in the muted arm, each judged on its own bar (OQ-HS-86) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: "most" had no N)*. If they answer "the number", the body cues are under-mixed or the HUD is too loud (route to the audio-director and ux-designer). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The test needs a muted arm:** the fantasy depends on audio body cues coming first, yet the concept excludes adaptive audio from the MVP while the systems index lists it (row 24), so a failure could be misattributed to the HUD. The playtest therefore runs with sound and muted, each judged against its own pass criterion, and all three body cues (breath cadence, valve click, visor fog) ship in the MVP (resolved by user ruling, OQ-HS-67). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

### Anchor moment (solo MVP): the door breach, from the screen's side

This reuses the Suit's door-breach timeline so the two GDDs describe one moment, not two. All times are the Suit's illustrative values; the HUD adds no timing of its own. The keys are real.

| t (s) (Suit's table) | Body / world (not the HUD's) | What the visor shows | The feeling targeted |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | hatch latch; draught, hiss | Nothing new. The O2 numeral (`perceived(SUIT_KEY(suit.o2), PlayerId)`, honest at MVP) begins to **tick** down at its hold grid; the strip's dial arc **settles** after each change. No mark, no text, no colour change. | The world speaks first. The HUD's first act is to stay quiet. |
| ~2.5 | breath quickens (STRAINED, the first body cue) | The needle crosses a zone tick on the arc. Still no mark: STRAINED never fires the mark. | "My breathing changed — the number agrees." The number is the second opinion. |
| ~6.4 | **valve click**, breath goes close and dry, visor fog | **The strip's breath dial swaps to the reserve** (`breath_src` becomes RESERVE, HS-12), on the render frame the HUD reads the new source: the numeral now reads whole seconds of reserve (starting at the reserve level, `RESERVE_S` 90 when full), the needle re-targets to permille of `RESERVE_S` and settles, the engraved tank tick and warn notch light and the dkPa zone ticks dim to Graphite; the ambient O2 numeral moves to the panel's ambient slot and keeps ticking on the same grid; the panel's reserve cell shows the same seconds and its underlay. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; Resolved 2026-10-27 — re-review, G2; the earlier row kept the ambient reading on the strip through the reserve leg, which left a dead ambient 0 at the glance point while the number that keeps you alive sat in the periphery)* | The click told you; the dial you are already reading now tells you how long. |
| ~12.5 (step 125) | The Graph reports `alarm_eligible` for ATMO_THIN (severity 4) once the compartment's 100-step fairness window has run (Graph D.11 Example B, the DOOR-BREACH fixture: THIN opens the window at step 25, so the gate opens at step 125), over the seat's body (Graph LS-13 (c): all loops of all sections of the body the seat stands on) | The sixth mark enters the panel's alarm slot (HS-25), before any breath mark. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the timeline had not been re-run after D89)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: the Graph's body-scope rule answers OQ-HS-79 (1), so the row is no longer conditional)* | It is the first Alarm Red of the sortie and the breath mark is the second. "Rarity is the signal": the HUD never shows a mark the Suit and Graph have not published. |
| ~76 | stagger, walk-only (CRITICAL after `RUNG_MIN_TICKS`) | **The warning mark** appears beside the strip's breath dial, which is showing the reserve (the breath rung is CRITICAL, HS-12, HS-22) and, because the reserve is engaged, also beside the reserve reading on the panel (HS-22); both pulse in the one beacon phase (`BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`); at the first-launch default (reduce flashing ON, D79) they are held solid instead (HS-23). It is the second Alarm Red of the sortie, after the panel's ship-alarm mark (row ~12.5). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | Rarity is the signal. Frightened, still reading. |
| ~141 | DEAD (`DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`) | Readings freeze. No death screen, no score, no text. | Death is a bitch. It happens. |
| restore | Save/Load restarts at the last autosave | On the first `RUNNING` frame the readings are drawn at their restored values: no settle, no abort frame, no notch; one plain ship-log line (no death wording, HS-46) is the only trace of the restore. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | You lost the interval, not the lesson (P5). The HUD does not grieve for you. |

**The second anchor — the HUD's best MVP beat, and it costs nothing (Framing B).** The MVP's one hallucination class is false states on **ship** instruments. On a low-sanity run, a derelict's wall O2 gauge, lit in your beam, sits steady in its normal zone. Your breath is quick and shallow. Your strip numeral reads a value inside THIN and is still ticking down. The suit is honest at MVP (`READING_LIE_STAGE` "never"), so the strip **is** the second instrument that falsifies the wall (Sanity's rung table: an honest `suit.o2` or `suit.warmth` reading of the seat's current compartment is free to read). You believe your own glass and your breath together, and you walk out. *Design consequence carried into Acceptance Criteria:* `displayed_value == view.o2_pp_dkpa == compartment p_o2` in a THIN fixture. If the HUD is ever the one that is wrong at MVP, the MVP's single hallucination class has no cheap check.

### Under hallucination the fantasy deepens, it does not break (Vertical Slice A; dormant at MVP)

The split is the art bible's 7.0 table applied to the screen. The left column is guaranteed by construction (fed nothing by the Filter; bit-identical frame with the Filter on or off). The right column reaches the HUD only through `perceived(SUIT_KEY(k), PlayerId)` per render frame, never cached.

| Cannot lie on the HUD (frame / meta register) | Can lie (reading register; Vertical Slice A at the earliest, through the Filter only) |
|---|---|
| The frame: plates, bezels, chamfers, rivets, geometry, position, scale | The O2 numeral and the strip arc's glass (`suit.o2`) |
| Prompt plates, keycaps, verb text, anchor tab, NEEDS_TOOL notch | The reserve numeral (`suit.reserve`) |
| The linear bar with needle-mark (the actor's own elapsed time) | The integrity dial (`suit.integrity`) |
| Tool glyphs, the equipped-tool bracket and glowstick count (rail counts are anchors; the suppressant's pips return with the tool, X6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | The warmth dial (`suit.warmth`) |
| The lamp **switch** state on the charge row | The charge cell fill (`suit.charge`) |
| The failed-write notice and the first-frame line | The scanner reading slot |
| The warning mark's *shape and rhythm* | Whether a suit-rung mark shows beside a **lying** reading (it inherits its reading's class; the Graph-driven ship-alarm mark never lies, HS-30) |

**How each lie leaks (tri-modal: the "subtle is allowed; absent is not" rule from P1).** Every lying reading carries the violet gleam on its own glass (fringe `#B07BE6`, never core), one motion signature, and a sound slot (Audio's). The strip's O2 lie rides the numeral (continuous **tick** or **stale-hold**) plus the gleam on the arc glass; **glide** lives on a suit-panel dial whose needle meets the eligibility floor (`NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX`, owned by In-World Instruments).

**The deepening beat.** In a cold derelict at low sanity, the warmth dial's needle on the suit panel drifts upward — slow, smooth, never quite at rest — with a violet hairline on its glass. The frame around it has not moved. Your breath is smoking in your own cone and you are shivering. The HUD has not broken; one needle inside a still frame has detached from the body, and the stillness of everything else is exactly what lets you see it. *You believe the shiver. You go home.* That is P4's design test passed: the lie costs time and light to disprove, and it can always be disproved.

**What the player learns, never told in text:** "needles that wander are lying; needles that shake are warning; numerals that step are honest." On the visor, tremor is not used on suit readings, so the vocabulary reduces to *settle and step* (honest) versus *glide and continuous tick or stale* (lie).

**The hard edge, stated plainly.** At Vertical Slice A a lying suit reading can lie its rung mark away (a suit-rung mark inherits its reading's class, HS-30; the Graph-driven ship-alarm mark cannot be lied away). The fantasy accepts this because P4 promises "at least one channel of truth always remains", and on the visor that channel is the body (breath, click, fog, rime, stagger) and the room. The HUD must never be designed as if a suit-rung mark were an independent honest backup.

**What hallucination never does to the HUD:** no vignette, tint, blur, glitch, scan-line, shake, chromatic aberration or scale change; no sanity number, bar, ring, icon or colour; no "lies spotted" count; no true value beside a lying one outside the Debug Overlay. A screen effect says "the HUD is broken"; the pillar says "the gauge is wrong".

### Tone and language

**Inherit the Suit's use/avoid list by citation** (suit, seal, breath, reserve, valve, cell, chest lamp, beam, go dark, visor, fog, rime, shiver, stagger, the hand, the rail, stow, the haul; never *health, HP, stamina, oxygen meter, O2 bar, encumbrance, inventory, flashlight, battery* for the suit's store, *vignette, tunnel vision, blur*). **HUD vocabulary (use):** visor, glass, strip (critical strip), panel (suit panel), rail (hand rail), plate (Ground plate), bezel, chamfer, rivet, keycap, bracket, dial arc, needle, pivot cap, zone band, numeral, the mark (warning mark), settle, tick, step, glide, gleam, stale, linear bar with needle-mark, prompt plate, anchor tab, cut (for removal). **Avoid** (the stale-token lint applies to this prose too): ring / radial / wipe / pie / circle as a UI shape ("ring clamp" and "depth rings" excepted), meter for anything that is not an instrument, bar except "linear bar", icon-as-state, toast, banner, popup, notification as a UI noun, widget in fantasy prose, HP, health bar, damage number, alert, threat, awareness, light level, exposure, lit state, minimap, compass, objective, quest, and "hand point" as a beam origin (the beam origin is the chest point). **Register.** Fantasy prose: second person, present tense, terse, physical. On screen: the instrument face for numerals and frame labels; the reading face only for the two meta lines (the failed-write notice and the first-frame line). **No warning words on screen, ever** — the mark is a glyph, not text, and Alarm Red is never text. Prompt verbs are verbs, not states ("Weld", "Cut away", "Install", "Drop"). No exclamation, no heroics, no system voice addressing the player.

**Death-language deny-list (user ruling X8, no death wording on screen).** Lint list `HUD_DEATH_WORDS` (data, case-insensitive, whole-word and stem): die, dies, died, dying, dead, death, deadly, kill, killed, lose, lost, fatal, perish, expire, game over, respawn, revive (the last because solo has none at MVP). Applied to every HUD string key, in particular `plate_give_up` (English placeholder "Give up", HS-42(b)), `line_record_restored` (HS-46), `notice_save_failed`, `verb_confirm_prompt` and the self plate's `verb_patch_self` (English placeholder "Patch suit", HS-37; Interaction's key, linted here because the HUD draws it) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)*. Verbs are the Controller's action name or plain verbs; the plate says nothing about what happens next. Tested by AC-HS-147 (d). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

### Reference techniques: adopt and reject

The Suit's reference table (Pandorum, Zomboid, Metro, Dead Space, Subnautica, Barotrauma, Death Stranding, Don't Starve, Beyond the Aquila Rift) is cited, not restated. HUD-specific rows:

| Reference | Adopt | Reject |
|---|---|---|
| **Apollo Command Module main console** (the art bible's HUD touchstone) | The meter's grammar: a thin moving needle inside a static engraved housing with a marked safe zone — frame versus reading at a glance; one reserved annunciator for every alarm (our single warning mark, one rhythm). | Switch density and the continuous panel plane (our HUD is a few small plates, about 1 % non-Ground area); cool internal panel light (collides with O2 Cyan); period nostalgia. |
| **Project Zomboid** (the user's touchstone) | Restraint: the world, not the UI, is where you learn you are in trouble; discrete named states that escalate in steps; zoom in and out with a HUD that does not rescale. | The moodle column as the primary channel; any status icon for hunger, fatigue or panic; any "panic" UI response to fear. |
| **Pandorum** | The chest lamp as the player's own equipment: the HUD shows its **switch** and **charge**, never what it illuminates. | A hand-held light on the rail. |
| **Metro 2033 / Exodus** | The wrist-watch filter timer: a reading worn on the body and consulted, while breath and visor fog are the alarm. Our reserve numeral in whole seconds is that watch. | Mask-wipe and filter-swap chores. |
| **Alien: Isolation** (motion tracker) | Nothing on the HUD. Its lesson is negative: a device that tells you where the threat is replaces dread with a radar. | Any proximity, direction, threat or "they are near" surface. |
| **Dead Space** | Already rejected as a full model; adopt only the idea that the HUD belongs to the suit, not the game. | Holographic UI that floats in the world; a health spine painted on a body. |
| **Subnautica** | The O2 numeral as a plain reading of a real reserve. | The radial O2 gauge (radial is banned) and instant-blackout-at-zero presentation. |
| **Genre-default screen effects for sanity or injury** | — | Vignette, desaturation, blur, screen tint for any state. Sanity never reads off the screen's energy. |
| **Beyond the Aquila Rift** | The kind lie with one physical detail that betrays it: the warmth needle's calm drift against the shiver. | Any HUD "reveal" (glitch-out, frame cracking, text corruption) — an authored story beat and a screen effect. |

### Anti-pillar guard rails (the fantasy's limits)

1. **Not a combat game.** No health bar, hit flash, damage number, threat bar, lock-on, kill count or reticle (the one exception is the opt-in, off-by-default accessibility aim indicator, HS-52, which draws a direction only, never a target) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The suppressant (Vertical Slice; not in the MVP kit, X6) shows its charges as a reading, never as ammunition. Integrity is a dial with zone bands, never a health number.
2. **Not a freeform builder.** No inventory screen, grid, weight readout, capacity or "slots free" count, mass bar or encumbrance indicator; the rail is four tools, two counts and the carried-module glyph. The weld prompt is a verb, not a build menu.
3. **Not intentional PvP.** No crewmate vitals, sanity sign or harm counter on any HUD surface. The one-bit harm prompt (Vertical Slice B, reserved) is the actor's own honest self-knowledge drawn on the ordinary plate: no red, no tell, no roster flag.
4. **Not an authored story.** No toast, banner, log pop-up, objective, quest marker or narrative text. The only text lines are meta (the failed-write notice and the first-frame line), plain, without cause or drama.
5. **Not a game you win.** No progress indicator, completion percentage, depth score, run timer or "time aboard" counter.
6. **P3 negative contract.** No Attention, noise, light-level, illumination, exposure, horror, proximity or direction indicator under any setting, including accessibility.
7. **P4 negative contract.** No sanity meter, bar, ring, number, icon or colour, anywhere, under any setting; the only violet in screen space is the gleam on a lying reading's glass.
8. **P1 frame contract.** The frame is an anchor: never shaken, jolted, tinted, blurred, rescaled or lie-capable. Ship state is never on the HUD (no room name, no loop summary).
9. **P2 rhythm contract.** The HUD is never the first warning of anything; the mark fires only at CRITICAL (after the hold) or `CELL_WARN_S`, never at STRAINED, in one beacon phase.
10. **P5 failure contract.** No death screen, no score, no restore beat on the HUD beyond the one plain ship-log line of HS-46 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

**The forbidden-surface list** (carried as a lint into Acceptance Criteria): a minimap, compass, objective marker, quest log, off-screen arrow, crew locator, ship-status or loop summary, room name, weight / capacity / inventory, health bar, Attention / noise / light meter, sanity meter / icon / colour, horror indicator, vignette, screen-edge tint, damage number, hit flash, kill or score, death screen, progress toast, loading spinner, autosave glyph, "repaired" toast, a countdown to an event the HUD names (the reserve numeral is a plain reading of the suit's own store and names no event, HS-17), or any text in Alarm Red.

**The feel word for the whole layer is *machined*:** things step, settle and stop. Nothing bounces, pops or scales; "juicy UI" is rejected by the art bible.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

Rule ids are `HS-n`. Each rule has *Rule.* (what is true), *Why.* (the decision it serves) and, where it matters, *Edge notes.* Implementation choices are marked `-> ADR`. A locked constant or formula is used by its registry name with the value its owner gives; a number tagged `[PH]` is a placeholder owned by this GDD (registered at Phase 5), with its range in Tuning Knobs; a name tagged *(unregistered)* is cited from its owner and is never given a HUD-side value. Constants and formulas defined by name here (`strip_budget`, `hud_flash_budget`, `reserve_seconds`, `mark_phase`, `o2_dial_angle`, `hud_settle_response`, `nonground_count`, `bottom_row_fit`, `anchor_snap`, `integrity_zone_ticks`, `charge_fill_and_warn`) are given their variables, ranges and worked examples in Formulas; the neighbours' formulas are cited by their owners' ids and never renamed here: Interaction D.UI-2 (the prompt show rule), Interaction D.UI-1 (`fill_px`, the bar), the registry's `lying_digit_tick_schedule` and Pipeline D.4 (`cap_px`).

The rules are grouped by surface: **A** identity, layer and reads (HS-1 to HS-10); **B** the critical strip (HS-11 to HS-15); **C** the suit panel (HS-16 to HS-21); **D** the warning mark (HS-22 to HS-25); **E** the lie slots (HS-26 to HS-31); **F** the hand rail (HS-32 to HS-36); **G** the prompt plate and channel bar (HS-37 to HS-45); **H** meta notices, the flash arbiter, settings, reservations, layout invariants and neighbours (HS-46 to HS-57).

#### A. Identity, layer and reads

**HS-1. The HUD draws; it decides nothing.** *Rule.* The HUD is a `PRESENTATION` subscriber of the kernel's subscriber classes. It owns no block, hook, command, mutator, reading key, RNG stream or `INPUT_SURFACE`, and "the HUD's prompts do not [issue commands]". It writes only `CanvasItem`, `Material` and audio-request properties, never a block, command, camera or viewport property. Every *when* — a rung and its hold, `prompt_state`, `length_class`, the tick schedule, the lie stage, the save-failure count, alarm eligibility — is cited from its owner and never derived by the HUD. *Why.* The scope guard: the HUD is the scene-tree side of the kernel's rule that simulation systems stay scene-tree free. *Edge notes.* If a needed *when* has no owner (the ship-alarm source; the warmth band edges had no owner until SE-29 published them), the HUD raises an ASK (Open Questions) and draws nothing in the interim; it never invents the rule.

**HS-2. Layer 2, in the root viewport.** *Rule.* The HUD is a `CanvasLayer` (layer 2, group `hud`) in the **root viewport**, never inside the 640 x 360 world `SubViewport`. It receives no world light, glow, ambient, occlusion, Filter pass, camera shake or jolt, by construction (a different viewport). The HUD scale `H` is an integer in `{H_default .. 2 * H_default}` (and, at MVP, in the `offered(P_w, P_h)` subset of it, D.8) with `H_default = max(k_auto, 3)` (`HUD_MIN_SCALE` 3; the Pipeline's PR-12 form: `k_auto` is the picture's automatic world scale, not the live zoom step `k`), independent of the world scale `k` and of the zoom ladder (`k` in `[1, k_auto + 2]`); *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* a zoom step or an `H` change alters no simulation value. No HUD code reads `k` or assumes `H == k`: its only scale inputs are `H` and the picture rect. *Why.* P1: the frame is an anchor. The Pipeline's two-integer-scales-in-one-window wiring (the Steam Deck: world 2x, HUD 3x) is an MVP requirement whose wiring is NOT SOURCEABLE until the Pipeline's spike step 11. *Edge notes.* A `Control` found under the world `SubViewport` fails the scene-tree lint (Pipeline lint (b)); in a release build it would receive world light and the Filter, so this is a build gate, not a runtime check.

**HS-3. Frame register versus reading register.** *Rule.* Screen space has two registers. The **frame register** — plates, bezels, chamfers, rivets, keycaps, labels, the prompt plate, the bar, the rail glyphs and counts, the failed-write notice, the first-frame line, the focus bracket — is fed nothing by the Filter, is bit-identical with the Filter on or off, and can never gleam, glide, tick-lie or go stale. The **reading register** is exactly six node families: `suit.o2`, `suit.reserve`, `suit.integrity`, `suit.warmth`, `suit.charge` and the scanner reading slot. Only their glass, needle, fill and numeral pixels are lie-capable. **Stated exceptions, both in the mark slots** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the mark slots are not a seventh reading family but a **visibility exception**. (1) A suit-rung mark slot's *visibility* (never its sprite or clock, which are frame) inherits the class of the reading it sits beside (HS-30): at MVP it is driven by the Suit's rungs and is honest; at Vertical Slice A it may take the Filter's mark field for a lying reading (class R by inheritance). (2) The one-bit ship-alarm mark `suit.alarm` (HS-25, accepted at MVP by user ruling D89) is Filter-fed but its lie stage is pinned `never`, so it sits in a mark slot as a TRUTH READING (class T, Visual/Audio Requirements), neither frame nor lie-capable. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The Filter-immunity lint (AC-HS-42, AC-HS-88) therefore has an **allow-list of exactly the mark-slot visibility properties**; every other frame node stays fed nothing and bit-identical with the Filter on or off. A fixed dial face and its zone ticks are frame (engraving); the needle position against them is the reading. *Why.* "Meta elements are drawn in a register that never carries the gleam because they never carry a lie." Lie-capable pixels are exactly the pixels fed by `perceived(...)`, which makes the Filter-immunity lint checkable per node.

**HS-4. One read path for readings: `perceived`.** *Rule.* The HUD obtains each of the five readings by `perceived(SUIT_KEY(k), PlayerId)` every render frame and uses the returned record's `displayed_pm`, `displayed_value`, `motion_mode`, `tick_phase_ms`, `leak_amplitude_pm`, `leak_modes` and `frame_override`. It **never calls `true_value`**: HUD modules hold zero `true_value` call sites (the Filter's AC-PF-51). A `Perceived` record is never cached across frames; the per-frame `HudModel` holds values *copied this frame* and overwritten every frame, which is a model, not a cache of records. **The read fills a reusable, caller-owned record** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the HUD passes one preallocated `Perceived` struct per reading to the Filter's read (`perceived(key, viewer, out)`), so steady state allocates nothing per frame (a fresh record per reading per frame would allocate in GDScript every render frame, AC-HS-92); "never cached" still means no value is held for reuse after the frame's draw. This is an ask to the Filter (its read signature). *The Suit's and the Filter's "must repeat" clauses, restated with the correction:* the O2 numeral is the suit's `p_o2` reading; every HR-3 reading reaches the HUD only through `perceived`, which wraps the Suit's `true_value(key, player_id)` for `suit.o2`, `suit.reserve`, `suit.integrity`, `suit.warmth` and `suit.charge`; no reading is corrupted in the MVP; and the Filter may neither remove nor add a body cue, move the chest point, or edit overlay visibility. *Why.* The registry entry `perceived_query` says the Filter is the only non-diagnostic caller of the Graph's `true_value(kind, id)` and of the Suit's `true_value(key, player_id)`. This GDD adopts that wording; the Suit's SE-29 clause "reach the HUD only through `true_value`" is the stale one (refresh owed, Cross-References). *Edge notes.* A missing record (a key not yet registered) draws the reading's plate with a blank numeral and the arc at rest, counts `HUD_READ_MISSING`, and never falls back to `view()`.

**HS-5. Non-reading fields come from `Suit.view(player_id)`.** *Rule.* Allowed: the per-axis rungs and `vitals_state`, `locomotion_cap`, `equipped_slot`, tool charges, `glowstick_count`, `carried_def_id`, `beam_shape`, whether the cell exists (`CELL_ENABLED`), plus `tool_in_hand`, `stowed_count`, `carried_instance` and `tool_charge`, **and the breath source `breath_src` (the Suit's enum 0 ROOM / 1 RESERVE / 2 GASP / 3 NONE; a `view()` field, not a reading)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. `breath_src` is the HUD's one input for three presentation choices, and for nothing else: which of two readings the strip's breath dial shows (HS-12), whether the source-exists hue is O2 Cyan or Graphite lit (ROOM or RESERVE: Cyan; GASP or NONE: Graphite lit; HS-20, HSV-4), and whether the reserve cell's mark slot is also eligible (HS-22). It is a published enum, so the hue and the dial choice are frame-register decisions (class A, bit-identical with the Filter on or off, AC-HS-139) and not a corruptible reading; the HUD never derives a source from a `displayed_*` value. The four pre-wrap reading fields of `view()` (`o2_pp_dkpa`, `reserve_pm`, `cell_pm`, `integrity_pm`) are **never drawn**: a HUD that draws a number from them is a defect (the Suit's lint AC-SE-77). *Why.* A number drawn from `view()` would bypass the single wrap point (P4). *Edge notes.* In tests only, `view()` reading fields may be compared against the drawn value on the honest path (the second-instrument assertion, HS-14).

**HS-6. The read interface is closed.** *Rule.* The HUD's reads are exactly those in the table below; anything else is a scope breach.

| Read | Owner | Fields the HUD uses | Frequency | Never read |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `perceived(SUIT_KEY(k), PlayerId)` x 5, plus the one-bit `perceived(SUIT_KEY(suit.alarm), PlayerId)` for the ship-alarm mark (a TRUTH key with its lie stage pinned `never`; how the one bit sits in the record is the Filter's, OQ-HS-8; **the Filter does not yet define the key, OQ-HS-79, so this read is provisional**) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Perception Filter | the record fields of HS-4 | every render frame | the true value, the lie id or kind as content |
| `perceived(SCANNER, PlayerId)` *(MVP-live and honest: Sanity SN-19, Interaction IA-30; only what a scan shows is provisional — ASK Sanity / Interaction)* | Perception Filter | same record | while the scanner slot is shown | — |
| `Suit.view(PlayerId)` + `tool_in_hand`, `stowed_count`, `carried_instance`, `tool_charge` | Suit & Equipment | HS-5 list | discrete part: on dirty key `S/<PlayerId>`; forced rebuild list in HS-7 | the four reading fields |
| `focus_view(seat)` | Interaction | `focus_ref`, `anchor_o`, `verb_id`, `mode`, `duration_class`, `tool_required`, `prompt_state`, `verb_name_key`, `glyph_action`, `focus_seq`, and the fields Interaction's design review added (IA-41): `press_blocked_moving` (true only on CHANNEL and HOLD_CONFIRM plates; the HUD draws the keycap hollow with the verb text Paper White and never hides the plate; Interaction IA-41 as amended in its re-review, AC-IA-142) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; CD ruling on PCI re-review)*, `carry_state`, `drop_available`, `drop_anchor_o` (the second drop plate, HS-43; drawing details are the UX spec's) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, `self_target` (bool, Interaction IA-41: true when the focus is the actor's own suit, the Controller's PC-6 self-target; selects the self plate of HS-37 / HS-39, which has no `anchor_o`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)* | every render frame | `interaction.state`, `node()`, `edge()`, any `PERCEPTION_OUT` block |
| `channel_view(seat)` (local seat only) | Interaction | `owner`, `verb_id`, `state`, `transition_seq`, `result_seq`, `progress_pm`, `length_class`, `outcome`, `ended_by` | every render frame | `abort_cause` (Interaction IA-41: "diagnostic only"; the cancel drain is keyed off the non-diagnostic `ended_by` instead, HS-41) |
| `confirm_view(seat)` *(the HUD's single named read for the hold and the press; the Controller publishes it as `confirm_view = {b, pressed, released}`, pci:953, and Interaction IA-41 names the same read `input_view(seat) = {hold_pm, armed, device_class, pressed_action}`, owned by the Controller; **the two names and field sets must be reconciled by the Controller (OQ-HS-18)**, this GDD uses `confirm_view` and the union below)* | Player Controller (Adapter-local, per render frame) | `{mode, hold_pm, armed, pressed_action, released, give_up_live}`: `mode` the Controller's `confirm_mode` (`HOLD` or `TAP`, pci:552 / 882); `hold_pm` 0..1000, the D.10 bar fill `b` as permille (the Controller drains it itself at `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE`; the HUD draws it, `fill_px = (hold_pm * L) div 1000`, and does no drain of its own for a hold); `armed` the tap-confirm armed state; `pressed_action` the closed action token whose press is being acknowledged, or none; `released` true on the frame a held action is released. The Controller's published shape lacked `mode` and `armed` (Interaction's lacks `mode` and `released`): an ASK to add them (OQ-HS-18), **met on the Controller's side 2026-10-28** (PCI UI Requirements 1 now publishes `{b, mode, armed, pressed, released, give_up_live}`); the remaining reconciliation is the field names (`b` / `hold_pm`, `pressed` / `pressed_action`) and the single read name, OQ-HS-18 (1) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. **The HUD's TAP-mode relabel and armed-bracket story (HS-42) depends on the Controller story that lands those fields** (no longer blocked at design level); the ACK, hold-fill and glyph-swap stories depend only on the fields already published (`b`, `pressed`, `released`, `device`, `token`) and are not blocked. **`give_up_live`** bool: the Controller's "`loco_state == DOWNED` in solo, context `gameplay`, and no other bound seat could revive the actor (Co-op Session's gating)"; it is the HUD's only trigger for the `give_up` confirm plate (HS-42(b)); `pressed_action` may be `give_up`; the HUD never reads `loco_state` itself. ASK to the Controller: publish `give_up_live` in `confirm_view` (PCI UI Requirements 1, which as amended 2026-10-28 publishes `{b, mode, armed, pressed, released, give_up_live}`) and add it to the registry entry's `input_view` values (OQ-HS-18). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; CD ruling on PCI re-review)* | every render frame while a hold, a tap-arm or a press is live, and every render frame while `give_up_live` is true | `loco_state` |
| `glyph_view(seat, action)` *(agreed in shape: pci:953)* | Player Controller (Adapter) | `{device, token}`: `device` in {KBM, PAD}, switched under `DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS`; `token` from the Controller's **closed `keycap_token` enumeration** (one token per key, mouse button and gamepad button or axis direction its binding table can hold; a binding outside it fails the Controller's data-load lint). The `?` keycap (HS-37) is the HUD's fallback only for a token its own **atlas** lacks, never a member of the Controller's enumeration. The closed action set includes `give_up` (Backspace / View) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | on device switch | `InputEvent.device`, any comparison to `0` |
| The result-event record *(Interaction IA-41 source 4; agreed in shape)* | Interaction / kernel CR-5 projection | `{seat, command_id, queued_tick, kind}` with `kind` in {APPLIED, REJECTED, DROPPED, SUPERSEDED}, for the local seat's own commands only, carrying **no reason**; used to withdraw an ACK and to time the reject presentation | on each result | the reject reason |
| `save_status`, the fallback-load flag, `restore_reason` (a request only) | Save/Load | `IDLE / WRITING / WRITTEN / FAILED`; the consecutive-failure threshold is cited, not counted | on change | the failure count's meaning beyond the threshold |
| Committed tick `T`, `tick_alpha`, `present_time`, picture rect, `H`, `world_to_hud(pos)` | Kernel, Render Pipeline | as named | every render frame | the jolt offset; the world camera pose directly |
| O2 band edges | Life-Support Graph | `O2_BAND_ANOXIC_PP`, `HYPOXIA_PP`, `O2_BAND_THIN_PP` (registry; HS-13) | at load | any compartment state, `failures()`, `alarm_eligible` |

The HUD never reads Attention, Horror, Sanity state, the Graph's state at a distance, `peer_id`, `lit(ref)` or the beam's exposure; `prompt_state` already carries what Interaction decided about lighting. It does not read the Controller's `input_ignored_view(seat)`: that read is refused (K43). The only visual response to an ignored or rejected press is the MOVING tell above (the presser's own state) and HS-41's reject keycap hold on a plate Interaction has already shown; a press at a HIDDEN-prompt (unlit) subject draws nothing (P3, AC-HS-143). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; CD ruling on PCI re-review)* *Why.* Reflexion pattern 11: list only what the consumer must supply, never a producer's internals; Graph UI item 4 forbids ship status at a distance; P3.

**HS-7. Update model: hybrid pull.** *Rule.* Each render frame the HUD pulls the five `perceived` records, `focus_view(seat)`, `channel_view(seat)` and the Adapter reads of HS-6 (they can change every frame: glide, re-projection, bar). The seat's dirty key `S/<PlayerId>` and `save_status` changes decide only whether the **discrete** model (rungs, `vitals_state`, rail counts, `equipped_slot`, `carried_def_id`, the notice) is rebuilt. A rebuild is **forced** regardless of the key on: the first `RUNNING` frame, any restore, an `ALL`-coalesced notification (above `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256), exit from `PAUSED`, and any change of `H`, locale or glyph device. Notifications carry no state. *Why.* A coalesced `ALL` must not strand a stale reading; readings can glide so they must be pulled; the discrete rebuild is a dozen integer reads so the skip is a convenience, not a correctness dependency. -> ADR candidate C.

**HS-8. Clocks.** *Rule.* Reading settle, prompt debounce, bar fill, bar drain, tick phases and lie motion advance on `present_time`, which is frozen while the kernel is `PAUSED`. The warning-mark phase is read from the committed kernel tick `T` and interpolated by `tick_alpha` (HS-23), **and so are the honest-numeral grid (`T mod HONEST_GRID_TICKS == 0`, HS-14) and the flash arbiter's window (`(T - TICK_RATE, T]`, HS-47)**, which freeze with `T` under pause; while `PAUSED` an active mark is held ON solid instead (HS-23), the one exception to the frozen-frame rule. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the grid moved from `present_time` to `T` so it cannot drift against the mark's own `T`-locked edges)* Layer 3 (the Chart Table) runs on process time. A time step fed to any presentation timer after a pause, a window-focus loss or a hitch is clamped (`HUD_DT_CLAMP_MS` 50 `[PH]`), and no integrator divides by the time step. *Why.* The art bible 7.4 "the world simply stops"; the Pipeline's own state table says "HUD visible (frozen readings); chart table live on process time". The Pipeline's PR-9 prose "Layers 2-4 run on the engine's process time" is the stale clause (refresh owed: "Layers 3-4"; Cross-References). *Edge notes.* The Pipeline's PR-8 item 6 ("never on the tick counter") needs the matching wording refresh "never counts ticks; may read `T` and `tick_alpha` for clock-locked phases". -> ADR candidate B.

**HS-9. Integer HUD-pixel placement and primitives, at every `H` and display.** *Rule (observable).* Every element is authored in HUD px (1 HUD px = `H` screen px), anchored to an edge or the centre of the HUD canvas `picture / H` (which may be fractional: the Deck's 1280 x 800 picture at `H` 3 is 426.67 wide), and snapped by `anchor_snap` so that every edge lands on a whole HUD px. Sizes are exact integer multiples of `H`. **Coordinate space (design review, 2026-10-27):** `HudLayout` outputs **integer HUD-px rectangles relative to the HUD-root `Control`** that the pipeline root sizes to `hud_rect / H` on every `RESCALING` (Pipeline PR-12, as amended; `world_to_hud` returns coordinates in the same space). The scale `H`, the sub-`H` screen-pixel remainder (`extra_x`) and any letterbox offset are carried by that Control's placement and the `CanvasLayer` transform, not by `HudLayout`; D.9's screen-pixel terms are the conversion a test uses, not a second layout space. **The default is manual snapping in `HudLayout`** (floor every rectangle to a whole HUD px and split the remainder as D.9 says); `gui_snap_controls_to_pixels` is neither sourced nor relied on, and spikes S1 and S4 are **prerequisites of the layout / widget ADR**, not non-gating (the property, if it works, is a bonus). Layout and settings changes (`H`, locale, picture rect, `k_auto`) take effect on the **next render frame in every state, including `PAUSED_FROZEN`** (the layout is a pure function of `(picture rect, H)`; no simulation or presentation timer is advanced by it). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `Control.scale` is (1, 1) everywhere; no node animates scale or rotation; the 4.7 `Control.offset_transform_*` properties are not used for settle. Bars, strokes and dial arcs are filled rectangles or atlas regions on the art-pixel grid, not `draw_line` or `draw_arc` primitives, because 4.7 line drawing has no antialiasing feather. A needle's tip is quantised to whole HUD px, and the Filter's never-rest rule (`NEVER_REST_MAX_S` 1.0 s) must hold at that quantisation. `gui_snap_controls_to_pixels` **is set to true on the root only as a bonus if spike S1 shows it exists and takes effect on the HUD `CanvasLayer`; nothing relies on it, and no criterion asserts a HUD-px position through it** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the rule had said both "not relied on" and "= true on the root")*: the property is the Pipeline's (K21), is **NOT SOURCEABLE** in the pinned reference, and its scope is `[UNV]` (it may be viewport- or project-scoped and so may not apply to a `CanvasLayer`'s controls at all; it appears nowhere under `docs/engine-reference/godot/`; what is sourced is the 4.7 line no-feather rule, breaking-changes.md:31 and rendering.md:11, and `offset_transform` in ui.md). *Why.* HR-6 and the Pipeline's rule that human-built geometry including the HUD is never rotated at runtime (PR-4 rule 5, which lists the HUD). *Edge notes.* How a needle is produced (a pre-baked frame strip, a snapped sprite, a polygon) is -> ADR candidate A; the observable requirement is only the quantisation and the never-rest proof. Behaviour of `gui_snap_controls_to_pixels` when `H != k` and with a fractional canvas is NOT SOURCEABLE until the spike.

**HS-10. The surface inventory is closed.** *Rule.* The HUD has exactly the surfaces in the table below. Anything else is a scope breach and a lint failure.

| Surface | Anchor | Contents | Source read | Register |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hud_strip` | centre-bottom, sitting on the bottom 5 % safe margin | the **breath dial**: arc with pivot cap, three-cell numeral, mark slot; it shows the ambient O2 while `breath_src` is ROOM, GASP or NONE and the suit O2 (the reserve) while it is RESERVE (HS-12) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | `perceived(suit.o2)` in ROOM, GASP and NONE; `perceived(suit.reserve)` in RESERVE; `view` rung and `breath_src` | reading |
| `hud_suit_panel` | bottom-left, inside the margin | reserve, integrity, warmth, charge + lamp switch; per-reading mark slots; the ship-alarm mark slot (HS-22, HS-25); the **ambient-O2 slot** (a three-digit numeral field in the reserve cell, lit only while the strip shows the reserve, HS-12) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; notice slot; reserved `own_identity` slot *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | `perceived` x 4 + `suit.alarm`, `view` | reading (glass, needle, numeral, fill) + frame |
| `hud_rail` | bottom-right, inside the margin | three tool slots in a four-slot geometry (the fourth cell reserved and empty at MVP, X6), glowstick count, carried glyph, scanner reading slot, device keycaps *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | `view`, `tool_*`, glyph token read, `perceived(SCANNER)` | frame + one reading slot |
| `hud_prompt` and `hud_bar` | `world_to_hud(anchor_o)` of the focused subject; the self-plate variant is placed per HS-39's self-plate rule (outside the chest keep-out, no world anchor) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)* | plate (keycap + verb), NEEDS_TOOL variant, self-plate variant (HS-37; `verb_patch_self`), the linear bar (riding on whichever plate is drawn) | `focus_view` (including `self_target`, which feeds the self-plate variant), `channel_view`, hold state | frame (meta) |
| `hud_give_up_plate` | just outside the chest keep-out, around the projected chest point (HS-39 placement for this plate) | plate (keycap token from `glyph_view(seat, give_up)` + verb text from string key `plate_give_up`, English placeholder "Give up") with the SHORT linear bar (`BAR_LEN_SHORT_PX` 16) and needle-mark; exists only while `confirm_view.give_up_live` (HS-42(b)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | `confirm_view(seat)`, `glyph_view(seat, give_up)` | frame (meta), never lie-capable |
| `hud_notice` | at the suit panel's edge | failed-write notice; first-frame ship-log line (after a fallback load or a `DEATH_STUB` restore, HS-46) | `save_status`, boot flag, `restore_reason` (a request) | frame (meta) |
| `hud_aim_indicator` *(conditional; exists only while `aim_indicator` is on, default off)* | at a fixed radius around the projected chest point, outside the chest keep-out | one direction mark (HS-52): the Controller's published aim direction (16 steps), nothing about any target, range, light or exposure | the Controller's aim direction | meta (class M), honest, never lie-capable *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| inspect card | layer 2, content by In-World Instruments | HUD supplies the frame, `Theme` and components only | Instruments | frame HUD-owned; reading Instruments' *(designed)* |
| reserved anchors | `crew_strip` top-left, `chat` left edge below it, `caption` plate slot, `own_identity` on the panel | nothing drawn at MVP | none | anchor (Vertical Slice B) |

**Forbidden surfaces** (lint-banned under any setting or state): minimap, compass, objective, off-screen arrow, crew locator, room name, ship status or loop summary, weight / capacity / inventory / slots-free, health bar, damage number, hit flash, Attention / noise / light / illumination / exposure meter, sanity meter / icon / colour, horror indicator, threat bar, proximity indicator or direction to a Horror, beacon or breach glyph, vignette, screen-edge tint, toast, banner, popup, death screen (the `give_up` confirm plate of HS-42(b) is not a death screen and not a banner: it confirms the player's own input, shows no cause, count, countdown or consequence, and carries no death wording *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*), score, autosave glyph, "repaired" confirmation, a countdown to an event the HUD names (not the reserve numeral, HS-17), loading spinner, any text in Alarm Red, ring / radial / pie / wipe progress, and a UI cursor in `gameplay` (the cursor, reticle and aim-line bans have exactly one exception, the opt-in, off-by-default accessibility aim indicator of HS-52, user ruling D9 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*). **No HUD surface displays or sonifies the Attention level, tell progress, sanity stage or a Horror's proximity; the chest-lamp state and suit vitals are the player's own, never the web's** (Attention, Adaptive Audio). The HUD contains no horror indicator, threat bar, proximity indicator or direction: a Horror is shown in the world, never by a meter (Horror Entities; Pillar 3). **The HUD draws no beacon or breach glyph under reduce-flashing or any other setting** (Attention C27 and V14: the 'HUD glyph' named by 2D Lighting's LO-26 would be an Attention indicator, which the art bible forbids at ART:741; under reduce-flashing the static world mark and the audio carry the breach). The HS-25 ship-alarm mark is not that glyph and never keys on Attention's phase.

#### B. The critical strip

**HS-11. Strip composition.** *Rule.* One opaque Ground plate, centre-bottom: `[breath dial arc with pivot cap][numeral][mark slot]`, left to right; the dial and the numeral show the ambient O2 in ROOM, GASP and NONE and the suit O2 (the reserve) while `breath_src` is RESERVE, in the same cells and the same 76 x 18 budget (HS-12; the swap adds no width, so D.1 is unchanged) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. There is **no separate 16 x 16 O2 loop glyph on the strip**; the pivot cap is part of the dial, not a glyph, so the art bible's "nothing smaller than 16 x 16" is not engaged. The mark slot is always reserved: the strip never reflows when the mark appears. Fit is the registered formula `strip_budget`: content width at most the 12 % cap (76 HUD px) and height at most the 5 % cap (18 HUD px) on the 1080p reference canvas. *Why.* The 12 % x 5 % cap is a user decision. The arithmetic with the font undecided is 63 to 65 HUD px with the mark and the pivot cap; adding a 16 x 16 O2 glyph makes 79 to 83, which does not fit. *Edge notes.* Height has zero slack (the 16-px mark fills the 16-px interior of an 18-px plate): any frame-border change breaks the cap. All widths are provisional on the instrument face, which does not yet exist (Open Questions). The 16 x 16 O2 ring-clamp glyph lives on the suit panel's reserve cell (HS-17). (orchestrator ruling: neither the 2x2 cell sizes nor the art-director's panel bounding box are rules; only the invariants of HS-16 are.)

**HS-12. The O2 numeral is the suit-sensed `p_o2` in integer dkPa, no unit label.** *Rule.* Three tabular digit columns, instrument face, 7 art px cap height, leading zeros suppressed, right-aligned in a fixed three-column field. The value is `perceived(suit.o2).displayed_value`, which on the honest path equals the suit-sensed `p_o2` of the occupant's compartment and equals `view.o2_pp_dkpa`. A nominal room reads `210`. A value above the dial clamps the needle at the stop and prints at most `999`. There is no percent O2 (`o2_pm` is display-only in the Graph) and no unit glyph; the zone ticks, not the unit, teach meaning. *Why.* The Suit's SE-30 and the Graph's UI item 3: "the suit O2 numeral on the critical strip is the suit's reading of compartment atmosphere via Suit, not a Graph overlay". *Edge notes.* This numeral is the MVP's second instrument (HS-14) in every source but RESERVE (ROOM, GASP, NONE); in RESERVE the second instrument is the ambient-O2 slot on the panel (below). **The breath dial has two sources** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; Resolved 2026-10-27 — re-review, G1 and G2, answers Suit OQ-41: the Suit's SE-30 (se:254, se:1465) already publishes the dial's content and its door-breach timeline (se:73) puts the CRITICAL mark on "the strip's breath dial (showing the reserve)"; the creative-director's recommendation is Option A, the strip shows the reserve; the user's D90 covers marks only and is untouched, so this is not a user decision)*: (1) **Content.** While `breath_src` is RESERVE the strip's breath dial (arc and numeral) shows the **suit O2**, `perceived(SUIT_KEY(suit.reserve))`: the numeral is D.3's `reserve_seconds` in the same three cells (`RESERVE_S` at full, `0` only at exactly empty) and the arc's `pm` is the reserve's own `displayed_pm`, the fixed scale "permille of `RESERVE_S`" (D.5, reserve branch), full at the right stop. In ROOM, GASP and NONE it shows the **ambient O2** exactly as above. The number that keeps the player alive is therefore at the glance point for the whole reserve leg, and the breath mark of HS-22 sits beside whichever reading the dial shows (the Suit's one mark anchor per axis). The numeral stays unlabelled in both sources. (2) **A frame-register discriminator that does not move the frame (HS-DT-3).** The dial face carries a second pre-engraved layer, present in the atlas at every `H`, lit only while the source is RESERVE: a **tank tick** (a 3-px radial notch with a 2-px foot) at the full stop and a **reserve warn notch** at `warn_pm = (RESERVE_WARN_S * 1000 + RESERVE_S - 1) div RESERVE_S` (334 at 30 s of 90 s, D.3), both Hull Grey; and the three dkPa zone ticks of HS-13 are drawn Graphite lit (dimmed to the dead-source ink) while RESERVE and Hull Grey otherwise. The layers differ in count and position of notches, so the two meanings are distinguishable in greyscale and in deafness; a "90" is therefore read against a dial that carries the tank tick (seconds of reserve) or the three zone notches (dkPa), never against the digits alone (AC-HS-137). Nothing in the frame moves, scales or recolours with the value: the layer switch is a source-state cut (HSV-4 class, counted once, never deferred). (3) **The swap edge.** The swap is drawn on the first render frame that reads the new `breath_src` (it follows the published enum, so it is never delayed) as **one** source-state event in the flash arbiter (HS-47: the numeral cells, the needle target, the engraved layers and the ambient slot all change together on that frame, one counted event); both honest numerals are **armed** at the swap tick (HS-14) so the first grid boundary that may change them is the next one; the needle retargets and settles (reduced motion: a step). The Suit's valve click is the audio edge of the same change. (4) **The demoted ambient numeral.** While RESERVE the ambient O2 (`perceived(SUIT_KEY(suit.o2))`) is drawn in the **ambient-O2 slot** of the suit panel: a three-digit field of the same 7-cap tabular face inside the reserve cell (D.8 cell tally: 17 HUD px wide for the digits, fenced by a Hull Grey three-notch echo of the strip's dkPa zone ticks so the field reads as the dkPa scale), blank (unlit) in every other source. It updates on the shared honest grid (one boundary is one event however many numerals change, EC-HS-21) and is the second instrument of HS-14 for the whole reserve leg; its reading-family slot set (gleam host edges, tabular tick cells, sound-request slot) is built as reserved empty geometry in both hosts the O2 family can occupy (HS-26a). The reserve cell's own numeral stays drawn in RESERVE (the reserve then reads twice, strip and panel; this is accepted so that no cell reflows and the panel's reserve underlay keeps its numeral). **This also answers Suit OQ-41 (b)**: the panel's reserve entry is **not** merged into the strip or hidden while the dial shows the reserve; the strip mark and the reserve-cell mark stay two beacons on one breath fact, as the user-ruled per-rung marks (X9 / D90, OQ-HS-65) require, and the cap of six marks holds; the reserve-cell mark's predicate is the Suit's published `mark_reserve` (`rung_breath >= 2 and breath_src == RESERVE`, Suit D.10), which the HUD reads where it is published and otherwise forms from the two published fields, never from a `displayed_*` value. (5) **Lie class.** At Vertical Slice A each host draws its own family's `Perceived` record (the strip draws `suit.reserve` in RESERVE, the ambient slot draws `suit.o2`); the Filter's `READING_LIE_STAGE` order is per key, so nothing about the Filter changes.

**HS-13. O2 dial zones are read from the Graph, never literals.** *Rule.* The dial's travel is `O2_DIAL_MAX_DKPA` 300 `[PH]` (range 250-300). Zone ticks are shapes (notches of increasing depth) at the ANOXIC/HYPOXIC edge, the `HYPOXIA_PP` edge (registered, default 140, range 120-160) and the THIN/NORMAL edge. The three O2 edges are registered names: `O2_BAND_ANOXIC_PP` (100, range 80-120), `HYPOXIA_PP` (140, range 120-160) and `O2_BAND_THIN_PP` (180, range 160-200); `DECOMP_P` is a pressure, not a `p_o2` edge. A lint forbids any dkPa band literal in HUD data or source, including a dial-generator script. `DECOMP_P` is a pressure, not a `p_o2` edge; the dial does not draw it. *Why.* Two owners of one edge is a conflict (reflexion pattern 1). *Edge notes.* At the range ends of `HYPOXIA_PP` a band shrinks to about 2.3 px of arc, so the **tick** is the information and band width is never relied on below 3 px (pattern-coded zones are a secondary channel). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The three dkPa zone ticks describe the ambient scale only: while the strip shows the reserve (HS-12) they are drawn Graphite lit and the reserve layer (tank tick at the full stop, warn notch at `warn_pm`) is lit instead; the Graph's band edges are still read by name and the reserve layer's positions come from `RESERVE_S` and `RESERVE_WARN_S` (cited from the Suit, never literals; the same lint covers them).

**HS-14. Strip motion and the honest-numeral grid.** *Rule.* The arc's needle **settles** on a change: a monotone, overshoot-free response (critically damped) whose 2 % settling time is exactly `HUD_SETTLE_MS` 300 `[PH]` (named to avoid a collision with Instruments' own `INSTR_SETTLE_MS`, CF-HS-20), range 250-400, held as a `(position, velocity)` state and advanced by the exact per-frame propagator of D.6 (valid for any time step up to `HUD_DT_CLAMP_MS`); a new target replaces only the target, never the state; then still; reduced motion: a one-frame step. The overshoot the art bible 7.3 allows (5 %) is a ceiling, not a requirement, and at 0.2-0.4 px it never rendered (Game Feel); the damped-spring form it replaces had no defined behaviour under a mid-settle retarget, which is the normal case in the breach (the Graph steps every 6 ticks against a 300 ms settle). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The numeral does **not** tween and does not change on every real change: all honest numerals (the O2 numeral and the reserve numeral) update on one shared **hold grid** of `HONEST_TICK_MS` pinned at 500 (`HONEST_GRID_TICKS` 30 ticks, derived), **anchored to the committed tick** (a boundary is a tick `T` with `T mod HONEST_GRID_TICKS == 0`, so the grid never drifts against the mark's own `T`-locked edges), showing the *latest* value at each grid boundary, with intermediate values skipped (never queued, never faded). A grid boundary is **skip-only**: it is drawn at its own tick or skipped, never delayed to a later tick. **"Armed" (defined once, used by EC-HS-10, EC-HS-29, AC-HS-97, AC-HS-114 and the RESTORE_FRAME and RETURN rows):** a numeral is *armed at tick `t_a`* when it is set directly at `t_a` (a restore, a pause exit, the end of a lie, a breath-source swap, HS-12); the first boundary that may change it is the first tick with `T mod HONEST_GRID_TICKS == 0` strictly after `t_a`, and after a RETURN additionally one full cell is skipped (the first boundary at least `HONEST_GRID_TICKS` after `t_a`). A grid boundary is one HUD luminance event however many digits change. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The needle is positional and is not limited by the grid. No tremor on any suit reading; no colour change on the plate for a rung (the rung is shape: the mark, HS-22). *Why.* **This amends the locked rule "numerals tick one step per real change" (art bible 7.3); the amendment is a user ruling (D91 (i), OQ-HS-2) and the art-director applies the text in the Open Questions amendment list, item 5.** During a decompression the integer `p_o2` can change on the order of 3 to 10 times a second (the Graph steps every 6 ticks; a venting rate above 3 dkPa/s changes the integer digit more than 3 times a second), while HR-13 / the Pipeline's PR-13 / the Filter's AC-PF-42 forbid any HUD element changing luminance faster than `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3, and the mark pulses at the same moment. At the 500 ms grid the numerals count 2 events/s; with the mark the total is at most 3 (<=, not <) under the ruled counting unit of HS-47 (the edge unit is retired). The range floor is derived, not borrowed from the Filter: `ceil(1000 / (FLASH_MAX_PER_S - u * TICK_RATE / BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS))` is 396 ms at `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` 128, 400 ms at its range floor 120 and 375 ms at 180 (`u` = 1, the ruled unit), so the average-rate floor is 375-400 ms (the Filter's PF-18 arithmetic gives 334 for lying ticks alone and ignores the mark). **That floor is necessary, not sufficient** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the arbiter tests a rolling 60-tick window, not an average. Below a 30-tick grid one window can hold three grid boundaries (a 24-tick grid has boundaries at 1, 25 and 49) plus a mark edge, which is four against the ceiling of three; at exactly 30 ticks every window holds exactly two boundaries, so with one mark edge the total is three. 30 ticks (500 ms) is therefore the only feasible value, and `HONEST_TICK_MS` is **pinned at 500** (the 400-500 range is withdrawn; registry request); **the pin is ratified by user ruling (D91 (iii), OQ-HS-15)**. This window proof was written for a pulsing mark beside counted numerals, a combination the two counting modes of HS-47 make rare (STRICT, reduce flashing, solidifies the mark; WEIGHTED exempts the digit swaps and returns a swap to the counted arithmetic only when the cumulative guard of D.2 trips); the window check therefore stays live in the validator, and the pin itself rests on the ratification and on the one cadence that both modes and the inspect card share. (The edge unit `u` = 2, which would have pinned `MARK_DUTY_PM` at 500 so that the mark's ON and OFF edges stay 60 ticks apart, is retired by the ruled counting unit; the 400-600 range of `MARK_DUTY_PM` stands.) The numeral lags the truth by at most `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS` (the grid-skip rule of HS-47). (orchestrator ruling: one name, `HONEST_TICK_MS`, replaces the three proposed by the art-director, ui-programmer and ux-designer, which disagreed on the value (500 ms versus 333 ms), the unit and whether the numeral is inside the budget; the amendment of art bible 7.3 is routed to the art-director and the accessibility-specialist; the counting unit and the pinned value are now user rulings (D91 (ii) and (iii)), and the existence of the grid was never the question, because In-World Instruments (iw:264, K-16) has adopted the same grid and rejected exempting honest ticks, and two GDDs sharing one constant cannot carry opposite conditionals.) *Edge notes.* An honest latched numeral and a STALE lie are told apart by duration (an honest numeral never holds a value that the needle contradicts for more than `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS`, the grid-skip bound of HS-47), by the gleam, and by the dial, not by the numeral alone. The **second-instrument assertion** (a criterion): in a ROOM-source THIN fixture the drawn numeral equals `perceived.displayed_value`, equals `view.o2_pp_dkpa`, equals the compartment's `p_o2` at steady state. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**HS-15. The strip cap is defined in HUD px at the 1080p reference; higher `H` knowingly exceeds it.** *Rule.* The cap is 76 x 18 HUD px, the 12 % x 5 % of the 640 x 360 reference canvas, which is the HUD canvas on every 16:9 desktop at `H_default` (1080p at `H` 3, 1440p at `H` 4, 2160p at `H` 6). At `H > H_default`, and on the Steam Deck (`H` 3 over a 2x world: canvas 426.67 wide, the strip plate about 17.8 % of width (76 of 426.67 HUD px; the content about 15 %)), the cap is knowingly exceeded: the art bible says "at a higher UI scale the plate grows with the integer step and the cap is knowingly exceeded — the player's explicit accessibility trade". The Deck overage is recorded as an accepted platform default and routed to the art-director to confirm. *Why.* The 7-art-px numeral floor cannot shrink with the picture; re-expressing the cap per display would need about 38 x 9 HUD px at `H` 6, below the numeral height.

#### C. The suit panel

**HS-16.
