Panel invariants (layout is the UX spec's).** *Rule.* The panel sits bottom-left inside the 5 % safe margin and holds four readings in compact cells: reserve, integrity, warmth, charge + lamp switch; each cell is a 16 x 16 glyph plus its reading on an opaque Ground plate, with a reserved mark slot at the cell's edge so nothing reflows when a mark appears; the failed-write notice and the first-frame line stack at the panel's edge; the `own_identity` slot is reserved (Vertical Slice B). The binding invariants are: (a) the panel lies inside the safe margin and clears the strip's 18 px and the chest keep-out at every `H` and display of HS-54 and HS-55; (b) **every needle-only reading (warmth and integrity, which have no numeral) has a needle of at least `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX`** (cited by name: 16, owned by In-World Instruments (designed), registered as its own constant (iw:1706, iw:1076; registry entry `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX`)), because the strip's arc cannot carry the glide (HS-29) and a needle-only reading whose needle is not eligible would have no HR-5 motion signature; the authored needle length is the UX spec's, at or above that value, and the warmth dial is the priority carrier (first in `READING_LIE_STAGE` order); (c) it adds no masking to the bottom-centre band; (d) its non-Ground pixel budget share is within `nonground_count` (HS-56). *Why.* Panel cell sizes cannot be committed here: the three specialists disagree (a 2 x 2 arrangement of about 129 x 38 HUD px with 18-px cells, which could host a 16-px glide needle only at the limit — a flat 180-degree sweep with the pivot on the border row; an art-director bounding box of at most 100 x 44; and a 4-row column of about 75 px tall as the alternative), and the font that fixes the widths does not exist. (orchestrator ruling: the GDD binds invariants only; the arrangement, cell sizes and the 2 x 2 versus column choice are decided in `/ux-design` with a drawn font, all sizes `[PH]`.) The bottom-row fit failure at high `H` is HS-54. **Decided together with the arrangement (design review, restated in re-review):** `PANEL_SWEEP_DEG` is **180** (a flat arc whose pivot sits on the cell's bottom interior row, which is the only form a 16-px glide needle fits in an 18-px cell and that D.5, D.11 and D.15 were written for); a 240-degree sweep needs a dial bounding box of 2L wide by 1.5L tall (D.11), 24 px at `L` 16, which no drawn arrangement holds in an 18-px row, so the sweep is limited to the arrangement that has actually been drawn (180) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **The 100 x 44 envelope is withdrawn** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the earlier text bound the panel to it, but the panel's own content does not fit it. The cell tally of D.8 (two needle-only cells of 68 HUD px, a reserve cell of 72 including the ambient-O2 slot of HS-12, a charge cell with its keycap and mark of 52, and the 18-px ship-alarm slot) needs about 144 x 40 as a 2 x 2 row arrangement or about 74 x 80 as a 4-row column, plus the notice stack above either. D.8 therefore carries a **budget per arrangement** with its own `fit_v`, `fit_notice` and `offered()` row, and **`/ux-design` chooses the arrangement by the resulting `offered()` set, with the accessibility-specialist signing off any reduction** (OQ-HS-81). The rail is not recomputed (it has not been shown to fail). The invariants (a) to (d) above hold for either arrangement, plus (e) the notice stack (the failed-write notice and the restore line, which after every death both rise from the panel's edge) lies inside the canvas, clears the chest keep-out, the strip and the rail, and satisfies `fit_notice` (D.8).

**HS-17. The reserve cell.** *Rule.* Glyph: the O2 ring clamp with a tank silhouette (`ui_glyph_reserve_16`), O2 Cyan while a breathable source or reserve exists, Graphite lit otherwise. Reading: the numeral `reserve_seconds = ceil(displayed_pm * RESERVE_S / 1000)`, whole seconds, **three digit cells** (because `RESERVE_S` ranges to 240), showing `RESERVE_S` (90 by default) when full, `0` only at exactly empty. It falls at the honest hold grid and counts **upward** in whole steps during a top-up, never tweened (`top_up` is an MVP O2 TAP verb, user ruling X5: the count-up is MVP-live) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **A thin fill underlay with an engraved notch at `RESERVE_WARN_S` is required** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the reserve is the player's real death clock and had no engraved reference, so the numeral gains a 16-HUD-px, 1-px-high track under it whose fill is the `displayed_pm` fraction and whose fixed (frame-register) notch sits at `warn_notch_px = (RESERVE_WARN_S * 16 + RESERVE_S - 1) div RESERVE_S` from the empty end (6 px at 30 s of 90 s, D.3; a ceiling, like the fill, so the fill stands at the notch exactly when the numeral reads `RESERVE_WARN_S`); the notch is engraving and never moves, the fill is the reading. The UX spec sizes it; it costs about 20 non-Ground px, inside the 184-px headroom. The numeral is intentionally optimistic under a seam leak (it is nominal draw): the seam jet is the world's falsifier (P1), and the Suit owes a dependency check that it reaches the player before the error grows past a stated margin (below). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **While `breath_src` is RESERVE the strip's breath dial shows this same numeral and arc (HS-12)**, so the optimism sits at the glance point of the whole HUD, not only on the panel; the P1 reasoning, the Suit's cue-margin ask and the playtest probe (OQ-HS-58) therefore apply to the strip's RESERVE state first, the panel cell being its redundant copy. The panel cell keeps its numeral and underlay in RESERVE (nothing reflows) and gains the ambient-O2 slot (HS-12 (4)). *Why.* `RESERVE_WARN_S` and `SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S` are seconds, so the player reads the same unit the rules use, and a numeral gives accessibility a non-hue channel. *Edge notes.* Under a seam leak the true time left is shorter than the numeral says; this is documented, not a defect. **But it is an inaccuracy outside the Filter with no violet leak, sitting beside the HUD's own teaching that numerals that step are honest**, so P1 asks for a falsifier that is sure to arrive first. **Dependency check (an ASK to the Suit, OQ-HS-60; HS-DT-1):** every seam leak must carry a body or world cue (seam vapour jet, hiss, frost) that reaches the player before the reserve error (nominal seconds minus true seconds) exceeds a margin the Suit states (proposed Suit-owned `RESERVE_LEAK_ERR_MARGIN_S`, `[PH]`, **unregistered**, to be no larger than `SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S`; the Suit has two ways to close this and the HUD takes whichever it picks: (a) publish a **leak-aware remaining value** that D.3 then reads, or (b) register `RESERVE_LEAK_ERR_MARGIN_S` with a Suit test that the cue arrives before the error exceeds it; until it does, the numeral's optimism is documented, not hidden) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The Suit's own tables already put the leak debit and the seam jet on the same run (the suit leaks from the run `integrity_bp` falls below `SUIT_LEAK_HP`), so the cue lag is 0 and the error at the cue's first frame is 0; the ASK is that the Suit asserts this in a test and that the jet is an honest check, not a corruptible module-body cue (the Suit's CONFLICT C1 against the Graph's closed list must close first). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **The falsifier does not survive lamp-off or deafness as written** (the seam jet is seen only when lit; the hiss is heard): the leak-onset cue must also have a **non-audio body cue readable with the lamp off** (a body or visor cue at the first leak debit, never a HUD element), so HS-DT-1 holds for a deaf or dark player; this is an added ask to the Suit (OQ-HS-80 (4)). **If playtest finds players read the optimistic reserve as a lie (OQ-HS-58),** the Suit should model the leak in the reserve reading; D.3 would then take a leak-aware remaining-seconds value from the Suit instead of `displayed_pm * RESERVE_S`, the one HUD change that follows.

**HS-18. Integrity and warmth are zone-band dials, with no numeral.** *Rule.* Integrity is a dial of `perceived(suit.integrity).displayed_pm` with shape ticks at `SUIT_HP_OK` (7000), `SUIT_LEAK_HP` (5000) and `SUIT_HP_FAIL` (2500) basis points converted to permille (`integrity_zone_ticks`), **never** the Graph's `HP_OK` / `HP_FAIL` (7000 / 3000), which are a different symbol by registry rule. Warmth is a dial of **five load zones** separated by four notches, cold on the left and hot on the right, in the order `WZ_COLD_CRIT` (0), `WZ_COLD_STRAIN` (1), `WZ_COMFORT` (2), `WZ_HOT_STRAIN` (3), `WZ_HOT_CRIT` (4), with the centre zone `WZ_COMFORT` as the notched reference band; the notch positions are the Suit's published edges (SE-29: 0 / 333 / 466 / 533 / 666 / 1000 at the defaults, the images of -`TL_MAX`, -`TL_CRITICAL`, -`TL_STRAINED`, +`TL_STRAINED`, +`TL_CRITICAL`, +`TL_MAX`, so at the nominal `warmth_pm` 500 the `WZ_COMFORT` zone is the lit one), read by name and never as literals; pattern-coded, with **no hue** (Paper White ink, HS-21). The zones are load zones, not the Suit's ambient `band_t` names (COMFORT, COOL, FROST, FREEZING, DEEP_COLD, HOT, SCORCH): those describe a room, the dial describes the wearer's accumulated load, and a band-driven STRAINED rung leaves the needle in `WZ_COMFORT` because the body is ahead of the number (SE-29). The zone ids are drawing ids only: the HUD never infers a rung from a zone (HS-1). Enum order is identical in HS-18 and D.14. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; the earlier seven-cell, equal-spacing axis contradicted SE-29 and drew the wrong zone at rest)* There is **no health number and no temperature number**. The art bible's HR-12 "every critical needle carries a numeral beside it" is read as binding on the critical strip's O2 needle only; the wording amendment is routed to the art-director. *Why.* A health number and a body-temperature number are Suit anti-requirements; the anti-pillar "not a combat game" forbids a health bar; the zone band is the numeral-equivalent and meets "colour is redundant with shape and position". *Edge notes.* **Answered by the Suit (SE-29):** `suit.warmth`'s `Perceived.displayed_pm` is `warmth_pm = (500 * (thermal_load + TL_MAX)) / TL_MAX`, 0..1000, cold below 500, with the four inner edges 333 / 466 / 533 / 666 at the defaults `[PH]` (the Suit's, tunable there; the HUD reads them). The HUD adopts the encoding and the edges; the equal-spacing placeholder is retired (EC-HS-20, OQ-HS-9 closed). Remaining asks to the Suit: publish the four inner edges as named reads or registry names (they are cited from SE-29 today), confirm the convention that an edge value belongs to the upper zone, and confirm the `Perceived` field is `displayed_pm`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**HS-19. The charge cell and the lamp switch.** *Rule.* Glyph: the cell silhouette (`ui_glyph_charge_16`), outline plus a fill proportional to `perceived(suit.charge).displayed_pm` (`charge_fill_and_warn`), the `toggle_beam` keycap beside it. The **lamp switch** is shown, never the lamp's effect: switch on is a Paper White outline with a Tungsten fixture fill; switch off is a Graphite-lit outline with the fill held. The glyph never shows whether the player is lit, exposed, seen or detected. The cell is on at MVP (`CELL_ENABLED` 1, user ruling X5: the lamp cell and the "go dark" lever exist); it is absent (an empty slot, no "disabled" art) only when `CELL_ENABLED` is 0, a data edge case *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The mark appears beside the cell at `CELL_WARN_S` (20 s of cell at `CELL_S` 1200, about 16.7 permille: a cross-check value only; the Suit decides when). *Why.* The state is the wearer's own equipment, already visible as the cone; pad parity needs a home for the `toggle_beam` binding; P3 forbids an illumination or exposure state. (orchestrator ruling: the brief's pillar row "no 'lit' state of the player" is rewritten as "no illumination or exposure state of the player"; the lint adds `exposed`, `exposure`, `lit_state`.) *Edge notes.* The lamp-switch glyph change is a plate-scale luminance change that is not positional; it is a state-display draw of the shared limiter (HS-47 class A) so ten toggles in a second cannot flash the HUD, and the world beam is never debounced by the HUD (the cone is the truth), so the glyph may lag a rapid toggle by `GLYPH_STATE_LATCH_TICKS` 20 `[PH]` in the common case and by at most `MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 60 when the window is full; the latch now spans the whole class, not the lamp alone *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**HS-20. Glyph and hue discipline.** *Rule.* A loop-family glyph carries its loop hue only while its source runs; otherwise it is Graphite lit; a failed source keeps its hue and the mark sits beside it, never over it. The O2 and reserve glyphs (and the strip's arc band and pivot cap) are O2 Cyan while a breathable source exists, defined as `breath_src` in {ROOM, RESERVE} and Graphite lit in {GASP, NONE}: the hue is a function of that published enum alone (HS-5), never of a `displayed_*` value, so it is frame-register class A and a Filter lie cannot reach it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the integrity glyph (a rivet line of three rivets and a hairline) is Hull Grey; no other loop hue (Power Amber, Coolant Ice, Fuel Ember) appears on the MVP HUD because the HUD shows no ship state. A reading never changes colour with its value: zone crossings change the needle's position against fixed engraving, never ink colour; there is no red numeral, no amber numeral, no flashing numeral. A glyph never swaps shape to express state; state is a reading beside the glyph. Glyphs are 1-bit masks tinted at draw time by the `Theme`, so the palette cannot drift. Any HUD colour outside Ground `#1C2128`, Graphite lit `#5C6670`, Hull Grey `#8C949C`, Paper White `#DDE4EA`, Tungsten fixture `#FFCC66`, O2 Cyan `#3FD6E8`, Alarm Red `#E0102A` and the dormant fringe `#B07BE6` (plus Graphite mid, used only for the one-step plate dim of the OUTCOME frame, HS-41) is a defect. *Why.* P1 and the art bible's state-lives-in-the-sprite principle; it keeps the HUD from becoming a health bar.

**HS-21. The warmth glyph: a suit reading without a loop.** *Rule.* Warmth has no loop, so it takes a flat Paper White chamfered-plate silhouette with three horizontal fin hairlines, 16 x 16, with **no hue**. It is never the coolant collar (heat is not the coolant loop), never a thermometer or a flame (the art bible forbids abstract symbols), and it never borrows Coolant Ice, Fuel Ember or Alarm Red for cold or heat. *Why.* Using a loop hue would assert that heat is a loop. The amendment to art bible 7.2 ("a suit reading without a loop") is routed to the art-director; this GDD supplies the amendment text and does not edit the art bible. It closes the Suit's open pair on the warmth glyph.

#### D. The warning mark

**HS-22. The mark is defined once; the HUD draws it, the Suit decides when.** *Rule.* The mark is a chamfered 16 x 16 square filled Alarm Red carrying a Paper White exclamation stroke — a glyph, never text — and Alarm Red appears nowhere else on the HUD and never as text. It always sits **beside** a reading, never over it, in the reading's own slot, **each mark keyed to its own published Suit rung and never exclusive of another** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; creative-director ruling, confirmed by user ruling D90, OQ-HS-65)*: the **breath mark in the strip's slot**, beside the breath dial's reading, whichever reading the dial is showing (the ambient O2, or the reserve while `breath_src` is RESERVE, HS-12; the strip has no O2 glyph, HS-11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, whenever the Suit's breath rung is CRITICAL (`rung_breath >= 2`) whatever the breath source, because the strip is where the player looks for O2; the **reserve mark in the reserve cell's slot**, beside the reserve glyph, additionally while `breath_src` is `RESERVE` (a `view()` field, HS-5) and the breath rung is CRITICAL, which is the Suit's reserve-engaged CRITICAL (SE-30; the Suit has one breath axis and no separate reserve rung, so "the reserve axis reaches CRITICAL" and "breath CRITICAL while the reserve is engaged" are the same condition and the two earlier phrasings are one rule); and the warmth, integrity and charge cells' slots on their own rungs. The two breath marks may coexist (the reserve is low and engaged, which is the door-breach anchor moment); when the source is `GASP` or `NONE` only the strip mark shows. Both slot conditions read published fields: the HUD derives no rung, and `breath_src` only chooses a second slot. It appears when the Suit's rung for that axis is CRITICAL after `RUNG_MIN_TICKS` (the hold) and for the charge cell at `CELL_WARN_S`; never at STRAINED; FAILED / DOWNED persists it; a restore into a DEAD seat shows nothing. **The ship-alarm slot (the sixth, HS-25) sits on the suit panel, not on the strip:** a second 16-px slot on the strip makes its content 79 HUD px against the 76 cap (D.1, the arithmetic that removed the O2 glyph), so the strip keeps its one slot for the breath mark and the alarm slot's position is the UX spec's inside the HS-16 envelope (OQ-HS-33) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. At most six marks exist (the five above plus the ship-alarm mark of HS-25 in its own panel slot) and all share one phase. The HUD adds no threshold, hold timer or debounce and **never computes a rung from `displayed_value`**. The mark's ON phase is the filled square; its OFF phase is a Graphite-lit one-pixel chamfered outline of the same slot, so the slot and the eye's target never vanish. *Why.* The art bible defines the mark once; the rung rule is the Suit's. Keying each mark to its own rung keeps the alarm where the player looks for O2, makes the suit-rung mark count follow from five slots (six with the ship alarm), and matches AC-HS-102 and AC-HS-37; the earlier exclusive rule (strip only for `GASP` / `NONE`, reserve slot only when engaged) contradicted both. The Suit's SE-30, AC-SE-31 and its mark table (se:367, se:1204) word the two placements as exclusive; widening them is a cross-GDD note, not a HUD change of a Suit value. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The mark's asset is one sprite instantiated per slot, which refines the art bible's wording "lives on the critical strip" to "lives beside the reading it concerns" (amendment routed to the art-director). *Edge notes.* Whether a mark shows beside a **lying** reading is HS-30.

**HS-23. One alarm rhythm.** *Rule.* The mark phase is `mark_phase = ((T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS) + tick_alpha) / BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, with `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` 128 (2.133 s); the mark is ON while the phase is below `MARK_DUTY_PM / 1000` with `MARK_DUTY_PM` 500 `[PH]` (range 400-600). `MARK_PERIOD_TICKS` is `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` **by relation, never a second value**. The leading edge falls at `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0`, the same grid as the beacon sweep and the annunciator (Adaptive Audio's AAV-9). There are no per-mark offsets and no per-mark epoch: all marks share one phase, with or without a beacon in scope (the fallback epoch `(T - breach_enter_T)` is Audio's branch for a listener without a beacon, cited, not re-owned). The mark is drawn on the first render frame whose `(T, tick_alpha)` is at or past a boundary (at most one render frame of skew against the audio edge: 16.7 ms at 60 fps). Under reduced motion or reduce-flashing the mark holds ON solid and the sound carries the alarm. **At the first-launch default the mark is therefore solid:** `reduce_flashing` defaults ON (user ruling D79), so the beacon-phase pulse, the 64-tick appearance latency and the pause-bridge arithmetic below apply to a player who has turned reduce flashing off and kept reduced motion off, and the sound carries the alarm by default (note to Adaptive Audio, AAV-19; OQ-HS-76) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **While the kernel is `PAUSED` (solo pause, `PAUSED_FROZEN`) any mark that is active is held ON solid, with no edges: the same rule reduced motion already uses.** `T` does not advance under pause, so no mark can appear or clear during it; an inactive slot stays empty; the phase is not consulted while paused and resumes from the unchanged `T` on the first `RUNNING` frame, the mark bridged ON until the next leading edge (below). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* This is **the one stated exception to "last frame held"** (HS-53, States and Transitions), adopted at CD-GDD-ALIGN (stage 12) on the art-director's alternative. *Why.* "One rhythm for alarm everywhere" and P1/P2: a pause that froze the mark in its OFF outline beside a CRITICAL reading would make the honest frame register look safe when it is not, and the player would return from pause with no alarm showing. Holding ON keeps the alarm legible for as long as the world is stopped, at the cost of one element's departure from the frozen-frame rule. *Edge notes.* **Flash arithmetic** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: entering the hold from an OFF phase is one mark leading edge, and leaving it on resume (when the unchanged `T` is in an OFF phase) would be a trailing edge; both are mark edges in the arbiter (HS-47: never deferred, never delayed), so one pause cycle would cost one leading edge and one trailing edge. **Resume bridge (the HUD-side dwell guard, design review 2026-10-27):** on resume the mark does not leave the held-ON state until the **next leading edge** (`T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0`), after which it follows the phase again; the one OFF stretch it skips is the price. Result: a pause entered in an OFF phase costs one leading edge, a resume costs none, and a pause re-entered before that next leading edge finds the mark already ON and costs none, so toggling pause cannot drive the mark past the ceiling whatever the Chart Table does. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* No numeral grid boundary, prompt show-start or Filter tick occurs while paused (`present_time` is frozen), so a pause adds no other event. Under reduced motion or reduce-flashing the mark is already solid and a pause adds no edge at all. Pause re-toggle spam is bounded by the HUD's resume bridge above (AC-HS-133); the Chart Table's minimum re-toggle interval (OQ-HS-62) stays a recommended second guard, no longer the only one. Status: the creative-director's recommendation, still subject to user confirmation (OQ-HS-4).

**HS-24. The mark is never the first channel (P2).** *Rule.* The mark rides the Suit's rungs, which already hold for `RUNG_MIN_TICKS` and which carry the Suit's own minimum-warning floor (`SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S`); on the Graph's side `alarm_eligible` stays false until the fairness gate (`W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 s) opens. The HUD adds no second gate and never shows a mark earlier than its owner publishes it. *Why.* "Breach fires at a hidden threshold versus preceded by a 10-30 s tell: the tell."

**HS-25. The ship-alarm mark: accepted at MVP (user ruling D89 / D43).** *Rule.* The MVP draws six marks: the five suit-rung marks and the ship-alarm mark. The HUD's request to the Suit, formerly "recommended for MVP", is **accepted by the user** (the creative-director's position, CD-GDD-ALIGN stage 12: a one-bit `suit.alarm` is consistent with the fiction, "the suit receives the ship's alarm", and with HS-DT-2, provided it never names a loop or a place; no precedent exists in any GDD: a search for `suit.alarm` and `alarm_received` returns nothing) for a one-bit reading `suit.alarm`, true while an `alarm_eligible` descriptor applies to the seat's body (Suit SE-29: the Graph's `alarm_sections()` intersected with `sections_of(body_of(seat section))`, Graph LS-13 (c), derived inside the Suit; the HUD still never reads `failures()` or Graph state) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, wrapped by the Filter as a **TRUTH-class key that is never lie-capable** (HS-30: Graph-driven marks stay honest; this narrows the art bible 7.2 wording "lie-capable" for this one mark, an amendment routed to the art-director, art bible amendment text item 9). The Suit's "at most five marks" becomes six (Suit refresh owed) and the HUD draws the sixth mark from this reading only. **Slot and rule:** the ship-alarm mark has its own 16 x 16 slot on the suit panel (HS-22), draws the same asset on the same one phase as every mark, appears when `suit.alarm` is true, hides on the render frame it goes false, and is TRUTH-class (no lie shows, suppresses or tints it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The reason for accepting it at MVP is P2: the world's tell (the beacon sweep) goes static under reduced motion and a deaf player has no sound channel, so the P2 ship-alarm tell must not depend on audio alone. The HUD never reads `failures()` or Graph state, and the mark never names a loop or a place. It keys on `suit.alarm` (the Graph's `alarm_eligible`) only, never on Attention's phase, and is not the beacon glyph 2D Lighting's LO-26 once named (Attention C27). *Why.* P1 and the single wrap point. *Edge notes.* **Accessibility gap: closed by the ruling.** Under reduced motion the world's alarm sweep goes static and a deaf player has no sound channel; the `suit.alarm` mark is that player's HUD cue for a ship alarm (CF-HS-4 closed). The "if declined" branch (creating `design/accessibility-requirements.md` with an entry for the gap) is moot; that document is still to be authored later with `/ux-design accessibility` (it is noted here and not created) and receives the HUD's contributions listed in UI Requirements. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

#### E. The lie slots (built at MVP, dormant until Vertical Slice A)

**HS-26. All six reading families carry the full tri-modal slot set.** *Rule.* Each of the five readings and the scanner slot is built with a gleam slot, a motion-signature slot (glide where eligible, else tick / stale) and a sound-request slot. They are driven at MVP only by a **stub Filter** in tests (the Suit's AC-SE-30 and the Filter's AC-PF-39) and are enabled in builds only through `READING_LIE_STAGE` for the five suit readings and `SCANNER_LIE_STAGE` (Sanity's, registered, default never, stage 3 only) for the scanner slot (all "never" at MVP). Each slot's inputs are only the `Perceived` record and the HUD-supplied calibration; the slot holds no stage logic and never reads another reading's value. The slot switches on `motion_mode` only: HONEST draws the settle (arcs) or the grid (numerals); GLIDE moves the needle directly from `displayed_pm`; TICK substitutes digits at `tick_phase_ms`; STALE holds digits and needle; RETURN follows `displayed_pm` directly. *Why.* The Suit's UI-2: Vertical Slice A enables a lie without a redraw. The Filter's calibration contract makes the owning scene supply the arc, needle length, zone bands and a mask slice per lie-capable surface; for these six the owning scene is the HUD, so `O2_DIAL_MAX_DKPA` is a calibration the HUD **supplies** to the Filter (changing it rescales lie magnitude). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* No Filter GDD names the constant yet, so the registry's note that it has a Filter consumer is **owed, not true**: either the Filter names it in its calibration contract (cross-GDD note) or the claim is removed (registry request); until then it is the HUD's own tuning knob. **Scope allocation (ratified as a default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): D93, OQ-HS-69) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*:** the dormant slots are heavy work that no MVP playtest exercises, so the rule is split in two without changing the contract. **HS-26a, MVP:** the *read path and the reserved geometry*: every reading node pulls `perceived` and dispatches on `motion_mode` with the HONEST branch fully built; the gleam host edges, the tabular tick cells and the sound-request id slot exist in the layout and the node tree as reserved, empty geometry, so nothing is re-laid out later; the stub-Filter tests cover the dispatch and the reserved geometry. **HS-26b, Vertical Slice A entry work:** the drawing code for the gleam, glide, tick / stale and sound request, the charge-cell motion signature, and the D.13 / D.20 behaviours, with AC-HS-40, 43 to 45, 103, 104 and 114 (all tagged RESERVED already) as its criteria. "Without a redraw" (the Suit's UI-2) is read as *without re-authoring assets or layout*, which HS-26a guarantees. **Vertical Slice A entry blockers recorded here** *(design review)*: (1) the Filter's tick-budget change: under VS-A the Filter's lying-digit ticks alone fill the 3-per-second ceiling (D.2, `N_r` 2 or more), so the overrun is structural, not a tuning accident; (2) the charge-cell motion signature (CF-HS-14); (3) the `LEAK_MAX_CH` unit (CF-HS-12); (4) the Filter's mark field for a lying reading (HS-30); (5) PF-17 for HUD dials (HS-29).

**HS-27. The gleam: edge-only, lit-length, in the exact fringe.** *Rule.* The gleam is drawn by the HUD on the reading's own glass, in HUD px, at the glass edge, the needle's leading edge or the numeral window's edge; never a fill, never over digits. Colour is `#B07BE6` exactly, never the core `#8A3FD1`, with no alpha and no intermediate tint. Width is `GLEAM_EDGE_PX` (unregistered; PF-16 `[PLACEHOLDER]` 1 art px, range 1-2) at its default of 1 art px mapped to 1 HUD px (2 HUD px at the range top) (at least 3 screen px at `H >= 3`, so HR-12's two-pixel stroke holds by construction). **Amplitude is encoded as lit length**: the number of edge pixels lit is `round(edge_len_px * leak_amplitude_pm / 1000)`, at least `max(2, ...)` whenever the lie is active (`AMP_FLOOR_PM` 400, the Filter's registry constant with range 250-600, is the Filter's floor), anchored at the needle tip or the arc's leading edge so it moves with the reading. It is position-stable and survives greyscale because it is a shape. The gleam draws only while its host reading draws. *Why.* The Filter's scene-tree lint bans any `CanvasItem` alpha above 0 sourced from the Filter on the HUD tree, and the palette is locked. The rejected alternative — a colour blend of the fringe toward Ground between about 650 and 930 permille — would need intermediate hexes and alpha. *Edge notes.* **`LEAK_MAX_CH` 0.85 is a Filter-internal conflict, not a HUD one:** the Filter's own PF-16 (pf:312) names both the fringe `#B07BE6` and the cap, and the same conflict applies to world gleams in the world `SubViewport`. The fringe's blue channel is 230/255 = 0.90 in sRGB-encoded terms (fails the cap) and about 0.79 in linear terms (passes); the cap's stated rationale (bloom of glow, processed before tonemapping) is a linear-space concern. **ASK 1 (Filter):** state the cap's unit. **ASK 2, only if the unit is sRGB:** waive the cap for layer 2 (HUD glass and the inspect-card glass drawn through the HUD helper), re-tested if HDR output is ever enabled on the root. No value is changed, and this GDD draws the exact fringe per the palette lock (CF-HS-12).

**HS-28. Motion signatures.** *Rule.* Only a HONEST record settles. A GLIDE record drifts 0.5 to 2.0 deg/s (`GLIDE_RATE_MIN_DEG` / `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG`) with wander `WANDER_AMP_PX` 2.2 at 0.40 to 0.45 Hz and never rests beyond `NEVER_REST_MAX_S` 1.0 s. A TICK or STALE record substitutes digits at the Filter's `tick_phase_ms` or holds them. A non-HONEST record never settles and never tremors. Reduced motion: settle becomes a step; glide slows by `RM_GLIDE_SCALE` 0.6 (floor 0.3 deg/s) but keeps its drift and wander; the lying tick never falls below `LIE_TICK_MS` 400 (floor 334 ms, may be raised to 600). *Why.* Every lie is tri-modal; the honest and the lying forms never share a motion. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*

**HS-29. Needle eligibility and the strip's arc.** *Rule.* A needle can carry GLIDE only if its length meets the eligibility floor: `rate_floor = max(0.5, 28.6 / needle_len_px)` must not exceed `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG` 2.0, i.e. the needle is at least 14.3 art px (the Filter formula's mathematical floor; the design value is `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX`, 16, range 14.3-32, which In-World Instruments (designed) registers as its own constant (iw:1706, iw:1076; registry entry `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX`); cited, never re-owned). The strip's arc, with an interior of 16 HUD px, carries a needle of about 11 px and is therefore **ineligible to glide by construction**; the O2 lie (Vertical Slice A) rides the numeral (continuous tick or stale-hold) plus the gleam on the arc's glass, and the glide signature is carried by a suit-panel dial whose needle meets the floor (HS-16 (b)). A flat-arc strip needle of at least 16 px is the spike candidate. *Why.* HR-5 requires every lie to have a motion signature; the strip is capped by a user decision. The Filter's PF-17 currently names in-world gauges; an ASK asks it to state the rule for HUD dials explicitly. *Edge notes.* The reserve numeral, as a numeral, is eligible for TICK / STALE; while the strip shows the reserve (HS-12) the strip's arc is still ineligible to glide, so a reserve lie on the strip rides the numeral plus the gleam on the arc glass exactly as the ambient lie does *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**HS-30. The mark's truth class: suit-rung marks inherit their reading's class; ship-alarm and world-housing marks stay honest.** *Rule (the one-line ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN stage 12; the creative-director's recommendation, still subject to user confirmation).* **A suit-rung mark (the Suit's rung marks: breath, reserve, warmth, integrity, charge) inherits the class of the reading it sits beside; a Graph-driven mark (the ship alarm `suit.alarm`, and the world-housing marks owned by In-World Instruments) is TRUTH and is never shown, suppressed or tinted by a lie.** At MVP (the honest path) every mark is honest because no reading lies, and the mark is driven by the Suit's rungs. At Vertical Slice A, on a lying suit reading, whether its rung mark shows is supplied by the Filter for that reading (through `Perceived.frame_override` or a mark field the Filter adds — an ASK to the Filter); the HUD never computes CRITICAL from a `displayed_value`. Stated plainly: a lying suit reading can lie its rung mark away (P4(b), "the lie extends from the gauge to the sprite"); the ship-alarm mark cannot be lied away; the body cue and the pipe remain in both cases. *Why.* The Suit's UI-3 ("not an independent honest backup") and P4's "at least one channel of truth remains" (the body). Risk R-7: the first-time player is protected by keeping suit readings honest until the cue ladder has been playtested. **Conflict CF-HS-19, settled on the HUD side:** Sanity (SN-19 / sn:288, with its truth-class rows at sn:1195 and sn:1228) says "the mark never lies" ("driven only by Graph `alarm_eligible`: a lie may neither show nor suppress it"), where the Suit (UI-3, se:1309) says the mark is "not an independent honest backup". The split above reconciles them: Sanity's sentence is right for the Graph-driven marks (Instruments iw:302 is Graph-driven; the HUD's ship-alarm mark reads the accepted `suit.alarm`, HS-25 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*) and the Suit's UI-3 is right for the suit-rung marks. **Routed wording change to Sanity SN-19 (and its rows at sn:1195 and sn:1228):** "the *ship-alarm and world-housing* warning marks never lie (Graph `alarm_eligible` only); a *suit-rung* mark sits beside its reading and inherits that reading's class" (OQ-HS-61). The ruling is needed before Vertical Slice A. *Retained:* no Acceptance Criterion asserts Vertical Slice A mark-on-lie behaviour either way; R11 stands for the build (the Suit drives the rung marks at MVP, dormant until Vertical Slice A).

**HS-31. Lying digits obey the Filter's schedule.** *Rule.* A lying numeral substitutes a digit when `tick_phase_ms` says, on the Filter's schedule `T_tick = max(LIE_TICK_MS, N_r * 334)` (stagger `T_tick / N_r` >= 334 ms; cited with its verbatim output of 400 / 668 / 1002 / 1336 ms for `N_r` 1 to 4, `LIES_MAX` "1 / 2 / 4 by stage", 4 at stage 3); a substitution is a digit-glyph swap on the tabular grid, never a plate inversion, and the plate never reflows. The HUD never reschedules it (HS-47). *Why.* The scheduler is the Filter's.

#### F. The hand rail

**HS-32. Rail contents.** *Rule.* Three tool slots at MVP in slot order — cutter, welder, scanner — as Paper White 16 x 16 glyphs derived from the hand-tool sprite profiles, in a **four-slot rail geometry** (user ruling X6: the suppressant leaves the MVP kit, Suit SE-11 owns the roster; the fourth cell is reserved and draws nothing at MVP, no plate, glyph, keycap or pips, so the tool can return without a re-layout); the equipped tool is the Tungsten fixture colour plus the HR-11 focus bracket (focus is a shape: bracket plus a one-pixel thickening of the bezel). Keyboard: keycaps `1` to `3` under the drawn slots (`4` returns with the fourth tool); gamepad: the cycle pair `LB` / `RB` flanking the rail cycles the three (the pad binds cycle only). **Dormant until the suppressant returns (Vertical Slice, X6):** it shows its charges 0 to 3 (`charges_max` 3) as three pips and a numeral; **at 0** its glyph is Graphite lit with a diagonal Ground-coloured cut across the 16 x 16 silhouette — not hidden, not a banner. The glowstick glyph is **Paper White, never Chemlight `#86B896`**, with a count 0 to 4 (`GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` 4) in the instrument face; it steps when the throw is **accepted**, not when the stick lands, and a stowed stick has no HUD glow. The carried-module glyph (a raised-hold silhouette) appears with the drop keycap (`G`; the pad's `X`) only while a module is carried. The scanner slot is HS-33. There is no weight, capacity, slot count, condition, part count, grid or encumbrance moodle anywhere on the rail. Tool names (`name_key`) never appear on the rail face; they appear on focus or inspect only. The rail is display-only: no mouse capture in `gameplay`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Why.* The Suit's surfaces table, Boarding & Salvage's rail rows and the Item Database's silhouette-plus-name rule; anti-pillar "not a freeform builder". *Edge notes.* The diagonal cut is the default for the suppressant-at-0 state; the Suit's 8 x 8 `ui_mark_empty_08` is the alternative pending an art-director ruling on a "state tick, not a glyph" exception to 7.2's "nothing smaller than 16 x 16" (refresh owed to the Suit's asset row if the default stands).

**HS-33. The scanner reading slot (MVP-live, honest).** *Rule.* A transient slot beside the scanner glyph: Paper White numerals or a zone band, **no verdict icon, no green / red, no Alarm Red**, shown for `SCAN_READING_DWELL_S` 6 `[PH]` (range 3-12) and then removed by a cut; drawn at MVP and honest: Sanity SN-19 makes the MVP scanner an anchor in the reading register, Interaction IA-30 ships `field_check` SCAN at MVP, and In-World Instruments K-9 routes the reading to this slot. It becomes lie-capable at Vertical Slice A only through `perceived(SCANNER, viewer)` behind `SCANNER_LIE_STAGE` (Sanity's, registered, default never, stage 3 only; it is **not** gated by `READING_LIE_STAGE`). The slot is not drawn while no scanner is equipped. Only what a scan shows is provisional *(provisional — ASK Sanity / Interaction for the scan's content and unit)*. *Why.* Boarding & Salvage's field-check register ("no green check, red cross, 'OK' or 'FAILED' word or icon"); a tool's reading belongs to the tool.

**HS-34. Rail counts are anchors.** *Rule.* The glowstick count 0 to 4 is honest at MVP and Vertical Slice A (the suppressant's charges 0 to 3 join it when the tool returns, X6); there are no `suit.suppressant` or `suit.glowsticks` keys *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Sanity, as co-owner, confirms (Open Questions). *Why.* A lie must buy a falsification moment; a throw already falsifies a count, so the lie teaches nothing, and fewer keys means fewer tri-modal assets.

**HS-35. Glyph device: consume, never classify.** *Rule.* The glyph set follows the Adapter's last button or key device. The HUD consumes the Adapter's glyph token only and never classifies devices or reads `InputEvent.device` (4.7 reports `DEVICE_ID_MOUSE` and `DEVICE_ID_KEYBOARD`, not `0`). `DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS` (registry: 250 ms wall time, range 100-500, Player Controller) governs the switch; the swap is silent, takes at most one frame, shows no popup, and is latched while `channel_view.state` is not IDLE or a hold is non-zero (it updates at the next prompt show; the bar does not reset). Mixed input is permitted. The focus bracket shows on key or pad navigation and hides on mouse motion. The HUD renders the current binding for each `glyph_action` and survives any rebind. *Why.* One authority for device selection. *Edge notes.* The name is cited from the registry and given no HUD-side value.

**HS-36. Rail is correct when hidden.** *Rule.* The HUD is correct with the bottom-right rail covered by a co-op OVERLAY panel (the Chart Table's EC-CT-64 / R-AD-6): no HUD rule depends on the rail being visible, and nothing is drawn elsewhere to compensate. *Why.* Co-op pause is an overlay with the kernel RUNNING; the opaque Chart Table panel can cover the bottom-right (Vertical Slice B).

#### G. The prompt plate and the channel bar

**HS-37. The prompt rule.** *Rule.* One plate for the single focused candidate, drawn at `world_to_hud(anchor_o)` from the **unjolted** pose each render frame (so it re-projects at every zoom step `k`), and **only while `prompt_state` is not HIDDEN** (Interaction has already applied lighting and reach; the HUD draws exactly where Interaction says and never queries `lit`). Off-screen means no prompt: the plate is never clamped to the frame edge. `show = (prompt_state != HIDDEN) AND (stable_ticks >= PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS) AND (since_last_show >= PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS)` (Interaction D.UI-2's rule; the values are HUD-owned: `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` **13** (raised from 9 in the design review so that the debounce exceeds the 12-tick corridor churn: at 9 a plate that had been stable for 9 ticks showed for 3 ticks, 50 ms, before the next focus replaced it; range 13-20), `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` 20); **hide is immediate**, the same render frame, whenever `prompt_state` is HIDDEN or `focus_ref` changed. **An accepted press force-admits the plate:** if the focused candidate's plate has not been shown yet (inside the debounce, the minimum cycle or an arbiter wait), the Controller's accepted press (`confirm_view.pressed`, HS-6) shows it on that render frame so the ACK and the bar have a plate to ride on; **the force-admit is gated on `focus_view.prompt_state` being ENABLED or NEEDS_TOOL on that frame** (a press on a subject whose `prompt_state` is HIDDEN force-admits nothing and draws nothing, because a plate appearing on a press would otherwise tell the player that an unlit subject is there, a P3 oracle; AC-HS-143) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; this is an input-driven show-start (class B of HS-47) that skips the debounce, the minimum cycle and the arbiter's deferral, is never deferred, is rate-gated to one per `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` per seat, and is counted in the arbiter as a non-deferrable event (if accepted presses ever exceed the ceiling the HUD records `HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN` and does not drop the plate). A press the simulation rejects draws nothing further (HS-41). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The plate holds a keycap (the token for `glyph_action` on the current device) and the verb text from `verb_name_key`, in the instrument face; no condition or target text; one 4 x 3 art-px anchor tab on the edge nearest the anchor. Verb tokens: `verb_cut` ("Cut"), `verb_weld` ("Weld"), `verb_seam_cut` ("Cut away"), `verb_install` ("Install"), `verb_top_up` ("Top up", an MVP O2 TAP verb by user ruling X5; the token is Interaction's LS-26 entry, the English a placeholder) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, `verb_breaker`, `verb_valve`, `verb_door`, `verb_vent_hatch`, `verb_patch_self` ("Patch suit", the self plate below) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)*, and the closed carry set (`G . Drop`); `verb_confirm_prompt` (a HUD-owned string key; English "Confirm?" per the Controller's tap-to-confirm option, `confirm_mode` `TAP`, pci:841-846, pci:552) relabels the plate while a tap-confirm is armed. The keycap token is the Controller's closed `keycap_token` enumeration (single characters, F-keys, named keys drawn as a word or symbol, mouse buttons and wheel, pad buttons, the PlayStation symbol set inside the keycap); a token the HUD's own atlas lacks (the enumeration is the Controller's, the atlas is the HUD's) renders a wide keycap with `?` and counts `hud_unmapped_key_glyph`, never free text, so a rebind cannot change plate width. **Hold requirements are visible before the press:** when `focus_view.mode` is `HOLD_CONFIRM` the keycap draws its HOLD variant (a 1-px bar under the cap, one extra asset per keycap family), and in `TAP` mode (the Controller's `mode`, HS-6) a two-pip variant, so the player sees "this needs a hold" before pressing. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The plate is truth, meta register, never lie-capable. *Why.* "Hide is immediate because a prompt for a subject that is not lit is a lie by commission; show may be late because a late prompt omits a hint." *Edge notes.* The corridor can change focus every 12 ticks at 2 px/tick; the debounce (13 ticks, longer than the churn period) carries the guarantee Interaction's AC-IA-72 assigns to this GDD: no plate shows, even for one frame, under a 12-tick focus churn (replay AC-HS-54). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The self plate** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)*: when `focus_view.self_target` is true the single plate is the self-patch prompt (the Controller's PC-6 self-target, an `ACTOR_SELF` verb). Its keycap is the token for `glyph_action` (= `interact`) and its verb text comes from `verb_name_key` (= `verb_patch_self`, English placeholder "Patch suit"), both supplied by Interaction through `focus_view` like every other verb (the key is Interaction's, as `verb_top_up` is; it is added to the verb-token list above and is subject to the `HUD_DEATH_WORDS` and avoid-list lint, Tone and language), in the instrument face. It has no `anchor_o`, so it is **not** drawn at `world_to_hud(anchor_o)` but placed by HS-39's self-plate rule; the lit rule does not apply to it (`prompt_state` is set by Interaction, which ENABLES the self-target; the HUD still never queries `lit` and draws nothing while `prompt_state` is HIDDEN). The show / hide rule, the debounce (`PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS`), `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS`, the accepted-press force-admit (gated on ENABLED or NEEDS_TOOL as above) and the flash arbiter apply unchanged; it hides on the same `focus_ref` / `focus_seq` change as any plate, so it is cut on the render frame a world candidate takes focus. The channel bar (patch is a CHANNEL verb, Controller PC-7) rides on the self plate exactly as on any plate (HS-40, HS-41 unchanged). Like every HS-37 plate it is not drawn while `give_up_live` is true (HS-42(b) co-existence).

**HS-38. NEEDS_TOOL variant.** *Rule.* A notched chamfer plate, taller (about 18 versus 12 art px), with the 16 x 16 tool glyph of `focus_view.tool_required` in place of the verb. Not red, not flashing; same show / hide rules as HS-37. *Why.* The Interaction visual contract.

**HS-39. Placement and keep-out.** *Rule.* Placement follows Interaction's AD-5 (cited, not restated): beyond the subject on the line from the player through the anchor, then above, below, right, left. The chest keep-out `PROMPT_KEEPOUT_PX` 16 around the projected chest point is never overlapped, including in the fallback; the fallback offset is `PROMPT_ANCHOR_OFFSET_WORLD_PX` 28 (world px) after AD-5; `PROMPT_EDGE_MARGIN_PX` 4 from the safe margin. If no position satisfies keep-out and margin, the plate is **not drawn** (as off-screen), never clamped over the chest. Plate and bar sizes are HUD px, independent of zoom. The strip, panel and rail rectangles are an exclusion region: a prompt is moved or withheld, never drawn over a reading. *Why.* The chest lamp's beam origin is the chest point; a plate over it would mask the beam. The plate swims against its anchor by at most `H / 2` screen px because plates are integer multiples of `H`; this is the cost of that rule and is not a defect. **The give_up plate (HS-42(b)) uses the same keep-out:** it is placed with its nearest edge exactly `PROMPT_KEEPOUT_PX` (16) HUD px from the projected chest point (the same unjolted projection as the chest keep-out), centred on the axis, trying above, below, right, left in that order, subject to `PROMPT_EDGE_MARGIN_PX` 4 from the safe margin and to the strip, panel and rail exclusion rectangles. Unlike a prompt it is not tied to a world anchor, so it is never withheld for lack of an AD-5 side (the discoverability of the exit is its purpose): the layout function must find one of the four candidates at every offered `H` for any chest point the camera can produce (AC-HS-147 (c)); if none passes, `UI_PLACEMENT_WITHHELD` increments and that is a build-gate defect, not a runtime fallback. It never overlaps the chest keep-out. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: the Controller's 'at the chest point' conforms to this keep-out, PCI UI Requirements 1)* **The self plate (HS-37) uses the give_up plate's placement** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)*: nearest edge exactly `PROMPT_KEEPOUT_PX` (16) HUD px from the projected chest point (the same unjolted projection), centred on the axis, trying above, below, right, left in that order, subject to `PROMPT_EDGE_MARGIN_PX` 4 and the strip, panel and rail exclusion rectangles; AD-5's world-anchor side order and the `PROMPT_ANCHOR_OFFSET_WORLD_PX` fallback do not apply; it is never withheld for lack of an AD-5 side, and if none of the four passes at an offered `H`, `UI_PLACEMENT_WITHHELD` increments and that is a build-gate defect (as for the give_up plate, AC-HS-150), not a runtime fallback. The two plates never co-exist (HS-42(b)), so they never compete for the same side.

**HS-40. The channel bar: a linear bar at three length classes.** *Rule.* One linear bar with a needle-mark on the focused plate's long edge facing away from the anchor; never screen-fixed, never a ring or radial. "A linear bar at three sizes" is read as **three length classes keyed to `channel_view.length_class`** — SHORT, MEDIUM, LONG — with interior lengths `BAR_LEN_SHORT_PX` 16 / `BAR_LEN_MEDIUM_PX` 24 / `BAR_LEN_LONG_PX` 40 HUD art px `[PH]` (each 12-64, ordered S < M < L), **not three HUD scales** (HR-13 forbids any HUD scale change). `length_class` is Interaction's derivation (SHORT if HOLD_CONFIRM; MEDIUM if the declared duration is below `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` 240; else LONG), frozen at the press and never a function of `hp`. Anatomy: a track of outer height 6 (1 border, 4 interior, 1 border) on the plate's own Ground, fill in Tungsten fixture `#FFCC66` as stepped rectangles, a Paper White needle-mark 2 wide by 8 tall overhanging the track by one pixel above and below; `fill_px = floor(progress_pm * L / 1000)`. Fill steps are positional and are not luminance events. The bar stays until the simulation's abort even after the plate hides because `lit` was lost (at most 5 dark ticks). Interaction's Open Question 1 ("a linear bar at three sizes has no repo source") is answered *adopted* (three length classes; confirmed 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R20, OQ-HS-1) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R20)*. *Why.* The routed thread and HR-13; the past decision "a linear bar, never a ring".

**HS-41. Bar states, mapped to `channel_view.state`.**

| `state` | Bar | Plate | Cue source |
|---|---|---|---|
| IDLE | none | per `focus_view` | none |
| ACK (Adapter-local pre-play; never a simulation state, ia:304) | the keycap face drops 1 art px and the track appears at fill 0, from `confirm_view.pressed` (agreed shape, HS-6), on the same render frame as the accepted press, **force-admitting the plate if it was not yet shown** (HS-37); withdrawn within `PREPLAY_MAX_TICKS` 2 if no result arrives | stays | the press read |
| CONFIRMED (the simulation's confirmation) | keeps the pre-play (or draws the track at fill 0 at once if no ACK was drawn); a CONFIRMED that does not arrive within `PREPLAY_MAX_TICKS` withdraws the pre-play | stays | `transition_seq` |
| PROGRESS | `fill_px` and the needle-mark at the fill edge; stepped | stays; hides the same frame `prompt_state` goes HIDDEN, while the **bar stays until the simulation's abort** | `progress_pm` |
| ABORT | **by `channel_view.ended_by`, never `abort_cause`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the row said "one frame set for every cause", contradicting the cancel drain below, D.16 and AC-HS-65)*: `WORLD` (and `NONE`): the fill snaps empty and a 2 x 2 Ground break-notch is held `BAR_HOLD_DONE_TICKS` 12, then the bar is removed, the same frame set for every world cause; `PLAYER_CANCEL`: the fill drains right to left over `BAR_DRAIN_MS` 400 (D.16; reduced motion snaps to 0), then the bar is removed, with no notch | stays 12 ticks (WORLD) / until the drain ends (PLAYER_CANCEL) | `transition_seq`, `ended_by` |
| DONE | full; holds `BAR_HOLD_DONE_TICKS`, then a cut. No checkmark, flash, sparkle or "repaired" text | stays | `result_seq` |
| OUTCOME | fills exactly as DONE, then is removed at once; the plate border steps Graphite lit to Graphite mid for 12 ticks; no text says why | dimmed one step | `outcome` |
| immediate reject | nothing is drawn; any pre-drawn acknowledgement is withdrawn within one render frame; no shake, flash, red or text, identical for every reason. On a reject the bar is removed and the keycap is **restored and held in the one-step Graphite-lit state for `BAR_REJECT_HOLD_TICKS` 9 `[PH]`** (range 6-18; Interaction V-10 and IA-15, as amended in its design review; this knob is HUD-owned), with **at most one reject presentation per seat per `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS`**, so a rejected press leaves a perceivable trace on a plate that is shown, while still no shake, flash, red or text and the same frame set for every reason; a rejected press with no plate shown draws nothing (a dip on an unlit subject would be an oracle). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* This row is the HUD's entire response to a rejected press: there is no separate ignored-input glyph dim, no per-reason dim and no first-use caption (the Controller's `input_ignored_view` and captions are refused, K43; a visible response to a press at an unlit subject would reveal that a subject is there, P3). The one pre-press tell is Interaction's `press_blocked_moving` hollow keycap (HS-6), which depends on the presser's own state only. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated CD ruling)* | withdrawn | the result event `kind` REJECTED |

Cues are edge-detected from `transition_seq` / `result_seq` per seat (a render frame may span several ticks, so a terminal transition is never skipped; intermediate loops are skipped). Only the **local seat's** `channel_view` is drawn. *Edge notes.* The Controller GDD's phrase that the bar "holds then drains on a world abort" is the older text; this GDD adopts Interaction's snap-plus-notch, the later owner of verbs and visuals, and a refresh is owed to the Controller prose. **The cancel drain.** Interaction specifies a drain for a player cancel (its visual row V-6: "Cancel, hold released ... Bar drains right to left at `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE` x fill speed (cosmetic: the sim channel already ended)"; the channel table at ia:336: "the bar snaps empty (cancel drains at the cosmetic drain rate)"; IA-15 lists `CANCELLED` among the closed abort causes; AC-IA-100 expects four visibly different sequences — drain; snap with a 12-tick break-notch; full bar then plate dim; held full bar) **and** says in IA-41 that `abort_cause` is "diagnostic only; the HUD uses one abort frame set for every cause". Those two statements are inconsistent inside Interaction. Resolution: Interaction publishes the non-diagnostic `channel_view.ended_by` (`NONE | PLAYER_CANCEL | WORLD`, registry `ended_by`); the HUD draws the **drain** on `PLAYER_CANCEL` and the **snap with break-notch** on `WORLD`, and never reads `abort_cause`. **The drain is capped and written in integers** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the drain at the earlier `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE` x fill-speed scaled with the channel (a 360-tick weld bar took up to 3 s to empty, and two channels could overlap), so the HUD's cosmetic drain takes `BAR_DRAIN_MS` 400 `[PH]` from any fill and any `length_class` (D.16), and a new ACK snaps any drain in progress to its end before the new bar starts, so drains never stack. (For a hold-to-confirm release the Controller's own `b` drains at `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE` over 0.4 s from full, D.10, and the HUD draws `b`; the HUD adds no second drain.) The 400 ms cap shortens Interaction's V-6 cosmetic drain for long channels: a cross-GDD note to Interaction. **The one-tick race (why the Adapter's release read is not used):** a world abort inside tick t's hook can land before the Adapter's `cancel_channel` is applied as `IA_CANCEL_NOOP` at t+1 (ia:741), so a HUD keyed off the Adapter's release would draw a drain for an UNLIT abort; `ended_by` is written with the terminal state, so that abort reads `WORLD`.

**HS-42. Hold-to-confirm.** *Rule.* The same bar at SHORT on the focused control's plate, `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` 0.8 by default (48 ticks, 3 ticks per px; the player may adjust the hold, so the fill rate follows the Controller's `hold_pm` and the HUD holds no hold constant); release drains it (the Controller's own `b` drains at `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE`, D.10, and the HUD draws it); no Alarm Red, no mark, no dialog. **Two modes (`confirm_view.mode`, HS-6), both required at MVP:** `HOLD` fills the bar over `CONFIRM_HOLD_S`; `TAP` (the Controller's one-handed preset, `confirm_mode` `TAP`, `CONFIRM_TAP_WINDOW` 3 s) shows no fill but thickens the bracket and relabels the plate `verb_confirm_prompt` ("Confirm?") while the first tap is armed, and disarms on a focus or context change as the Controller says. The relabel, the bracket thickening and their disarm or expiry restore are **state-display draws of the shared limiter** (HS-47 class A, re-review): the Controller's armed state changes at once, the drawn relabel follows when the limiter admits it and always shows the latest armed state, and the second tap is never gated by what is drawn *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The hold fraction `b` is in neither Interaction simulation view; `focus_view` supplies `mode = HOLD_CONFIRM` and `duration_class = SHORT` (ia:310), which selects the SHORT bar, while the hold fraction and the armed state come from the Controller's Adapter-local `confirm_view` (HS-6; the shape is published at pci:953 and Interaction IA-41, with the `mode` / `armed` fields now published by the Controller, OQ-HS-18 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*). The 0.8 s hold is the core loop's main motor-accessibility barrier, which is why the tap mode is a setting at MVP and not an Alpha accessibility add-on (the two-tap alternative and the adjustable hold are defaults applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): D80); the Chart Table's menu items use the same component on layer 3 **for the look only** (its Delete hold of 1.6 s, 2 x `CONFIRM_HOLD_S`, is a default applied confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20), D81: 96 ticks over a 16-px track is 6 ticks per px, inside the 10-tick bound of Game Feel): the Chart Table's destructive-action bar snaps to empty on abort (CT-22); the release drain is the Controller's `b` for a hold (Chart Table C19). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *Why.* The Controller's UI Requirements and Interaction's statement that the simulation never sees a partial hold. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**(b) The give_up confirm plate** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; user ruling D7; CD ruling on PCI re-review)*. A DOWNED player has no focused object, so the HUD owns one DOWNED-only plate (surface `hud_give_up_plate`, HS-10) for the solo "give up and reload" action (user ruling D7; the Controller's PC-2 and UI Requirements 1). **Trigger:** `confirm_view.give_up_live` (HS-6) only; the HUD reads no `loco_state` and no Co-op state (the Controller folds DOWNED, solo, context `gameplay` and Co-op Session's revive gate, R9: undesigned, provisional, constant false at MVP, into the one bool). **Show:** on the first render frame it is true, with the track at fill 0 and no press needed (the exit is discoverable), without the prompt show-debounce; re-shows are spaced by EC-HS-57. **Contents:** one keycap from `glyph_view(seat, give_up)` (HOLD variant in HOLD mode, two-pip variant in TAP mode, as HS-37), the verb text from string key `plate_give_up` (English placeholder "Give up", `tr()`, instrument face), and the SHORT linear bar with needle-mark: `fill_px = (hold_pm * BAR_LEN_SHORT_PX) div 1000` with `BAR_LEN_SHORT_PX` 16 (0.8 s hold = 48 ticks = 3 ticks per px, as above); the HUD does no drain of its own (the Controller drains `hold_pm` at `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE` 2.0). A press (`confirm_view.pressed_action == give_up`) drops the keycap 1 art px (ACK, HS-41); in TAP mode the bracket thickens and the plate relabels `verb_confirm_prompt` while armed (class A state display, HS-47). **Hide:** on the first render frame `give_up_live` is false (recovery, DEAD, or a revive-capable seat in VS-B), with the bar zeroed; never drawn in any other state. It is not suppressed by `TRANSIT_HOLD` (statuses run in the pod and a DOWNED in the pod is the Controller's, D60), freezes undimmed under `PAUSED_FROZEN` like any plate, and after a restore into DOWNED it is drawn at fill 0 on the first `RUNNING` frame. **Co-existence:** while DOWNED the object prompt (HS-37) is not drawn (the Controller ignores `interact` in DOWNED), and that includes the self plate, which is an HS-37 prompt sharing this plate's placement *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)*, so at most one plate exists; the plate reuses the prompt-plate and bar components, adds one node and no new asset beyond the existing keycap variants. **Placement:** HS-39 (outside the chest keep-out, never withheld). **Wording:** verb text only, no cause, count, countdown, consequence or death word (X8; `HUD_DEATH_WORDS` lint, Tone and language); no Alarm Red, no mark. Truth, meta register, never lie-capable; no sound requested by the HUD. A result event REJECTED for `give_up` (for example the race with a revive) draws nothing: the plate is already gone. The plate depends on no world state (no lit, exposure, Attention or Horror read), so it cannot be an oracle (P3).

**HS-43. The carry state and the closed prompt set.** *Rule.* While carrying, the only prompts are `G . Drop` anchored at the foot origin plus the three carry-admitted verbs of Boarding's BS-9 (Interaction IA-28, registry `admitted_while_carrying`): `verb_install` (`install_module`) on a HARDPOINT whose `compatible_defs` lists the carried def, the pod-console hop plate (`pod_hop` on the CONSOLE), and the door face's ordinary V4 `set_opening` plate (the same plate and verb token as the uncarried door prompt, no new token, no carry-specific reason) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; nothing on any other world target. Any reason shown for an unavailable `pod_hop` uses Web Map's WM-9 reason words, and no plate or reason names mass, weight or condition; no carried-weight number, inventory or value tag is drawn (HS-32). The carried-module glyph and drop keycap are on the rail (HS-32). The thrown glowstick in flight is a cosmetic Adapter-side arc (Interaction's Open Question 7); the HUD draws nothing for it and the rail count steps on acceptance. *Why.* Boarding & Salvage's closed carry-prompt set and Assembly's "the prompt may be late, never wrong".

**HS-44. The one-bit harm prompt and the name reveal (reserved, Vertical Slice B).** *Rule.* When Sanity makes a crewmate target eligible, Interaction sets `prompt_state` and the HUD draws the ordinary plate: no red, no counter, no tell, no roster flag. A name-reveal component slot (an instrument-face name on a Ground plate) is reserved. Neither is MVP. *Why.* Anti-pillar "no intentional PvP": the prompt is the actor's own honest self-knowledge, never a crewmate readout.

**HS-45. Prompt debounce versus the flash budget.** *Rule.* `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS >= ceil(TICK_RATE / FLASH_MAX_PER_S)` = 20 is the registered two-bound constraint (floor 20 under the ruled counting unit; 40 is the tuning ceiling and no longer an edge-unit floor). A prompt show-start is also a deferrable event in the flash arbiter (HS-47), so the effective spacing with a mark and the honest grid running can exceed 20 ticks. **The consequence, stated plainly (STRICT mode, reduce flashing, the first-launch default, HS-47):** a breach in progress costs the honest numerals up to 2 events/s and the mark 0.47/s (solid at the default, so 0 there), leaving about 0.5 events/s for prompt show-starts when the mark pulses — roughly one admitted prompt every two seconds while the player is reaching for a valve. **In WEIGHTED mode** (reduce flashing off) the numerals and prompt show-starts are exempt, only the mark's 0.47/s counts, and the prompt waits for its debounce alone. This is acceptable because a held focus shows once and stays (the churn case is rare), and a prompt that is late is never wrong; but it is exactly what the Formulas worked example must show (the counting unit is now ruled). **Competence check (CD-GDD-ALIGN stage 12):** the Challenge aesthetic (ranked 2) is most at stake at this moment, because the player reaching for a valve must feel the prompt arrive in step with the reach. At MVP (no lying ticks) in STRICT mode *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the typical and the worst case are separated; the 717 ms figure had been presented as the typical first-launch latency)* the **typical** prompt show-start latency from a stable focus is the **debounce alone, 13 ticks (217 ms)**, because the honest grid puts at most two events in any 60-tick window and the prompt makes the third, which `admit` passes; the plate waits for a grid cell only when a **third counted event is already in the window**, and in the breach scenario that event is named: the one-event breath-source swap at the valve click (HS-12, HSV-42), a source-state hue cut, the ACK of an earlier press, or **the orphan hide of the previous plate** (a plate that was shown for another subject and hid within the last 60 ticks, D.2). The worst case with that event is the debounce plus at most one grid cell (HS-47: the prompt outranks the honest grid boundary and waits at most one cell **when at most one other counted event is in the window**), `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS + ceil(HONEST_TICK_MS * TICK_RATE / 1000)`; the worked example is 13 + 30 = 43 ticks (717 ms) at the defaults, and the bound with several such events is 13 + `MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 60 = 73 ticks (1,217 ms), after which the plate is force-admitted and counted; in WEIGHTED mode it is the debounce alone, 13 ticks (217 ms) (the debounce was 9, giving 39 ticks / 650 ms, before the design review raised it above the 12-tick corridor churn; the grid is pinned at 30 ticks so there is no shorter-grid case). The normative claim is the existing HS-47 rule and D.2's admission test; 43 is its example. **That latency applies only when the player has not yet pressed:** an accepted press force-admits the plate on the press frame (HS-37), so a player who reaches for the valve and presses early gets the ACK and the bar at once, and the wait (217 ms typically, 717 ms when a third counted event is in the window) is that of a player who has stopped to read. "Late is never wrong" stands on pillar grounds, but whether a breach-time valve prompt is ever perceived as late is a Game Feel acceptance hook (AC-HS-127) and a playtest question (OQ-HS-59). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Why.* Reflexion pattern 2: a formula with two bounds is registered as a formula, not as constants.

#### H. Meta notices, the flash arbiter, settings, reservations and neighbours

**HS-46. The failed-write notice and the first-frame line.** *Rule.* **Notice:** a 16 x 16 Paper White "record slot" glyph on a chamfered Ground plate at the suit panel's edge, with the one phrase (string key `notice_save_failed`, English placeholder "The game could not write its record.") in the reading face, shown only while Save/Load reports the failed-write escalation: `save_status` reads `FAILED` on every failed write (sl:739), and the notice is raised only after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` (registry: 3, range 1-10, Save/Load) consecutive failures (sl:739, sl:1006). **The HUD counts nothing**: it reads a Save/Load-published escalation flag (`fail_escalated`, bool, a Save/Load SL-21 host-local read, registry `save_status` companion_read; true from the 3rd consecutive failed write until the next `WRITTEN`); persistent, non-modal, no animation, no cause, cleared by the next `WRITTEN`; no Alarm Red, no Flesh, no Violet; never lie-capable. There is **no per-write glyph**: UI-1 is retired, `STATUS_WRITTEN_HOLD_TICKS` stays retired, and no sound, text or mark announces an autosave. **First-frame line (UI-7):** one plain reading-face line ("Newest record unreadable. Continuing from the one before it.", placeholder) on a Ground plate stacked at the panel's edge above the notice slot, on the first `RUNNING` frame after a fallback load, for `UI7_DWELL_S` 8 `[PH]` (range 5-15) and then a cut; if the Chart Table already shows it under Continue, the HUD shows nothing. **Second trigger, `DEATH_STUB` (user ruling D15 / D84):** on the first `RUNNING` frame after a restore whose `restore_reason` is `DEATH_STUB`, the same slot shows one plain ship-log line (string key `line_record_restored`, English placeholder "Restored from the last record."; no death wording, no cause, no count, no summary of what was lost) for `UI7_DWELL_S`, then a cut. The Chart Table shows nothing at a death restore (the restore is in-game and automatic, SL-7), so the HUD always shows it. **Both at once:** if the fallback flag is also set, the fallback line is shown and the death line is not (the fallback line already says the game is continuing from an earlier record; one line at a time, no queue). `restore_reason` stays a request: it keys this one line and alters nothing else (HS-53) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. (orchestrator ruling: the ui-programmer's "centre-low" placement is dropped; both meta plates share the panel's edge so nothing new enters the bottom-centre band.) *Why.* Save/Load's UI-10 and UI-7; the Chart Table's ASK-CT-5 agrees.

**HS-47. The flash arbiter (`hud_flash_budget`).** *Rule.* One arbiter counts luminance events per rolling second across the HUD. **Its clock is the committed tick `T`** (window `(T - TICK_RATE, T]`): `present_time` drives animation, not the budget; an event that originates in render time (a focus becoming stable, a press) is stamped with the tick current at that render frame, and while the kernel is `PAUSED` `T` does not advance, so no deferrable event is admitted while paused. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The event inventory** (every HUD luminance event, with its class; the events added by the review are marked *new*): **clock-locked, never delayed:** mark leading edges (at `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0`); honest-numeral **grid boundaries** at `T mod HONEST_GRID_TICKS == 0` at which at least one displayed digit actually changes (a boundary with no displayed change is not an event, so the 2 events/s is a ceiling reached only while the numerals are falling), which are **skip-only**: drawn on their own tick or skipped, never delayed. **Never rescheduled by the HUD:** lying-digit ticks (the Filter's schedule). **Follow the honest source state, counted, never deferred:** source-state hue cuts (HSV-4, L-5: a glyph or arc band changing between its loop hue and Graphite lit). **Source-state swap** *(new, re-review)*: the strip's `breath_src` swap (HS-12, HSV-42), counted once, never deferred, like a hue cut. **give_up plate show-start and hide** (HS-42(b), HSV-43) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: they follow `confirm_view.give_up_live`; the show is never deferred (it must appear on the first DOWNED frame), a hide is immediate and counts under the orphan rule if its show lies before the window; re-shows are spaced by `since_last_show >= PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` (EC-HS-57), so the plate contributes at most 3 show-starts per second; in WEIGHTED mode the show-start is treated as a prompt show-start for the `flash_weight_pm` exemption (neither counted nor deferred below `FLASH_AREA_EXEMPT_PM`), in STRICT it is counted and may overrun (`HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN` recorded, nothing dropped). **Orphan transitions** *(new, re-review)*: a plate hide whose show-start lies before the window, and a mark OFF edge whose ON edge does, are counted once in `N(w)` for the windows that contain them (they are known only when they occur, so they are non-deferrable and may overrun, D.2). **A counted mark event is an actual change in the drawn luminance of a mark slot, not a scheduled boundary** *(new, re-review)*: three cases were missing from the inventory. (1) A **leading edge** at `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` counts only when the slot is active and was drawn OFF (outline to fill); at a tick where the mark is already drawn ON (held solid by STRICT or reduced motion, held by pause, or bridged after a resume, HS-23) the natural edge changes nothing and is **not** counted. (2) A **mid-phase appearance**: an inactive slot becoming active draws the filled square at once when it is solid (STRICT, reduced motion) or when the live phase is inside the duty (D.4), which is one counted, non-deferrable event at that tick; in the pulsing case outside the duty it draws the OFF outline (a luminance change, one event) and the next leading edge is then counted normally. (3) The **pause-induced edge** (entering the hold from an OFF phase) is one event, per HS-23. The scheduled mark entry in `W(t)` is conditional: it exists only for a slot that is active **and** pulsing (not solid, not paused), so a solid mark reserves no capacity. **Input-driven, two classes** *(re-review: the earlier single class, "counted, never deferred", let spam raise the HUD above the ceiling and is replaced)*: **(A) State displays**, whose *simulation state changes at once and is never delayed* but whose *drawn state* goes through **one shared coalescing limiter** for the whole class: the tool-switch bracket and Tungsten move (HSV-13), the glowstick count substitution (HSV-15; the suppressant's, HSV-14, is dormant until the tool returns), the lamp-switch glyph (HS-19, HSV-12), and the TAP-mode "Confirm?" relabel and its disarm or expiry restore (HS-42, *new to the inventory*), the give_up plate's TAP relabel and bracket (HS-42(b)), and the hollow-keycap tell of `press_blocked_moving` on and off (HS-6; admitted through the one limiter, latest state always shown) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. The limiter admits at most one state-display draw per `GLYPH_STATE_LATCH_TICKS` 20 across the **whole class** (so at most 3 per rolling second from the class, not 3 per kind), each admitted draw shows the **latest** simulation state (intermediate states are never drawn, the count and the bracket jump straight to the current value), and each admitted draw is also a deferrable event through `admit` (so it yields to prompts and the grid and never lands in a full window); a pending draw that has waited `MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 60 is force-admitted and counted (overrun, recorded). The final drawn state therefore equals the simulation state within `MAX_DEFER_TICKS` ticks of the last change (AC-HS-140). **(B) Acknowledgement cues**, which cannot be deferred without a press feeling unreceived (Game Feel step 2, the same render frame as the accepted press): the force-admitted show-start of an accepted press and the ACK keycap dip (one event, they land on the same frame, HS-37, HSV-24), the reject keycap hold (HS-41), the keycap dip on a `give_up` press (ACK, one event, rate-gated to one per `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` per seat, HS-42(b)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, and the OUTCOME plate-border step (HSV-29), which follows the simulation's result. They are counted and never deferred, **rate-gated by their own gates** (at most one ACK-plus-force-admit and at most one reject presentation per seat per `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS`; one OUTCOME step per channel), and **may overrun a full window**: an overrun is recorded as `HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN` and nothing is dropped. **Deferrable:** prompt show-starts with no press; the scanner reading's show-start (HSV-34, *new*); the notice and first-frame-line show-starts; and the state-display class (A) as above (lowest priority). Positional changes (bar fill, needle motion, arc settle, charge-fill steps) are not luminance events. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Two counting modes (user rulings D91 (iv) and D79)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: `flash_mode` is STRICT while `reduce_flashing` is on (its first-launch default) and WEIGHTED otherwise. STRICT counts every event of the inventory above, as before. WEIGHTED weights a flash by the area it changes (WCAG 2.3.1; the registry's `flash_count` takes a region as a ninth of the displayed picture) and exempts exactly two classes, **honest-numeral digit swaps (grid boundaries) and prompt show-starts**, when the event's `flash_weight_pm` is below `FLASH_AREA_EXEMPT_PM` (D.2). An exempt event is neither counted nor deferred, so a prompt waits for its debounce alone; the grid stays clock-locked and skip-only in both modes because it is a shared cadence, not a budget decision. **The Alarm Red mark's edges are always counted strictly, in both modes** (a red flash is held to the strict ceiling; `flash_count` includes red flash). Every other event of the inventory (source-state hue cuts, input-driven small-area events, notice, scanner and lamp-glyph changes) stays counted in both modes. Lying-digit ticks are the Filter's schedule and are counted in both modes until the Filter adopts the same weighting (note to the Perception Filter). A deferrable event is admitted at tick `t` only if the count in the half-open window `(t - TICK_RATE, t]` plus itself plus the clock-locked events already scheduled in that window stays at or below `FLASH_MAX_PER_S`; otherwise it waits. The normative admission test is the Formulas entry D.2 (`hud_flash_budget`), which also checks every forward window ending at an already-scheduled clock-locked event in `(t, t + TICK_RATE)`; this sentence is its summary.
