Hide events are never deferred. **Priority order (HUD-owned; it never touches the Filter's scheduler; in WEIGHTED mode the exempt classes leave the order and it governs what remains):** hides are never deferred; mark edges are never deferred; Filter lying-digit ticks are never rescheduled; among the HUD-owned deferrable events (the grid boundary is skip-only, never delayed) the order is prompt show-start, then the honest grid boundary, then notice or scanner show-start, then the state-display class (the lamp-switch glyph, the bracket, the count, the TAP relabel). **Grid-skip rule, with its bounds** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: a grid boundary that would exceed the budget while a prompt show-start waits is skipped, and the next boundary shows the then-latest value; **a boundary is never skipped twice in a row** (`GRID_MAX_CONSEC_SKIPS` 1, a rule constant, not a knob): the boundary after a skip is admitted and counted whatever waits, so **numeral lag is bounded by `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS` unconditionally**. A waiting prompt then waits **at most one grid cell when at most one other counted event is in the window** (the case of the breach example), and otherwise at most **`MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 60** `[PH]` ticks (range 30-120; one full window), after which it is force-admitted and counted as an overrun, so every deferrable event (prompt, notice, scanner, state display) has a stated worst-case wait and none can starve; the previous claim "a prompt waits at most one grid cell" held only with at most one other counted event, because nothing limited consecutive skips, and is restated with its precondition. The scanner reading's `SCAN_READING_DWELL_S` starts at its **admission**, not at the scan result, so a deferred show-start cannot eat the dwell (the reference breach scenario in Game Feel confirms the order: AC-HS-127; the bound cases are AC-HS-140 and AC-HS-141). The inspect card's honest numeral (In-World Instruments) shares the same grid boundary and adds no event; the card's lying digits are Filter-scheduled, counted and never delayed. Positional changes (bar fill steps, needle motion, arc settle) are not luminance events. If the arithmetic shows that the sum can exceed `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` with every HUD-owned event at its floor — which it can at Vertical Slice A when a lying digit's schedule (3.0 transitions/s at `N_r` 2 or more) already fills the ceiling — that is an **ASK to the Filter and the accessibility-specialist**, never a HUD override; the HUD records `HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN` and does not change the Filter's schedule. *Why.* The Filter owns the tick scheduler; the HUD owns only its own events. **The counting unit is ruled (user, D91 (ii))** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: a flash is one pair of opposing transitions and a window's flash count is `ceil(n_transitions / 2)` (registry `flash_count`). The arbiter counts *leading-edge events* (mark cycle, grid boundary, show-start, substitution), and D.2 shows that this count bounds `ceil(n / 2)` from above, so staying at or below `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` events keeps the ruled count at or below it; under it the mark is 0.47/s and the honest grid 2/s. The edge unit (each edge of a cycle counted) is retired. The literal `ceil(n / 2)` would admit more single transitions per second; the HUD does not use that headroom, because it would unpin the grid proof of HS-14 and the Filter counts its own ticks separately. *Edge notes.* Unreachable at MVP (lies are dormant), but the honest-numeral grid already depends on the unit.

**HS-48. One `Theme`, one component set.** *Rule.* The HUD owns the single Godot `Theme` (`ui_theme_main`) and the shared components: keycap, Ground plate, chamfered bezel, focus bracket, linear bar with needle-mark, tabular-numeral label, gleam-on-glass helper and nine-slice bezels. They are specified in `design/ux/interaction-patterns.md`, which the HUD's UX spec lands first; the HUD's UX spec lands first and the Chart Table cites it (dependency lateral, Soft). The shared component is the look only; each owner keeps its behaviour (the HUD's release drain, HS-42; the Chart Table's snap on abort, CT-22; Chart Table C19). *Why.* The HUD has the more constrained components (7-px numerals, 16-px glyphs, opaque plates) and is the earlier MVP surface. Refresh owed to the Chart Table's row once this GDD exists.

**HS-49. Text and localisation.** *Rule.* All text through `tr()` with StringName keys; a missing key renders the key and counts `UI_MISSING_KEY`; plates wrap to two lines at `PROMPT_PLATE_MAX_W_PX` 72 and never truncate or shrink the face; a string that still overflows two lines **grows the plate taller** (never clips, never shrinks the face) and counts `hud_plate_overflow`; the bound a test asserts is "at most two lines at 72 HUD px for the shipped English and the +40 % pseudo-locale, and the plate never enters the exclusion region (the strip, panel and rail rectangles)" (orchestrator ruling closing the QA-lead's F-4: grow, not clip); a +40 % pseudo-locale lint is run on every plate. Item Database `name_key` strings appear on focus or inspect only. The instrument face is Latin-only by construction; locales without coverage render verb text in the reading face with the plate geometry taken from a measurement of the face actually used (text-measurement calls are NOT SOURCEABLE in the pinned reference). Digits are drawn from the tabular digit cells of the instrument atlas, so a tick substitution is a region swap and cannot reshape text. *Why.* Interaction's localisation rule and Assembly's open item 13 (do not reuse `verb_cut`). Right-to-left fill direction and CJK / Arabic coverage are open (Open Questions).

**HS-50. `HudSettings`: hooks, not screens.** *Rule.* One `HudSettings` resource carries `ui_scale_step`, `reading_face_scale`, `reduced_motion`, `reduce_flashing` (ON at first launch, user ruling D79), `aim_indicator` (off, user ruling D9), `hud_opacity` (100 %, hidden at MVP), `pattern_strength` (0), `captions` (off), plus the Filter's three published scalars consumed, not owned (`reduced_motion_glide_pm`, `breath_depth_pm`, `lie_tick_min_ms`). They are anchors (HR-6): no game state reads or writes them. The screens, the tiers (21 / 15 versus 24 / 18 px) and the opacity floor belong to Accessibility Settings and Settings & Input Rebinding *(provisional — both Alpha, no file)*. The project's accessibility tier is **standard** (D80, default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20)); `design/accessibility-requirements.md` is authored later with `/ux-design accessibility` and is not created here *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **There is no HUD opacity setting at MVP**: the hook exists at 100 % and is not exposed; Alpha decides the floor with a measured contrast over the beam core. *Why.* HR-1 / HR-2 (opaque Ground plates); the Pipeline's "hooks exist from MVP".

**HS-51. Co-op reservations.** *Rule.* One `HudLayout` function carries named anchors: `crew_strip` (top-left), `chat` (left edge below it), `caption` (the plate slot for sound captions), `own_identity` (on the suit panel); nothing is drawn in them at MVP. Layout is a **pure function of `(picture rect, H)`** and never of game state; the render pipeline's root is its only caller. Local seat only; addressing by PlayerId, never `peer_id`; pings may mark a thing, never locate a crewmate. **Captions (Alpha):** if the `caption` slot is filled, the HUD draws only the plate and repeats Adaptive Audio's AA-41 constraints: a Ground `#1C2128` plate with Paper White `#DDE4EA` text; no family colour, icon colour or direction arrow; shown on the sound's start; captionable families only (`wit`, `ann`, `run`, `suit`, `foot`, `ia` terminals, `asm`, `body`, `pod`, `ui`); never the tell, breach, alarm cadence, pre-tell creaks, a Horror, `hal` or `p1c`. Caption policy and content stay Audio's (AAV-38). The Suit's `cap.suit.*` caption ids (user ruling D44, accepted) arrive through this same slot under AA-41; the HUD adds none, and haptics are not a HUD surface (if a tactile channel is ever added it is driven only by the player's own true body-cue events, Audio AA-42) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *Why.* The art bible: "designed now so the single-player HUD leaves room"; layout that depends only on the settings is not a "shift" under HR-6. *(provisional — Crew Strip, Co-op Session, Chat / Voice / Pings undesigned)*

**HS-52. No cursor and no mouse capture in `gameplay`.** *Rule.* The OS cursor is hidden in `gameplay` (mouse and pad) and shown in the non-gameplay contexts `chart_table` and `chat`; in `inspect_card` it stays **hidden (inert)**, because `set_aim` stays live on a mouse there and a visible cursor would be a second pointer over the beam (In-World Instruments OQ-IW-21, AC-IW-103); the HUD captures no mouse input; every HUD node is non-focusable and mouse-ignoring in `gameplay` (4.5 recursive mouse-filter disable; 4.6 dual focus means a focusable prompt could steal gamepad focus from a menu). The chest beam is the pointer; a UI cursor would be a second pointer over the dark. Assembly's in-world socket cursor is unrelated. **The one exception (user ruling D9, 2026-10-27):** an **opt-in, off-by-default accessibility aim indicator** (`HudSettings.aim_indicator`, never enabled by game state). While it is on the HUD draws one `hud_aim_indicator` node: a direction mark only, not a reticle or crosshair on a target and not an aim line to a distance. Invariants: (a) it shows only the aim direction the Controller already publishes (its 16 aim steps) and nothing about any target, range, lit state, exposure or hit; (b) it sits at a fixed radius from the projected chest point, outside the `PROMPT_KEEPOUT_PX` 16 keep-out, and never masks the beam origin; (c) it adds no light or glow (layer 2 receives none) and has no hue beyond Paper White; (d) it captures no mouse input and takes no focus; (e) it is class M, honest, never lie-capable, and not an Attention indicator (it echoes the player's own input); (f) with the setting off no such node exists and the forbidden-surface lint passes (AC-HS-7, AC-HS-134). Its drawn form and radius are the UX spec's (UI Requirements item 10, OQ-HS-78); it does not replace the in-world lamp-off cue below, which stays the default answer for every player *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *Why.* P1 / P3. *Edge notes.* With the lamp off and mouse aim there is no beam, so there is no beam-as-pointer. **P3 makes restraint and going dark a real strategy, so the interface must not punish it: if going dark also removed the mouse player's aim reference, the HUD would penalise restraint where only the world should.** This is therefore an explicit UX-spec requirement (UI Requirements, "Items carried to the UX specs", item 10; OQ-HS-63, owner ux-designer): the answer must be a **non-HUD cue inside the world picture** (the avatar's facing or hand-pose read), and **never a HUD cursor, reticle or crosshair**, which the anti-combat guard rail and HS-DT-2 forbid. Cursor-mode API names are NOT SOURCEABLE in the pinned reference. **Default stated as an outcome (design review 2026-10-27):** with the lamp off, a throw's direction is predictable to within the Controller's aim quantum (its 16 aim steps; the Controller's Art and UX asks, item 27 (i), asks for a non-lit lamp-housing swivel cue on the chest plate readable at 16 steps with no light and no glow). A hand pose quantised to five facings is too coarse for throws on its own, so the cue that carries the outcome is that chest-plate swivel, not the facing pose; and because the Pipeline's PR-4 rule 5 forbids rotating human-built geometry at runtime, it is authored as 16 pre-drawn steps, not a rotation. **Owners:** the Controller (the aim quantum and the swivel ask), the Avatar Presenter (self-visibility with the lamp off, the 16 step frames) and 2D Lighting (no light, no glow, readable at the darkest ambient the avatar sprite is drawn in); due by MVP sprint planning and in any case before `/ux-design` for `design/ux/hud.md` begins (default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): D93; OQ-HS-63). AC-HS-126 is a **go / no-go for the vertical slice**. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**HS-53. Pause, DOWNED, DEAD, restore and transit.** *Rule.* The full state table is in States and Transitions. In brief: solo pause freezes the HUD undimmed, with one exception: an active mark is held ON solid (HS-23); DOWNED keeps the readings updating and the lamp lit with marks persisting, and additionally draws the one `give_up` confirm plate of HS-42(b) while `confirm_view.give_up_live` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; DEAD freezes the readings with no death screen, score or text; the first `RUNNING` frame after a restore draws the readings at restored values with no settle, no abort frame, no notch, clears every presentation-local counter (debounce, hold grid, bar fill, tick phases, flash history), and reads `restore_reason` only as a request, never altering hallucination content from `DEATH_STUB` (it keys only the one record line of HS-46); `TRANSIT_HOLD` and the pod leg leave the HUD's layout unchanged while statuses run in the pod (user ruling D60): the readings keep updating from `perceived` as in `RUNNING` and stay lie-capable, and a death in the pod is the Controller's `DEAD` (the DEAD state below). **Who owns the visible step from death to restore, and what it is** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; Save/Load SL-7)*: **Save/Load owns it, and it is automatic.** At the tick boundary where the seat becomes `DEAD`, Save/Load settles or abandons the in-flight write, picks and stages a generation (SL-8), and, once its epoch write is durable (SL-23 (2), normally the next frame), calls `request_restore` with `restore_reason = DEATH_STUB`; the DEAD state's frozen readings hold across that interval *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: R14)*; the kernel enters `LOADING` (the command queue is flushed); the Pipeline shows layer-1 black (`TRANSIT_HOLD`) while the HUD stays unchanged, holding its last model; the first `RUNNING` frame is the `RESTORE_FRAME` below, with the camera cut at the pre-load zoom step. **The player presses nothing, and there is no designed dwell** (no death screen, no dim-out, no sound, SL-7), so the HUD's DEAD state lasts only until the restore completes; if no candidate loads (`SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`) the Chart Table shows a boot-flow screen (Save/Load OQ-42). The cryo re-wake sequence is Failure Contract & Re-wake's at Vertical Slice A. The HUD adds nothing to this step beyond the one record line on the first `RUNNING` frame (HS-46); AC-HS-132 and AC-HS-136 assert the HUD's side of it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **Whether the player understands why the game restarted** (P5 depends on it; the one peripheral line after a hard cut may read as a crash) is a **measured playtest question, OQ-HS-84** (pass bar: 7 of 8 testers can say why); the ruling is not re-opened, and any fix is owned by the world or by Save/Load, never by HUD text *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. *Why.* P5 and the user decision that MVP death restarts from the last autosave (owned by Save/Load).

**HS-54. The bottom row must fit at every offered `H` and display (`bottom_row_fit`).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *Rule.* The panel, the strip and the rail, their minimum gaps and the safe margins must fit the HUD canvas at **every `H` that `offered(P_w, P_h)` returns** (D.8, one closed function and one table, including 1366 x 768, 1600 x 900 and 2048 x 1152) on every supported picture (1280 x 720, 1280 x 800 `keep` and `expand`, 1920 x 1080, 2560 x 1440, 3840 x 2160 and an ultrawide). The test is **per side**, because the strip is centred: `max(W_panel, W_rail) <= side_avail(P_w, H) = ((canvas_w_int - W_strip) div 2) - margin_hud_x - GAP_MIN_PX` (about 102 HUD px each at 1280 x 720, `H` 4, not 204 for the pair), plus a **vertical** fit (`margin_hud_y + H_row(arr) <= canvas_h_int * frac(arr) div 100`, per arrangement, plus the notice-stack fit `fit_notice`, D.8; the single 100 x 44 budget of the earlier text is replaced) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the old sum is only a quick reject. The strip is counted at its 76-HUD-px plate cap (HS-11), once and everywhere. The budgets the UX spec is sized against are the **per-arrangement budgets of D.8** (ROW 144 x 40, COLUMN 74 x 80, `RAIL_MAX_W_PX` 100, `ROW_H_MAX_PX` 44 for ROW; the art-director's 100 x 44 box is withdrawn because the panel's own content does not fit it, HS-16, D.8) `[PH]`; a drawn panel that needs more shrinks `offered()` and needs the accessibility-specialist's sign-off *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **MVP policy (a stopgap only):** the settings offer exactly `offered(P_w, P_h)` and nothing else, which is option (a) as the Pipeline's PR-12 already states it ("H 5 and 6 are not offered at a 1280-px picture"). Its cost is recorded, with an owner (the accessibility-specialist, OQ-HS-34): on every `k_auto` = 2 display (720p, 1366 x 768, the Steam Deck) the ceiling is `H` 4 under the COLUMN arrangement at its 50 % share and **`H` 3 (100 %) under ROW**, against the art bible's 100-200 % (HR-12), so the largest reachable numeral cap height there is 28 screen px (21 / 28 against 21 / 28 / 35 / 42 on a 1080p screen) under COLUMN and 21 under ROW (D.8 arrangement table; OQ-HS-34, OQ-HS-81) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Option (b), a deterministic stacked layout under a width breakpoint (a pure function of `(picture, H)`, never of game state, so not a "shift" under HR-6), is to be evaluated **before Alpha** by the ux-designer; option (c), slim variants, remains open. The binding constraint is unchanged: **no `(picture, H)` pair that the settings offer may produce an overlap or a rectangle outside the safe margin**; the layout function returns a defect flag for any other pair and the pipeline root falls back to the layout at **`H_default`** (which is always offered; the earlier "keep the previous valid layout" is withdrawn because after a window resize or at first mount there is no previous layout). **A drawn feasibility sketch is a prerequisite of `/ux-design`:** one image, at 1280 x 720 / `H` 4 and at 1920 x 1080 / `H` 3, showing the strip (76 x 18), the panel in **each candidate arrangement** (ROW at most 144 x 40, COLUMN at most 74 x 80, with the ambient-O2 slot, the ship-alarm slot and the notice stack drawn) and the rail (at most 100 x 44) with the 16 x 9 HUD-px margin, the 4-px gaps and the chest keep-out, filed beside the UX spec before the spec is started (owner: the art-director with the ux-designer). *Why.* Reflexion pattern 6: an invariant written against the default must be re-checked at every reachable state. *Edge notes.* At the top of the range every `k_auto >= 3` display still has at least 102 HUD px per side (D.8 table).

**HS-55. Layout invariants are asserted at every reachable state.** *Rule.* Every layout invariant — the strip fits `strip_budget`, every plate lies inside the 5 % safe margin, no plate overlaps the chest keep-out, every edge is on a whole HUD px, `Control.scale` is (1, 1) — is asserted at every `H` in `offered(P_w, P_h)` (D.8) and at each display in HS-54, not at the 1080p default only. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The safe margin is computed in integer screen pixels `(5 * P + 99) div 100` per axis and converted to HUD px with a ceiling, so it is never below 5 % and is testable with no node. *Why.* Reflexion pattern 6.

**HS-56. Non-Ground area at rest.** *Rule.* At rest (all readings OK, no prompt, no mark, no notice, no `give_up` plate — the plate exists only in DOWNED, never at rest *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; the baseline is measured with no tool in hand that has an `ACTOR_SELF` row, because the self plate (HS-37) is a prompt *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)*) the number of HUD-layer pixels that are neither Ground `#1C2128` nor transparent, captured with the world hidden and the root clear colour set to a sentinel, is at most `HUD_NONGROUND_PX_MAX` = 1 % of the 640 x 360 HUD canvas = 2,304 HUD px `[PH]` (range 1.0-1.5 %): `nonground_count`. This is the measurable form of the art bible's "about 1 %, tunable". It is a screenshot-diff criterion (manual, with a retained screenshot). *Why.* P1: the world, not the HUD, is the picture. The art-director's first allocation (strip about 300, panel about 875, rail about 885, headroom 184 of 2,304) is an estimate the UX spec re-measures. The 1-HUD-px letterbox hairline the Pipeline suggests is **declined for the HUD layer** (a 640 x 360 perimeter hairline is 1,996 px, 87 % of the budget); if the Pipeline wants one it is drawn on layer 1 and excluded from the count.

**HS-57. Neighbour boundaries.** *Rule.* **Inspect card:** layer 2, frame and components HUD-owned; content, context and card-gauge parity (the card reads the same `perceived` ref as the wall gauge and lies exactly when it lies) are In-World Instruments' *(designed)*. Instruments accepts this division (iw:508, iw:529), adopts `HONEST_TICK_MS` for the card numeral and requests its flash slots from `hud_flash_budget` (iw:264), confirms HS-33 (the field-check reading is the HUD's, iw K-9), registers `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` as its own constant at 16 unchanged (iw:1706, iw:1076; registry entry `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX`, source Instruments), places the card outside the at-rest non-Ground count (iw:327), and asks the HUD to repeat three clauses, which this GDD repeats here and in Acceptance Criteria: the HUD draws **no sanity surface**; the HUD **may not read `node()` or `edge()`**; and HS-56's non-Ground cap is measured **at rest only** (no prompt, mark, notice or card). **Debug Overlay:** layer 4, a `DIAGNOSTIC` subscriber, dev builds only, never an accessibility feature *(designed)*; its P-1 (do:423, do:783) asks the HUD's Acceptance Criteria to repeat "no `true_value` call site" (AC-PF-51) and "no Overlay control in any HUD state", and no HUD node hosts, instantiates or parents an Overlay node. The HUD exposes a read-only `hud_debug_snapshot()`; it carries **no `k`**, because no HUD code reads `k` (HS-2) and the Overlay reads `k` from the Pipeline. The Overlay consumes the snapshot as a read-only channel (do:417, do:777): it carries `H`, the picture rect, the plate rectangles, the mark phase, the last model and the nine AC-HS-122 counters, with no `k`; the HUD exposes no other overlay hooks. **Onboarding:** no tutorial overlay, hint or tooltip at MVP; a future first-use prompt uses the existing plate *(provisional — Onboarding undesigned)*. The Controller's proposed first-use captions (first MOVING rejection, first NOT_LIT press) and its Settings-data "seen" flags are refused: captions break anti-pillar 4 at MVP, and a caption keyed to a NOT_LIT press is an unlit-subject oracle (P3); the three core rules are taught by the world in the Cryo Bay onboarding. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated CD ruling)* **Player Controller:** the HUD draws the DOWNED `give_up` confirm plate (HS-42(b)) and the MOVING pre-press tell via `focus_view.press_blocked_moving` (HS-6); it refuses `input_ignored_view` and the NOT_LIT dim (K43). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated CD ruling)* **Chart Table:** layer 3; in solo pause the HUD stays visible and frozen behind it. *Why.* Reflexion patterns 1 and 11.

### States and Transitions

The HUD has no simulation state. Its states are **presentation states** that follow the Pipeline's state (`PAUSED_FROZEN`, `TRANSIT_HOLD`) and the local seat's life state, plus a few presentation-local latches. No HUD state is ever written to the simulation, saved, replicated or hashed; on a restore every presentation-local counter is cleared (HS-53).

#### Surface-level states

| State | Entry | Exit | HUD behaviour | Who may cause the transition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **UNMOUNTED** | Boot, or the front-end (Chart Table main menu) with no world loaded | The world scene is live and the first committed tick exists | Nothing drawn; no model; no read of `view()` before the first commit (draw the last committed state; none means nothing) | Pipeline / boot flow |
| **RESTORE_FRAME** (one render frame) | The first `RUNNING` frame after any load or restore | The next render frame | Readings drawn directly at the restored values (needle at the restored fraction, numerals set directly, the hold grid armed from that frame); **no settle, no abort frame, no break-notch**; debounce counters, bar fill and drain, tick phases, flash history and the device latch are cleared; the first-frame line (HS-46: after a fallback load or a `DEATH_STUB` restore) may start; the mark phase comes from the live `T` with no start artefact; `restore_reason` is read only as a request and never alters hallucination content from `DEATH_STUB` (it keys only the one record line) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Save/Load (a restore), kernel boot |
| **RUNNING** | RESTORE_FRAME done | `PAUSED`, `TRANSIT_HOLD`, DOWNED, DEAD | Full behaviour: readings pulled every frame, prompt and bar live, mark on the beacon grid | Kernel |
| **PAUSED_FROZEN** (solo pause) | The kernel is `PAUSED` | Resume | The last frame is held (one exception, below), **not dimmed, blurred or desaturated**; readings frozen (`present_time` and `T` stop); **an active mark is held ON solid, the one stated exception to "last frame held" (HS-23)**; the prompt and bar freeze; layer 3 (Chart Table) is live on process time over the HUD; on exit a forced discrete rebuild (HS-7) and a clamped time step (HS-8), and the mark stays ON until the next leading edge (the resume bridge, HS-23) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Kernel pause; Chart Table |
| **OVERLAY** (co-op pause, Vertical Slice B) | A co-op overlay opens with the kernel RUNNING | The overlay closes | The HUD runs normally; the bottom-right rail may be covered by the opaque overlay panel; no HUD rule depends on rail visibility (HS-36) | Chart Table / Co-op Session *(provisional)* |
| **TRANSIT_HOLD** / pod leg | The pod hop begins | Arrival | The HUD's layout is unchanged (HR-3: layer 1 goes black, the HUD stays) but **statuses run in the pod** (user ruling D60): the readings keep updating from `perceived`, and the needle, numerals, marks and arbiter behave as in RUNNING; still lie-capable; the prompt is hidden unless a lit plate exists; a death in the pod is the Controller's DEAD (below) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Web Map & Transit, Suit, Controller |
| **DOWNED** | The Suit's `locomotion_cap` is NONE with `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` running | Revive (Vertical Slice B), the solo "give up and reload" action (user ruling D7, the Controller's) or DEAD | Readings keep updating; the lamp stays lit; marks persist (FAILED / DOWNED persists the mark); no banner; walk-only is surfaced by the readings, never by text; the HUD draws exactly one thing for the give-up action, the DOWNED-only `give_up` confirm plate (`hud_give_up_plate`, HS-42(b)), shown for the whole time `confirm_view.give_up_live` is true (the Controller's `loco_state == DOWNED` in solo, withheld whenever another bound seat could revive the actor) and gone on the first frame it is false; no banner, no tint, no death wording (X8, Tone and language `HUD_DEATH_WORDS`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; CD ruling on PCI re-review: the entry condition stays the Suit's `locomotion_cap` NONE for the readings; the plate's trigger is only `confirm_view.give_up_live`, a single source)* | Suit |
| **DEAD** | The local seat is DEAD (on a sortie or in the pod, user ruling D60) | A restore | Readings freeze at their last drawn values (the HUD stops pulling and holds the last model); **no death screen, no score, no text, no fade**; the last light and the cryo-bay lamp are world beats of Failure Contract & Re-wake at Vertical Slice A (the MVP stub has none, SL-7). The step from DEAD to the restore is **Save/Load's and automatic** (HS-53): it stages the generation at the `DEAD` boundary and requests the `DEATH_STUB` restore once its epoch write is durable (SL-23 (2), normally the next frame), so this state lasts only those staging frames plus the `LOADING` time *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: R14)*; the first `RUNNING` frame then shows the one record line of HS-46 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Suit, Save/Load |
| **NOTICE_FAILED** (substate of RUNNING and the other drawn states) | `save_status = FAILED` and the escalation flag is set (`fail_escalated`, Save/Load-published after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` consecutive failures; CF-HS-21) | The next `WRITTEN` | The notice (glyph and one phrase) is visible at the suit panel's edge; no animation; no other change | Save/Load |
| **FIRST_FRAME_LINE** (substate) | RESTORE_FRAME after a fallback load (unless the Chart Table has already shown the line) or after a `DEATH_STUB` restore (user ruling D15 / D84; the fallback line wins if both apply) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | `UI7_DWELL_S` elapsed (on `present_time`) | One reading-face line on a Ground plate; cut in, cut out | Save/Load, Chart Table |
| **SCALE_CHANGE** (transient) | `H` changed in Settings, **the picture rect changed (a window resize, an aspect change) or `k_auto` changed** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **A zoom-ladder step does not enter SCALE_CHANGE** (re-review): the layout is a pure function of `(picture rect, H)` and the zoom ladder changes neither, so a zoom step recomputes no layout and only the prompt plate re-projects (HS-37, EC-HS-2) | The next render frame | A cut, never a tween; the layout is recomputed (HS-51, HS-54); the prompt re-projects; the bar's fill is recomputed from `progress_pm`; **no simulation effect** | Settings, Controller (zoom) |
| **DEVICE_LATCHED** (substate) | `channel_view.state` is not IDLE, or a hold is non-zero | IDLE and hold zero | The glyph set cannot swap; the swap happens at the next prompt show after `DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS` | Controller (Adapter) |

#### Per-reading states (each of the six reading families)

| State | Entry | Exit | Behaviour | Who |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **HONEST** | `Perceived.motion_mode = HONEST` (always, at MVP) | A non-HONEST record | Needle settles; numerals update on the honest hold grid (HS-14); no gleam | Filter (record), HUD (draw) |
| **GLIDE** (dormant) | `motion_mode = GLIDE` | RETURN | The needle follows `displayed_pm` directly with drift and wander; no settle; gleam of lit-length `leak_amplitude_pm`; the honest latch state is dropped | Filter |
| **TICK** (dormant) | `motion_mode = TICK` | RETURN | Digits substitute at `tick_phase_ms`; gleam; the honest latch is dropped | Filter |
| **STALE** (dormant) | `motion_mode = STALE` | RETURN | Digits and needle held; gleam | Filter |
| **RETURN** (dormant) | `motion_mode = RETURN` | HONEST | The needle returns at the Filter's return rate; the gleam length follows the remaining discrepancy; no event, no pop; on arrival the honest hold grid restarts armed (no immediate change allowed for one grid cell) | Filter |

#### Warning-mark states (per slot)

| State | Entry | Exit | Behaviour | Who |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ABSENT** | The reading's axis is below CRITICAL (or inside the hold) | The Suit's rung reaches CRITICAL after `RUNG_MIN_TICKS`, or `CELL_WARN_S` for the cell | Nothing in the slot | Suit |
| **ON** | Present and `mark_phase < MARK_DUTY_PM / 1000` | The phase leaves the duty | The filled Alarm Red mark with the Paper White stroke; enters ON at once if the phase is inside the duty, else at the next leading edge (worst-case latency about 64 ticks) | Kernel clock |
| **OFF** | Present and the phase is outside the duty | The next leading edge | A Graphite-lit one-pixel chamfered outline in the slot; the slot never vanishes | Kernel clock |
| **SOLID** | Reduced motion or reduce-flashing is on (reduce-flashing is ON at first launch, user ruling D79, so SOLID is the first-launch default) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | The setting is cleared | Held ON; no edges | `HudSettings` |
| **PERSIST** | The axis is FAILED or the seat is DOWNED | Revive or restore | The mark persists; a restore into a DEAD seat shows nothing | Suit |

Hide is the render frame the rung drops. All marks share one phase; marks have no animation of their own beyond the cut between ON and OFF.

#### Prompt and bar states

The prompt and bar substates are the table in HS-41 (`channel_view.state`: IDLE, CONFIRMED, PROGRESS, ABORT, DONE, OUTCOME, plus the immediate reject) layered on the plate states of `focus_view.prompt_state` (HIDDEN, ENABLED, NEEDS_TOOL) and the debounce of HS-37. Plate transitions: HIDDEN to ENABLED / NEEDS_TOOL after `stable_ticks >= PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS`, the minimum cycle and the arbiter's admission; ENABLED / NEEDS_TOOL to HIDDEN immediately. The bar can outlive its plate (PROGRESS with the plate hidden for up to 5 dark ticks) and never outlives the simulation's abort. The `give_up` plate (HS-42(b)) is outside this machine: it has two states, hidden and shown, driven only by `confirm_view.give_up_live` (shown on the first frame it is true with no debounce, re-shows spaced by EC-HS-57, hidden on the first frame it is false), and its bar is the Controller's `hold_pm`. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

### Interactions with Other Systems

**What the HUD registers with the kernel: nothing but a subscription.** It registers no hook (CR-3), no state block (CR-8), no command, no mutator (CR-9) and no RNG stream; it is a `PRESENTATION` subscriber (never `INPUT_SURFACE`, `PROJECTOR` or `REPLICATION`) and writes nothing to the simulation (CR-12: it reads no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block). Its only dirty-key interest is the Suit's `S/<PlayerId>` (and `save_status`), used only to skip discrete rebuilds (HS-7). Each row below says what the HUD reads, what it hands out, who owns the interface, and — as the dependency GDD's own word — what that GDD's Interactions table says about the HUD, quoted from the file at the line read on 2026-10-26. Where the quoted row is stale against this GDD, the row says so and the refresh is listed in Cross-References. Status: **designed** = a GDD file exists and is Designed; **in design** = a file exists and may still move; **in flight (draft on disk)** = a draft file exists with Status In Design (2026-10-27) and may still move; **undesigned-provisional** = no file (the assumption is marked, and repeated in Open Questions with an owner).

**Ownership summary (who owns what).**

| Concern | Owner |
|---|---|
| Whether, when and for whom a reading lies; the displayed trajectory; the tick scheduler; the leak amplitude | Perception Filter |
| The rung rule, hold, thresholds, locomotion cap, death timing, body cues, `RESERVE_S`, `CELL_S`, counts | Suit & Equipment |
| Which verb, when a prompt exists, durations, `length_class`, rejection classes | Interaction (with Controller for the Adapter) |
| Layers, `H`, canvas math, `world_to_hud`, `present_time`, pause rendering, the picture rect | Render Pipeline |
| The layout, glyphs, numeral format, motion drawing, the gleam drawing, the `Theme`, the prompt and bar placement and knobs, the notices, the flash arbiter's HUD-owned events | **This GDD** |
| The pause panel, web map, settings page, boot-flow screens | Chart Table |
| Every sound | Adaptive Audio |

## Formulas

Nothing in this section is hashed, replicated or saved: the HUD owns no simulation state (HS-1), so the kernel's determinism rule (integer-only, `rng_derive`, `state_hash`) does not bind these formulas. They are nevertheless written in **integer arithmetic wherever that is cheap** (`div` is floor division, `ceil_div(a, b) = (a + b - 1) div b`), because ADR candidate G requires the HUD maths to be pure static functions that a headless gdUnit4 run can assert exactly without a renderer. Two formulas use real numbers because they describe motion that is never compared bit for bit (`hud_settle_response`, `cancel_drain_fill`). Formulas owned by this GDD are D.1 to D.16; D.17 to D.20 are neighbours' formulas, cited by their owners' ids with their verbatim outputs and never redefined. "HUD px" is one art pixel at HUD scale `H` (one HUD px = `H` screen px). Every name below is registered at Phase 5 from this text; the HUD-owned constants are `[PH]` and their ranges are in Tuning Knobs.

### D.1 strip_budget (HS-11, HS-15; registered, two bounds)

The strip_budget formula is defined as:

`W_content = 2*B + 2*G + 2*P_in + W_arc + W_num + W_mark ; W_num = 3*A + 2*LS ; H_content = 2*B + H_interior ; slack_w = W_cap - W_content >= 0 ; slack_h = H_cap - H_content >= 0 ; W_cap = (STRIP_MAX_W_PCT * 640) div 100 ; H_cap = (STRIP_MAX_H_PCT * 360) div 100`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `B` | int HUD px | 1 | art-director frame geometry | Plate border width (Graphite lit hairline) |
| `G` | int HUD px | 1-3 | UX spec (`[PH]` 2) | Gap between the arc, the numeral and the mark slot (two gaps) |
| `P_in` | int HUD px | 0-2 | UX spec (`[PH]` 0) | Inner padding of the plate |
| `W_arc` | int HUD px | 20-28 | UX spec (`[PH]` 24) | Width of the dial-arc cell including the pivot cap |
| `A`, `LS` | int HUD px | A 5-6, LS 1 | instrument face (does not yet exist; `[PH]` 5 and 1) | Tabular digit advance and letter-spacing of the 7-cap face |
| `W_num` | int HUD px | 15-20 | derived | Three tabular digit columns: `3*A + 2*LS` = 17 |
| `W_mark` | int HUD px | 16 | art bible 7.2 (the 16 x 16 cell includes its 1-px clearance; live 14 x 14) | The always-reserved mark slot |
| `H_interior` | int HUD px | 16 | art-director (the mark fills it) | Interior height of the strip |
| `STRIP_MAX_W_PCT`, `STRIP_MAX_H_PCT` | int % | 12, 5 | art bible ab:751 (user decision), cited | The cap, defined at the 1080p reference canvas (640 x 360 HUD px) |
| `W_cap`, `H_cap` | int HUD px | 76, 18 | derived | The cap in HUD px; 76 is `floor(76.8)`; 18 is exact |

**Output Range:** `slack_w` and `slack_h` are integers, both required `>= 0` at every `H`, because the strip is authored in HUD px. The strip fails the budget when `W_num > 30` at the default other widths (`76 - (63 - 17)`). The cap is knowingly exceeded in *screen* terms at `H > H_default` and on the Steam Deck (HS-15); the invariant is in HUD px.

**Example:** the default content is `2*1 + 2*2 + 2*0 + 24 + 17 + 16 = 63` HUD px, so `slack_w = 76 - 63 = 13`; with the conservative 18-px mark slot the content is 65 and the slack is 11. Height: `2*1 + 16 = 18`, `slack_h = 0` (zero slack: any frame-border change breaks the cap). Adding a 16 x 16 O2 loop glyph gives `63 + 16 = 79` (or 83 with its gap) `> 76`, which is why HS-11 omits it. A second 16-px mark slot for the ship-alarm mark overflows the same way (`63 + 16 = 79`), which is why HS-22 puts that slot on the suit panel *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Every width is provisional on the instrument face (risk R-9): a face with `A` 6 gives `W_num` 20 and content 66, still inside.

### D.2 hud_flash_budget (HS-14, HS-45, HS-47; registered, with the honest-tick floor)

The hud_flash_budget formula is defined as:

`admit(e, t) = TRUE iff for every window end w in W(t): N(w) + [e counts in w] <= FLASH_MAX_PER_S ; W(t) = {t} U {tick of every clock-locked event already scheduled in (t, t + TICK_RATE)} ; N(w) = number of luminance events, admitted or clock-locked, with tick in (w - TICK_RATE, w]` and `honest_tick_floor_ms(u) = ceil_div(1000 * BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS, FLASH_MAX_PER_S * BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS - u * TICK_RATE)` with `u` = 1; **user rulings D91 (ii), (iv) and D79 add** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: `flash_weight_pm(e) = ceil_div(1000 * 9 * area_px(e), canvas_w_int * canvas_h_int)` ; `exempt(e, t) = (flash_mode == WEIGHTED) AND (class(e) in {DIGIT_SWAP, PROMPT_SHOW_START}) AND (flash_weight_pm(e) + X(t) < FLASH_AREA_EXEMPT_PM)` ; `X(t) = sum of flash_weight_pm over the exempt events with tick in (t - TICK_RATE, t]` ; an exempt event is admitted at once and is added to no `N(w)`; every other event, and every event in STRICT mode, goes through `admit`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `e` | event | prompt show-start (and the give_up plate show-start, HS-42(b), which takes the prompt's WEIGHTED exemption test but is never deferred, HS-47 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*), honest grid boundary (skip-only), notice or scanner show-start, state-display draw (the lamp glyph, tool bracket, glowstick count, TAP relabel: HS-47 class A, coalesced by the shared limiter) | HUD | A deferrable HUD-owned luminance event (a boundary with no displayed digit change is not an event; a grid boundary is admitted at its own tick or skipped, never delayed, and never skipped twice in a row) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | int ticks | 60 `[PH]` (range 30-120) | this GDD (HUD-owned, unregistered until Phase 5) | The longest a deferrable event may wait for room; on expiry it is force-admitted and counted (overrun, `HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN`). 60 is one full window, the longest wait that a window of deferrable-only load can impose; below 30 the guarantee degenerates into overrun *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `GLYPH_STATE_LATCH_TICKS` | int ticks | 20 `[PH]` (range 20-60) | this GDD | The minimum gap between two admitted state-display draws across the whole class (HS-47 class A); formerly the lamp glyph's own latch *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `t`, `w` | int ticks | 0.. | kernel `T` | The candidate tick and each forward window end; windows are half-open `(w - 60, w]` |
| `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` | int | 3 | Render Pipeline PR-13 (locked), cited | Luminance changes per rolling second per HUD |
| `TICK_RATE` | int | 60 | kernel (locked), cited | Ticks per second, the window length |
| `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` | int ticks | 128 (range 120-180) | Emitter State (registry), cited | The mark's period by relation; worst-case arithmetic uses the range floor 120 |
| `u` | int | 1 (the ruled unit; 2, the edge unit, is retired) | user ruling D91 (ii): a flash is a pair of opposing transitions, counted `ceil(n_transitions / 2)` (registry `flash_count`) | How many luminance events one mark cycle counts as: 1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `flash_mode` | enum | STRICT, WEIGHTED | `HudSettings.reduce_flashing`: STRICT while on (on at first launch, user ruling D79), WEIGHTED while off | Which events the arbiter counts *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `area_px(e)` | int HUD px | 35 (one 5 x 7 digit cell) to about 2,160 | the changed rectangle: a grid boundary is the digit cells that change (up to 6 cells, 210 px, when the O2 and reserve numerals both change, EC-HS-21); a prompt show-start is the plate rectangle (`PROMPT_PLATE_MAX_W_PX` 72 by 12 for ENABLED, 18 for NEEDS_TOOL, 30 for a grown two-line plate) | Pixels whose luminance the event changes (every changed pixel is taken as a full-scale change, the worst case) |
| `canvas_w_int`, `canvas_h_int` | int HUD px | `P_w div H`, `P_h div H` (640 x 360 at 1080p, `H` 3) | Render Pipeline, `H` | The HUD canvas; one region is a ninth of it (registry `flash_count`: region = a ninth of the displayed picture) |
| `FLASH_AREA_EXEMPT_PM` | int permille | 100 (range 50-100, never above 100) | the Pipeline's rule that a transition counts at a 10 % area-weighted change (registry `flash_count`) | Below this a flash is sub-threshold *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `HONEST_TICK_MS`, `HONEST_GRID_TICKS` | int ms; int ticks | 500 (pinned; the 400-500 range is withdrawn), `HONEST_GRID_TICKS = HONEST_TICK_MS * TICK_RATE div 1000` = 30 | this GDD | The shared honest-numeral hold grid (HS-14), **anchored to the committed tick** (`T mod HONEST_GRID_TICKS == 0`); also used by the inspect card's numeral. The registry's restatement of the Instruments grid in wall milliseconds (`t_grid(j) = j * HONEST_TICK_MS` on `present_time`) is to be restated in ticks (cross-GDD note to Instruments). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `T_tick` | int ms | 400 / 668 / 1002 / 1336 | Filter `lying_digit_tick_schedule`, cited (D.20) | Lying-digit period; never rescheduled by the HUD |

Non-deferrable events (hides, mark leading edges, Filter lying-digit ticks, source-state hue cuts and the breath-source swap, orphan transitions, and the class-B acknowledgement cues of HS-47 (ACK plus force-admitted show-start, reject hold, OUTCOME step); the class-A state displays are deferrable) always happen *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the honest grid boundary is clock-locked and skip-only (its tick is fixed, it may be skipped, it is never delayed). Mark leading edges, lying-digit ticks and source-state hue cuts (HSV-4, L-5: a glyph or arc band changing between its loop hue and Graphite lit; counted, never deferred, because they follow the honest source state) always count. Under the ruled unit (`u` = 1) a hide is not a counted event (the edge unit, which counted it as the trailing edge of its show-start's cycle, is retired). **Priority among HUD-owned deferrable events:** prompt show-start, then honest grid boundary (skip-only), then notice or scanner show-start, then the state-display class. The grid boundary is clock-locked, so it is *scheduled first* into every window that `admit` tests (it is in `W(t)` before any prompt asks); "the prompt outranks the grid" means only that a boundary is skipped to make room for a waiting prompt, which is the order HS-47 states (this reconciles the earlier example line that read as allocating the grid first). **Grid-skip rule:** a boundary that would exceed the budget while a prompt waits is skipped (never delayed) **but never twice in a row**; the next boundary shows the then-latest value; numeral lag is bounded by `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS` unconditionally. A prompt waits at most one grid cell **only with at most one other counted event in the window**; in general it waits at most `MAX_DEFER_TICKS` and is then force-admitted and counted (the bounds of HS-47 are stated with these preconditions) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**Counting unit and its bound (user ruling D91 (ii))** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: let a window hold `m` **complete pairs** (two opposing transitions inside the window, counted once at the leading edge: a mark cycle's ON and OFF edges, or a plate's show-start and its hide), `k` **single-transition events** (a grid boundary, a substitution, a show-start whose hide lies after the window, a mark ON edge whose OFF edge lies after it) and `o` **orphan transitions** (a hide, or a mark OFF edge, whose leading edge lies before the window; *new, re-review*). Then `n = 2m + k + o` transitions, the ruled flash count is `ceil(n / 2) = m + ceil((k + o) / 2)`, and the arbiter's count is `E = m + k + o`, so `ceil(n / 2) <= E` for every `k, o >= 0`: keeping `E <= FLASH_MAX_PER_S` keeps the ruled count at or below it. *(The earlier statement counted a hide as nothing and so assumed `o` = 0: a window with orphan hides at its edges, hide, show, hide, show, hide, show, hide, holds 7 transitions, ruled count `ceil(7 / 2)` = 4, while `E` counted only the three show-starts, so the arbiter passed a window the ruling forbids. The orphan hide is now counted from the tick it occurs, as a non-deferrable event, so the next show-start is refused while it stands; the first orphan of a run can still overrun by one, recorded as `HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN`, because a hide is immediate and unpredictable, AC-HS-142.)* Worked example: two numeral boundaries and one mark cycle in a window give `n = 4` (or 3 with only the ON edge inside it), the ruled count is 2, and `E` is 3, so the margin is one flash; the seven-transition window above gives `n = 7`, ruled 4, `E` = 4 once the orphan hide is counted, which is over the ceiling and refused. The literal `ceil(n / 2)` would admit up to six single transitions per second; the HUD does not use that headroom (it would unpin the grid proof and the Filter counts its own ticks).

**Weighted exemption (user ruling D91 (iv); creative-director exemptions)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: a flash is weighted by the area it covers (WCAG 2.3.1), measured as the registry's region, a ninth of the displayed picture, with every changed pixel taken as a full-scale change so that the weight can only be over-stated. An event is sub-threshold when `flash_weight_pm < FLASH_AREA_EXEMPT_PM` (100, the Pipeline's 10 % area-weighted transition). Exactly two classes can be exempt, and only in WEIGHTED mode: honest-numeral digit swaps and prompt show-starts. The Alarm Red mark is never exempt (its edges are counted strictly in both modes, red flash included), and in STRICT mode (reduce flashing) nothing is exempt. A **cumulative guard** keeps the exemption from becoming a loophole: WCAG sums the area of concurrent flashes, so an event is exempt only while the rolling-second sum `X(t)` of exempt weights plus its own stays below the threshold; otherwise it is counted and goes through `admit` (it is returned to the strict arithmetic, never dropped). **Worked weights** (every figure is `ceil_div(9000 * area_px, canvas_w_int * canvas_h_int)`): the six-cell numeral change (210 px) weighs 9 at 1080p `H` 3 (canvas 640 x 360 = 230,400), 19 on the Steam Deck and at 720p `H` 3 (canvas 426 x 240 = 102,240) and 33 at 1080p `H` 6 (320 x 180 = 57,600), all far below 100. **The Deck's picture mode is pinned to `keep`** (the Pipeline's default until its spike steps pass: a 1280 x 720 picture with 40-px bars in the 1280 x 800 window, HUD canvas 426.67 x 240, integer canvas 426 x 240), so every Deck figure in this GDD equals the 720p `H` 3 figure; the earlier mixed use of a 426 x 266 canvas (`expand`, post-spike) for some weights and 426.67 x 240 for others is retired, and `expand` (canvas height 266.67) can only add vertical room *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The ENABLED plate (72 x 12 = 864 px) weighs 34, 60, 94 and 135 at 1080p `H` 3, 4, 5 and 6 and 77 on the Deck and at 720p `H` 3 (canvas 426 x 240). The NEEDS_TOOL plate (72 x 18 = 1,296 px) weighs 51 and 90 at 1080p `H` 3 and 4, 141 at `H` 5, and 115 on the Deck and at 720p. A grown two-line plate (72 x 30 = 2,160 px) weighs 85 at 1080p `H` 3 and 150 at `H` 4. So a prompt show-start is exempt for the ENABLED plate through `H` 5 on 1080p and on the Deck and at 720p, for the NEEDS_TOOL plate at `H` 3 and 4 on 1080p only (on the Deck and at 720p it is counted, as a first-priority deferrable event), and for a grown two-line plate at 1080p `H` 3 only. **Flag for the UX spec:** the 24 / 18 reading-face tier grows the plates (C-I), so the exemption must be re-checked against the drawn plates, and the table above is the test.

**Output Range:** `admit` is a boolean. For HUD-owned deferrable events `N(w) <= FLASH_MAX_PER_S` always holds; it can be exceeded only by non-deferrable events (Filter ticks at Vertical Slice A, a source-state hue cut or breath-source swap, an orphan hide, a class-B acknowledgement cue landing in an already-full window) and by a deferrable event force-admitted at `MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, which the HUD counts as `HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN` and does not alter; the class-A state displays are coalesced by the shared limiter and so cannot be raised above the ceiling by input alone *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. `honest_tick_floor_ms` is an integer in 375 to 400 (`u` = 1) over the whole `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` range: it is the **average-rate** floor, necessary but not sufficient, because `admit` tests a rolling 60-tick window. **Window feasibility (the validator's check, not a static range):** with the grid every `g` ticks a 60-tick window holds `ceil(60 / g)` boundaries at most (3 when `g` < 30, exactly 2 when `g` = 30); plus the mark's counted edges in the window (1 under the ruled `u` = 1) the total must not exceed `FLASH_MAX_PER_S`. At `g` = 30 and `u` = 1: 2 + 1 = 3 (feasible); at `g` = 24: 3 + 1 = 4 (infeasible). So `HONEST_TICK_MS` is pinned at 500 and the data validator **recomputes the check at the live `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` and `u`** and fails the data file when it is infeasible. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Since the mode rulings (D79, D91 (iv)), counted numerals and a pulsing mark coexist only rarely** (STRICT solidifies the mark; WEIGHTED exempts the digit swaps and returns one to `admit` only when the cumulative guard trips), so this check stays live in the validator for that case and for any future split of the mark's pulse from `reduce_flashing`; the pin rests on the user's ratification, on this proof and on the cadence shared with the inspect card *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

**Example:** (the floor) at `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` 128: `u` = 1 gives `ceil_div(128000, 3*128 - 60) = ceil_div(128000, 324) = 396` ms. At the range floor 120: `ceil_div(120000, 300) = 400` ms. At 180: 375 ms. (The retired edge unit `u` = 2 gave 485, 500 and 429 ms.) The Filter's PF-18 floor of 334 ms is `1000/3` for lying ticks alone and ignores the mark, which is why it is not the HUD's range floor.

**Example (rates, a breach in progress, numerals falling every boundary)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: at `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` 128 and `HONEST_TICK_MS` 500, with `u` = 1. **WEIGHTED mode** (reduce flashing off, the mark pulsing): the mark counts `60/128 = 0.469`/s, the numerals (2.0/s) and the prompt show-starts are exempt, so the counted total is 0.469/s of 3 and a prompt waits for its 13-tick debounce alone. **STRICT mode** (reduce flashing on, the first-launch default): the mark is solid, so it adds 0; the numerals count 2.0/s, leaving 1.0/s for prompt show-starts (about one per second, bounded below by `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS`). The case that used to be the worst, a pulsing mark beside counted numerals (0.469 + 2.0 = 2.469/s, headroom 0.531/s; at the range floor 120, 0.5 + 2.0 = 2.5/s, headroom 0.5), now arises only when the cumulative guard trips, and the grid-skip rule and the window proof cover it.

**Example (admission, no numerals; only show-starts are injected, with no hide events, so no pair completes in a window and no orphan hide arises: the hide cases are AC-HS-142)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: with plates hiding between show-starts a hide whose show-start has left the window is counted, D.2, and the admitted list below would shrink)*: show-start requests every 10 ticks from tick 0, filtered first by D.18 (`PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` 20), with a mark edge scheduled at tick 128, are admitted at 0, 20, 40, 60, 80, 100 and 140 (the requests are filtered first by D.18, `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` 20; `admit()` alone would also pass tick 10, whose window `(-50, 10]` holds only tick 0): the request at 120 is refused because the window ending at 128 would hold 80, 100, 120 and the edge (4); at 130 the window `(70, 130]` still holds 80, 100, 128 and the candidate; at 140 the window `(80, 140]` holds 100, 128 and the candidate (3). Long-run admitted rate `<= 3 - 0.469 = 2.53`/s.

**Example (grid-skip, STRICT mode)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the window is full (numerals 2.0/s plus a source-state hue cut, HSV-4, counted and never deferred, 1.0); a prompt show-start waits; the next grid boundary is skipped, the prompt is admitted in its slot, and the numeral shows the then-latest value one boundary later, so its lag is `<= 2 x 500` ms = 1000 ms. **Example (the bounds with their preconditions, STRICT mode)** *(re-review)*: the same window plus a second waiting prompt (a hue cut and two prompts): the first prompt is admitted at the skipped boundary's slot; the second would skip the next boundary too, but a boundary is never skipped twice in a row, so that boundary is drawn (numeral lag stays `<= 1000` ms, not the 1500 ms or more that unbounded skipping gave) and the second prompt waits for room, `<= MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 60 ticks, then is force-admitted and counted if the window is still full. The lamp glyph and scanner reading wait the same bound, so neither starves (AC-HS-141).

**Example (Vertical Slice A overrun, stated so it is not a surprise):** with the reserve numeral lying at `T_tick` 400 ms (2.5 transitions/s), the O2 numeral honest (2.0/s) and one mark (0.469/s), STRICT mode counts 2.5 + 2.0 = 4.5/s `> 3` (the mark is solid there) and WEIGHTED mode counts the lying ticks (kept counted until the Filter adopts the same weighting) plus the mark, 2.5 + 0.469 = 2.969/s `<= 3` (margin 0.031/s), the honest numeral being exempt. The STRICT overrun is structural: the HUD cannot meet the ceiling by deferring its own events without starving the honest numeral, so it records `HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN` and raises the ask to the Filter; it never alters the Filter's schedule (CF-HS-10) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

### D.3 reserve_seconds (HS-17; registered, HUD-owned)

The reserve_seconds formula is defined as:

`reserve_seconds = (displayed_pm * RESERVE_S + 999) div 1000`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `displayed_pm` | int permille | 0-1000 | `perceived(suit.reserve).displayed_pm` (the Filter's wrap), never `view.reserve_pm` | The reserve fraction as the player is shown it |
| `RESERVE_S` | int seconds | 90 (range 45-240) | Suit & Equipment (registry), cited | Seconds of breath at nominal draw |
| `reserve_seconds` | int seconds | 0..`RESERVE_S` | output | The numeral: three digit cells because `RESERVE_S` ranges to 240 |

**Output Range:** 0 to `RESERVE_S`; the numeral is `0` only at exactly empty (the ceiling guarantees that a non-zero reserve never reads 0). It is the *latest* value at each `HONEST_TICK_MS` boundary. Nominal draw only: it is intentionally optimistic under a seam leak.

**Example:** `RESERVE_S` 90: `displayed_pm` 1000 gives 90; 500 gives `(45000 + 999) div 1000 = 45`; 333 gives `(29970 + 999) div 1000 = 30` (equal to `RESERVE_WARN_S`); 12 gives `(1080 + 999) div 1000 = 2`; 11 gives 1; 1 gives 1; 0 gives 0. At nominal draw 1000/90 = 11.1 permille/s the displayed second changes about once per second, below the 3/s ceiling. With `RESERVE_S` 240 and 1000 permille the numeral is `240` (three cells). **Engraved warn notch** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `warn_notch_px = (RESERVE_WARN_S * 16 + RESERVE_S - 1) div RESERVE_S` on the 16-px underlay track, 6 at `RESERVE_S` 90 and `RESERVE_WARN_S` 30 (the numeral reads 30 when the fill stands at the notch), 2 at `RESERVE_S` 240 and `RESERVE_WARN_S` 20; the fill is `(displayed_pm * 16 + 999) div 1000` px, so the fill is 6 px at the notch when `displayed_pm` is 333. The notch is frame (engraving), the fill is the reading.

### D.4 mark_phase (HS-23; registered, HUD-owned)

The mark_phase formula is defined as:

`phase_pm = ((T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS) * 1000 + alpha_pm) div BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS ; ON iff phase_pm < MARK_DUTY_PM ; leading edge at T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0 ; override: an active mark is ON whatever phase_pm is while PAUSED_FROZEN, and under reduced motion or reduce-flashing`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T` | int ticks | 0.. | kernel committed tick | Frozen under solo pause; the phase is not consulted while paused (HS-23 holds an active mark ON) |
| `alpha_pm` | int permille | 0-999 | `floor(tick_alpha * 1000)`, Pipeline | Sub-tick interpolation so the edge is drawn on the first render frame at or past the boundary |
| `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` | int ticks | 128 (range 120-180) | Emitter State (registry), cited | The period, by relation: `MARK_PERIOD_TICKS` is never a second value |
| `MARK_DUTY_PM` | int permille | 500 `[PH]`, range 400-600 | this GDD | Fraction of the period the mark is ON |
| `phase_pm` | int permille | 0-999 | output | Position in the period |

**Output Range:** `phase_pm` in 0 to 999, monotone within a period and reset at the boundary; identical for all marks (no per-mark offset). One leading edge per period: `TICK_RATE / BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` = 0.469/s at 128 (0.5 at 120, 0.333 at 180); both edges 0.94/s at 128. Worst-case latency for a mark that appears just past its duty is `(1000 - MARK_DUTY_PM)/1000 * BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` = 64 ticks (1.07 s) at defaults, 76.8 ticks at duty 400. Reduced motion, reduce-flashing and `PAUSED_FROZEN`: ON while any mark is active, whatever `phase_pm` is (a pause cycle adds at most one leading edge, and a resume none: after a resume the mark is bridged ON until the next leading edge, HS-23). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Example:** `T mod 128` = 63, `alpha_pm` 500: `(63000 + 500) div 128 = 496` so ON; `T mod 128` = 64, `alpha_pm` 0: `64000 div 128 = 500` so OFF; `T` = 300: `300 mod 128 = 44`, `44000 div 128 = 343` ON; `T` = 360: 104, 812 OFF; `T` = 384: 0, the leading edge, ON. The ON phase lasts 64 ticks (1.067 s) at defaults.

### D.5 o2_dial_angle (HS-12, HS-13; registered, HUD-owned)

The o2_dial_angle formula is defined as:

`pm = clamp((displayed_value * 1000) div O2_DIAL_MAX_DKPA, 0, 1000) ; theta_cdeg = (STRIP_SWEEP_DEG * 100 * pm) div 1000 ; start_deg = 90 + STRIP_SWEEP_DEG div 2 (the left stop, measured counter-clockwise from +x; 180 at the default sweep) ; phi = start_deg - theta ; tip = (cx + round(L * cos(phi)), cy - round(L * sin(phi))) ; edge_theta(e) = (STRIP_SWEEP_DEG * e) div O2_DIAL_MAX_DKPA ; bbox_w = 2 * L, bbox_h = L * (1 + sin((STRIP_SWEEP_DEG - 180) / 2)) for a sweep of 180 or more` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the start angle and the bounding box are explicit, so a sweep other than 180 is computed rather than assumed; at 180 the tip is `(cx - round(L * cos(theta)), cy - round(L * sin(theta)))` as before)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `displayed_value` | int dkPa | 0-999 (printed); dial clamps at the stop | `perceived(suit.o2).displayed_value` | The suit-sensed `p_o2` of the occupant's compartment (SE-30); equals `view.o2_pp_dkpa` on the honest path |
| `O2_DIAL_MAX_DKPA` | int dkPa | 300 `[PH]`, range 250-300 | this GDD; a calibration it supplies to the Filter (HS-26; no Filter consumer is named yet) | Dial travel; changing it rescales lie magnitude |
| `STRIP_SWEEP_DEG` | int degrees | 180 `[PH]`, range 120-240 | this GDD (named distinctly from Instruments' `DIAL_ARC_DEG`) | Sweep of the strip arc, measured from the left stop |
| `L` | int HUD px | at most 11 | art-director | Strip needle length (below the glide floor by construction, HS-29) |
| `cx`, `cy` | int HUD px | arc-cell coordinates | UX spec | Pivot position (bottom interior row) |
| `e` | int dkPa | the zone-edge value | read by name from the Graph (`HYPOXIA_PP`, 120-160 default 140; by name `O2_BAND_ANOXIC_PP` 100, `O2_BAND_THIN_PP` 180) | Never a literal in HUD data or source (lint) |

**Source of the needle fraction:** the strip needle's `pm` is this formula's derivation from `displayed_value`; `Perceived.displayed_pm` for `suit.o2`, where the Filter publishes one, is asserted equal to it on the honest path (one source, so D.15's `displayed_pm` is this `pm`). **Output Range:** `theta` in 0 to `STRIP_SWEEP_DEG` degrees; the needle clamps at the stop and the numeral prints `min(displayed_value, 999)`. `1 permille = 0.3 dkPa < 0.5`, so `round(pm * 300 / 1000)` recovers every integer dkPa losslessly at range 300 (it would not above a 500-dkPa range).

**Example:** a nominal room reads 210 dkPa: `pm = 210000 div 300 = 700`, `theta = 180 * 700/1000 = 126` degrees; with `L` 11 and the pivot at (12, 15) the tip is `(12 - round(11 * cos 126), 15 - round(11 * sin 126)) = (12 + 6, 15 - 9) = (18, 6)`. For 175 dkPa (the THIN fixture): `pm` 583, `theta` 105 degrees, tip `(12 + round(2.85), 15 - round(10.63)) = (15, 4)`. The Graph's registered edges (the HUD reads them by name), `O2_BAND_ANOXIC_PP` 100, `HYPOXIA_PP` 140 and `O2_BAND_THIN_PP` 180 dkPa, sit at `edge_theta` 60, 84 and 108 degrees; `HYPOXIA_PP` across its range 120-160 moves the middle one from 72 to 96 degrees. **Reserve branch** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; Resolved 2026-10-27 — re-review, G2, HS-12)*: while `breath_src` is RESERVE the strip's `pm` is `clamp(displayed_pm(suit.reserve), 0, 1000)` directly (permille of `RESERVE_S`, so no `O2_DIAL_MAX_DKPA` division and no dkPa literal), `theta_cdeg`, `phi`, `tip` and the frame index of D.15 follow unchanged, and the engraved reserve layer sits at `tank_theta = STRIP_SWEEP_DEG` (the full stop) and `warn_theta = (STRIP_SWEEP_DEG * warn_pm) div 1000`, with `warn_pm = (RESERVE_WARN_S * 1000 + RESERVE_S - 1) div RESERVE_S`. Example at `RESERVE_S` 90, `RESERVE_WARN_S` 30: a full reserve is `pm` 1000, `theta` 180 degrees, tip at the right stop, numeral `90`; 45 s is `pm` 500, 90 degrees, numeral `45`; the warn notch is `warn_pm` 334 at `warn_theta` 60 degrees (the numeral reads 30 at `displayed_pm` 333, which is 59.9 degrees, 0.2 degrees short of the notch, well inside one needle frame, so the needle stands on the notch when the numeral first reads 30, as D.3's panel notch does); at `RESERVE_S` 240 and `RESERVE_WARN_S` 20 `warn_pm` is 84 and `warn_theta` 15 degrees. The ambient branch's printed example (210 dkPa, 126 degrees) is the ROOM case and is unchanged. `breath_src` NONE or GASP uses the ambient branch (the strip then reads the room's `p_o2`, EC-HS-14).

### D.6 hud_settle_response (HS-14; HUD-owned, presentation-only)

The hud_settle_response formula (registry: `hud_settle_response`) is defined as below. It is the HUD's own suit-arc settle, named apart from In-World Instruments' `settle_response` (registry, source Instruments: `INSTR_SETTLE_ZETA` 0.7, omega 20, `INSTR_SETTLE_MS` 400, trigger `INSTR_SETTLE_JUMP_PM`), which it does not cite: the HUD's damping and settling time differ and only the shared second-order form and the 5 % overshoot / 250-400 ms bounds of art bible 7.3 are common. *(Renamed 2026-10-27, consistency pass, so the name has one owner.)* **Redefined 2026-10-27 (design review): the needle is a state, not a curve.** The earlier form was only the response to a single step from rest; it said nothing about a target that changes again mid-settle, which is the normal case in the anchor breach (the Graph steps `p_o2` every 6 ticks, about every 100 ms, against a 300 ms settle), and at its own defaults it was red against its own acceptance criterion (`|x - 1|` was 0.0248 at 300 ms). The response is now a critically damped, monotone, overshoot-free system advanced by an **exact per-frame propagator**; this section defines the pure function, and AC-HS-20 tests that function, not the drawn (quantised) needle. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

`omega = K_SETTLE * 1000 / HUD_SETTLE_MS (rad/s) ; K_SETTLE = 5.834 ; state (p, v) in permille of dial travel and permille/s, target g ; e = p - g ; advance(dt): B = v + omega * e ; E = exp(-omega * dt) ; e' = (e + B * dt) * E ; v' = (v - omega * B * dt) * E ; p' = g + e' ; retarget(g_new): g := g_new, p and v unchanged ; rest: if |e'| < REST_PM and |v'| < REST_V then p := g, v := 0 ; sub-frame: if at rest and |g_new - p| < FRAME_PM then g := g_new and p, v are unchanged (no settle starts; the deltas accumulate in e = p - g and the settle starts the first time |e| >= FRAME_PM) ; reduced motion: p := g, v := 0 on every retarget` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

`K_SETTLE` is the root of `(1 + x) * exp(-x) = 0.02` (5.8339), the 2 % settle of a critically damped step, so the step response from rest `e(t) = e0 * (1 + omega * t) * exp(-omega * t)` is **exactly 2 % at `t = HUD_SETTLE_MS`** (0.019999 at the default) and is monotone with no overshoot. `REST_PM` (1) and `REST_V` (10 permille/s) are numeric hygiene so the exponential tail ends; they are not knobs. `FRAME_PM` is one needle frame step, `1000 / (N_frames - 1)` (D.15). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Properties the function guarantees (each is an acceptance criterion).** (P1) *Semigroup:* `advance(a)` then `advance(b)` equals `advance(a + b)` to float precision, so three 16.7 ms steps and one 50 ms step agree and the clamp `HUD_DT_CLAMP_MS` only bounds a hitch. (P2) *Continuity:* a retarget changes neither `p` nor `v`; the needle position is continuous at every retarget. (P3) *No backward motion:* between retargets `B' = -omega * B`, and a retarget by `delta` adds `omega * delta` to `B`, so for any sequence of retargets in one direction `B` keeps the sign of `e`, the needle never crosses its target and never moves against the direction of travel; only a retarget that reverses direction can cause one turn-around. (P4) *Bounded lag under a ramp:* a target moving at constant rate `r` (permille/s) is followed at a steady lag of `2 * r / omega`, plus at most one retarget step for a staircase. (P5) *Below one needle frame a change does not start a settle* (the sub-frame rule), **but sub-frame changes accumulate** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier form set `p := g_new` on every sub-frame retarget, so on the reference ramp of -12 dkPa/s, 4 permille per Graph step against a `FRAME_PM` of 29.4, the needle stepped with the target and the settle never engaged, which contradicted the steady-lag example below)*: the target moves, `p` waits, and when 8 such steps (32 permille) are owed the settle starts from `p` and follows the ramp at the steady lag of P4. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Fallback if the art-director insists on a visible overshoot:** use the underdamped form with `zeta >= 0.70` and `omega_n = ln(50 / sqrt(1 - zeta^2)) / (zeta * Ts)`, re-derive the peak (`pi / omega_d`, 0.217 s at `zeta` 0.70 and 300 ms; overshoot 4.6 %), and restore a `HUD_SETTLE_ZETA` knob, the peak assertions and registry entry. It is not adopted: art bible 7.3's 5 % is a ceiling, and the overshoot is 0.2-0.4 px, which never renders. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dt` | real seconds | 0..`HUD_DT_CLAMP_MS` | `present_time` step, clamped to `HUD_DT_CLAMP_MS`; no integrator divides by it | Frozen under pause (`dt` = 0); never process time. The propagator is exact for any `dt`, so the frame rate never changes the result |
| `HUD_SETTLE_MS` | int ms | 300 `[PH]`, range 250-400 | this GDD; art bible 7.3 bounds it | The 2 % settling time of a step from rest, exactly (named to avoid Instruments' own `INSTR_SETTLE_MS`, CF-HS-20) |
| `K_SETTLE` | real | 5.834 (derived constant, not a knob) | root of `(1 + x) * exp(-x) = 0.02` | Converts the settling time to `omega`; `HUD_SETTLE_ZETA` is retired |
| `p`, `v`, `g`, `e` | real | `p`, `g` in 0-1000 permille of dial travel; `v` in permille/s | the needle state, the target (`displayed_pm`, or D.5's `pm`), `e = p - g` | The state is held per needle; a retarget replaces `g` only |
| `FRAME_PM` | real permille | 1000 / (`N_frames` - 1): 29.4 at `L` 11, 20 at `L` 16, 13.3 at `L` 24 (180 degrees) | D.15 | One needle frame step; the sub-frame rule's threshold |
| `REST_PM`, `REST_V` | real | 1 permille; 10 permille/s | this GDD (numeric hygiene) | Ends the exponential tail |

**Output Range:** `p` in 0-1000, clamped to the dial stops; for a one-direction run of retargets `p` is monotone and never passes `g`; the drawn needle is then quantised to whole HUD px and frame (D.15). Reduced motion: `p = g` on the retarget (a step). A non-HONEST record never settles (HS-28). Paused: `dt` = 0, no change. Restore: `p = g`, `v = 0` (RESTORE_FRAME). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Example:** `HUD_SETTLE_MS` 300: `omega = 5.834 / 0.3 = 19.45` rad/s. A step from rest of 200 permille (36 degrees: O2 falling 210 to 150 dkPa at 300 dkPa travel and 180 degrees): `e` after 0.1 s is `200 * (1 + 1.945) * exp(-1.945) = 84.2` permille (42.1 %), after 0.2 s 20.0 (10.0 %), after 0.3 s 4.0 (2.0 %), and at frame 19 (316.7 ms) 3.0 (1.5 %): **the 2 % criterion holds at the settle time and at every later frame**. Ramp: `p_o2` falling 12 dkPa/s is `r` = 40 permille/s, the steady lag is `2 * 40 / 19.45 = 4.1` permille (about 1.2 dkPa, 0.74 degrees, 0.14 px at the strip's 11-px needle); the needle never moves backward. Mid-settle retarget: at `t` = 0.1 s into the step above (`e` 84.2 permille, `v` about -1,080 permille/s) the target drops another 4 permille (one Graph step): `p` and `v` are unchanged at that instant, `e` becomes 88.2 and the response continues from there, so there is no restart, no kink in position and no second 300 ms. Two 16.7 ms steps then one 16.7 ms step equal one 50 ms step to float precision. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.7 nonground_count (HS-56; registered, HUD-owned)

The nonground_count formula is defined as:

`nonground_count = #{ (x, y) in [0, 640) x [0, 360) : colour(x * H + H div 2, y * H + H div 2) is neither GROUND (#1C2128) nor SENTINEL } ; require nonground_count <= HUD_NONGROUND_PX_MAX = (HUD_NONGROUND_PCT_X10 * 640 * 360) div 1000`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `colour(px)` | sRGB | the captured layer | screenshot with the world container hidden and the root clear colour set to the SENTINEL (for example `#FF00FF`) | Transparent HUD pixels show the clear colour, not alpha, so the sentinel is how "transparent" is counted |
| `H` | int | `H_default` (capture at 3 on 1080p so the canvas is exactly 640 x 360) | Pipeline | Block-centre sampling at integer `H` |
| `HUD_NONGROUND_PCT_X10` | int | 10 (range 10-15), i.e. 1.0 %-1.5 % | this GDD `[PH]` | The art bible's "about 1 %, tunable" (ab:754) as a measurable cap |
| `HUD_NONGROUND_PX_MAX` | int HUD px | 2304 (range 2304-3456) | derived | The budget |

**Output Range:** 0 to 230,400; measured **at rest** only (all readings OK, no prompt, mark, notice, first-frame line or inspect card; the card is outside the measurement, iw:327). A scan is a tool-time cost (230,400 samples), dev only.

**Example:** the art-director's allocation is strip 320, panel 900, rail 900 and 184 headroom (total 2,304) against an estimate of about 2,060 (strip about 300, panel about 875, rail about 885); the ux-designer's independent estimate is about 1,875. Both are estimates the UX spec re-measures from a retained screenshot. The 1-px letterbox perimeter hairline would cost 1,996 px (87 %) and is declined for the HUD layer (HS-56).

### D.8 bottom_row_fit (HS-54, HS-55; registered, per-side plus vertical) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

*(Restated 2026-10-27, design review.)* The first form was one sum over the pair, `W_panel + W_strip + W_rail + 2 * GAP + 2 * margin <= canvas_w`. The strip is **centred**, so the true constraint is per side: a wide panel and a narrow rail can satisfy the sum while the panel still overlaps the strip. The sum is kept only as a quick reject (necessary, not sufficient). The 204 HUD px "floor invariant" for the pair it produced is withdrawn; the per-side figure replaces it (102 HUD px each at 1280 / `H` 4). A vertical fit is added, and the offered `H` set is one closed function with one table.

The bottom_row_fit formula is defined as:

`canvas_w_int = P_w div H ; canvas_h_int = P_h div H ; x_strip = (canvas_w_int - W_strip) div 2 ; side_avail(P_w, H) = x_strip - margin_hud_x(P_w, H) - GAP_MIN_PX ; fit_h(P_w, H) := W_panel <= side_avail AND W_rail <= side_avail ; fit_v(P_h, H) := margin_hud_y(P_h, H) + H_row(arr) <= (canvas_h_int * frac(arr)) div 100 ; fit_notice(P_h, H) := margin_hud_y(P_h, H) + H_panel(arr) + NOTICE_STACK_H_PX <= (canvas_h_int * NOTICE_MAX_FRAC_PCT) div 100 ; fit(P_w, P_h, H) := fit_h AND fit_v AND fit_notice ; offered(P_w, P_h, arr) = { H integer : H_default <= H <= 2 * H_default AND fit(P_w, P_h, H) evaluated at W_panel = W_panel(arr), W_rail = RAIL_MAX_W_PX } ; arr in {ROW, COLUMN} with (W_panel, H_panel, H_row, frac) = (144, 40, 44, ROW_MAX_FRAC_PCT) for ROW and (74, 80, 80, COL_MAX_FRAC_PCT) for COLUMN *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: a budget per arrangement, the notice stack in the vertical fit)* ; H_default = max(k_auto, 3) ; pre-check (necessary only): W_panel + W_strip + W_rail + 2 * GAP_MIN_PX + 2 * margin_hud_x <= canvas_w_int`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `P_w`, `P_h` | int screen px | 1280 x 720 (`MIN_WINDOW`) up | Pipeline picture rect | Picture size (under `keep` and `expand` a 1280 x 800 window gives a 1280 x 720 or a 1280 x 800 picture; both are in the table) |
| `H`, `H_default` | int | `H_default`..`2*H_default`; `H_default = max(k_auto, 3)` | Pipeline PR-12 | HUD scale; the picture's `k_auto` (not the zoom step `k`) sets the default |
| `W_strip` | int HUD px | **76**, frozen | this GDD (the plate cap, HS-11) | The strip's counted width is the plate, once and everywhere; the 63-65 px content width is not used by any formula, example or criterion |
| `PANEL_MAX_W_PX` (= `W_panel(arr)`), `RAIL_MAX_W_PX` | int HUD px | panel **144 (ROW) or 74 (COLUMN)** `[PH]`, the width tallies of the cell table below; rail 100 `[PH]`, range 80-130 | this GDD (the drawn-content tally; the art-director's 100 x 44 box is withdrawn, HS-16) | The width **budgets** the UX spec must stay inside; they are what `offered()` is computed from. A wider panel shrinks the offered set (see the tables) and is an accessibility cost needing the accessibility-specialist's sign-off *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `H_panel(arr)`, `ROW_H_MAX_PX` (= `H_row(arr)`) | int HUD px | panel **40 (ROW) or 80 (COLUMN)**; `ROW_H_MAX_PX` 44 for ROW (the rail is the tallest surface) and 80 for COLUMN | this GDD | Height budget of the tallest bottom surface (`ROW_H_MAX_PX` stays 36-56 for the row arrangement) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `ROW_MAX_FRAC_PCT`, `COL_MAX_FRAC_PCT` | int % | 30 `[PH]`, range 25-35 (ROW); 50 `[PH]`, range 39-50 (COLUMN) | this GDD | The bottom row (its height plus the bottom margin) may take at most this share of the HUD canvas height. For ROW this keeps the upper 70 % of the picture HUD-free (P1: the world, not the HUD, is the picture). For COLUMN the tower is narrow and at the left edge but rises to 49.4 % of the canvas at its worst offered cell, so the 70 % rule is **restated for COLUMN as "the upper 50 % of the picture is HUD-free", a P1 cost routed to the art-director** (OQ-HS-81); `ROW_MAX_FRAC_PCT` is not moved to make COLUMN fit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `NOTICE_STACK_H_PX`, `NOTICE_MAX_FRAC_PCT` | int HUD px; int % | 38; 75 `[PH]` | this GDD | The notice stack (the failed-write notice plate, 18 tall, and the restore line plate, up to two lines, 20 tall) rises from the panel's edge. It is a **declared, bounded, transient exception** to the at-rest rule (it exists only while a notice shows: the restore line for `UI7_DWELL_S` after a restore, the failed-write notice only after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` failures; HS-56 measures at rest, with no notice), and `fit_notice` bounds it at `NOTICE_MAX_FRAC_PCT` of the canvas height, an invented placeholder routed to the art-director (OQ-HS-82). Its width is the panel's or `PROMPT_PLATE_MAX_W_PX` 72, whichever is larger; the English strings are placeholders to be authored to two lines at that width *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `GAP_MIN_PX` | int HUD px | 4 `[PH]`, range 2-8 | this GDD | Minimum gap between a side surface and the strip |
| `margin_hud_x`, `margin_hud_y` | int HUD px | see D.10 | derived | Safe margin on the HUD canvas, per axis |
| `W_panel`, `W_rail` | int HUD px | at most the budgets of the selected arrangement | `/ux-design` | The drawn widths; `fit_h` is evaluated at the budgets until the UX spec supplies drawn ones, then at the drawn ones |
