| `arr` | enum | ROW, COLUMN | `/ux-design` chooses by the resulting `offered()` set (OQ-HS-81) | The panel arrangement; **no default is set here**, so the validator and AC-HS-79 / 80 / 82 / 115 run both until the UX spec picks one |

**Output Range:** `side_avail` is an integer, possibly negative; `offered()` is a set of integer `H`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `offered(P_w, P_h, arr)` takes the panel arrangement; **wherever this GDD writes `offered(P_w, P_h)` (HS-2, HS-54, HS-55, EC-HS-3, the `HudSettings` rows) it means the set of the chosen arrangement, and until `/ux-design` chooses, the validator and the criteria evaluate both**. The layout function returns a defect flag when `fit` is false for the requested `(picture, H)`. **Fallback:** the layout at `H_default` (which is in `offered()` on every supported picture, below); "keep the previous valid layout" is withdrawn because after a window resize, or at first mount, there is no previous layout. The MVP offers exactly the `offered()` set and nothing else (Pipeline PR-12 option (a), adopted here as a **stopgap only**).

**Example (the closed table; `W_strip` 76, `GAP_MIN_PX` 4): `side_avail` in HUD px per `H`, which does not depend on the panel arrangement, and (last column) the offered set under the withdrawn 100 / 100 / 44 / 30 % budgets, kept as the reference the two arrangement tables below are compared with.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

| Picture (`k_auto`, `H_default`) | `H` 3 | `H` 4 | `H` 5 | `H` 6 | `H` 7 | `H` 8 | `H` 9-11 | `H` 12 | `offered(P_w, P_h)` at the withdrawn 100 x 44 budget (reference only) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1280 x 720 (2, 3) | 149 | **102** | 73 x | 53 x | - | - | - | - | {3, 4} |
| 1280 x 800 `keep` / `expand` (2, 3) | 149 | 102 | 73 x | 53 x | - | - | - | - | {3, 4} |
| 1366 x 768 (2, 3) | 162 | 110 | 80 x | 59 x | - | - | - | - | {3, 4} |
| 1600 x 900 (2, 3) | 197 | 138 | 102 | 77 x | - | - | - | - | {3, 4, 5} |
| 1920 x 1080 (3, 3) | 246 | 174 | 130 | 102 | - | - | - | - | {3, 4, 5, 6} |
| 2048 x 1152 (3, 3) | 264 | 188 | 141 | 110 | - | - | - | - | {3, 4, 5, 6} |
| 2560 x 1440 (4, 4) | - | 246 | 188 | 149 | 121 | 102 | - | - | {4, 5, 6, 7, 8} |
| 3440 x 1440 ultrawide (4, 4) | - | 246+ | 188+ | 149+ | 121+ | 102+ | - | - | {4, 5, 6, 7, 8} (wider than 16:9 only adds `side_avail`; the vertical fit depends on `P_h` alone) |
| 3840 x 2160 (6, 6) | - | - | - | 246 | 204 | 174 | 149 / 130 / 114 | 102 | {6, 7, ... 12} |

`x` marks a cell that is **not offered** at the withdrawn 100 / 100 / 44 / 30 % reference budgets: it fails `fit_h` (`side_avail` below 100: every `x` cell here) and, on the smaller pictures, `fit_v` as well (1280 x 720 at `H` 5: the row needs 8 + 44 = 52 HUD px against 43; at `H` 6: 50 against 36; 1600 x 900 at `H` 6: 52 against 45). **Worked cell:** 1280 x 720, `H` 4: `canvas_w_int` 320, `x_strip = (320 - 76) div 2` = 122, `margin_hud_x = ceil(64 / 4)` = 16, `side_avail = 122 - 16 - 4` = **102**. Vertical: `margin_hud_y = ceil(36 / 4)` = 9. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **The reference budget is withdrawn: the panel's own content does not fit 100 x 44.** The cell tally, in HUD px at `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` 16, with 2-px gaps and 18-px rows:

| Cell | Contents | Width |
|---|---|---|
| Reserve | glyph 16 + gap 2 + numeral 3 digits 17 + gap 2 + **ambient-O2 slot 3 digits 17** (HS-12) + gap 2 + mark slot 16 | 72 |
| Integrity | glyph 16 + gap 2 + flat 180-degree dial 32 (needle `L` 16, pivot on the bottom interior row) + gap 2 + mark slot 16 | 68 |
| Warmth | as integrity | 68 |
| Charge and lamp | glyph 16 + gap 2 + `toggle_beam` keycap 16 + gap 2 + mark slot 16 = 52, + gap 2 + **ship-alarm slot 16** (HS-25) | 70 |

Two needle-only cells alone are 136 HUD px in one row (against 100), and the four cells stacked as a column need 78 HUD px of height (against 44), so a 100-wide, 44-tall box cannot hold them with their glyphs, marks and slots. **Arrangement ROW (2 x 2):** row 1 reserve 72 + gap 2 + integrity 68 = 142, row 2 warmth 68 + gap 2 + charge and lamp 70 = 140, plus a 2-px border, so **144 wide x 40 tall** (18 + 2 + 18 + 2). **Arrangement COLUMN (4 rows):** the widest cell 72 plus the border, so **74 wide x 80 tall** (4 x 18 + 3 x 2 + 2). Above either, the **notice stack** (HS-46: the failed-write notice plate 18 tall plus the restore-line plate up to 20 tall, 38 in all) rises from the panel's edge; it is bounded by `fit_notice` as a declared transient exception, not by the at-rest row share. The rail stays at 100 x 44 and is not recomputed (it has not been shown to fail). A third arrangement, any wider-than-ROW or shorter-than-COLUMN layout, is admitted only if it is drawn and its `offered()` row is computed the same way.

**The offered sets (the arrangement table; computed from the formula above, every cell reproducible from `side_avail` and the row-share arithmetic).** `fit_v` per arrangement: ROW `margin_hud_y + 44 <= canvas_h_int * 30 div 100`; COLUMN `margin_hud_y + 80 <= canvas_h_int * COL_MAX_FRAC_PCT div 100`; `fit_notice` `margin_hud_y + H_panel + 38 <= canvas_h_int * 75 div 100`.

| Picture | Withdrawn 100 x 44 (reference) | ROW 144 x 40 (30 %) | COLUMN 74 x 80 at `COL_MAX_FRAC_PCT` 50 | COLUMN at 39 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1280 x 720 | {3, 4} | {3} | {3, 4} | {3} |
| 1280 x 800 `keep` / `expand` | {3, 4} | {3} | {3, 4} | {3} |
| 1366 x 768 | {3, 4} | {3} | {3, 4} | {3} |
| 1600 x 900 | {3, 4, 5} | {3} | {3, 4, 5} | {3} |
| 1920 x 1080 | {3, 4, 5, 6} | {3, 4} | {3, 4, 5, 6} | {3, 4} |
| 2048 x 1152 | {3, 4, 5, 6} | {3, 4} | {3, 4, 5, 6} | {3, 4} |
| 2560 x 1440 | {4, 5, 6, 7, 8} | {4, 5, 6} | {4, 5, 6, 7, 8} | {4, 5, 6} |
| 3440 x 1440 | {4, 5, 6, 7, 8} | {4, 5, 6, 7, 8} | {4, 5, 6, 7, 8} | {4, 5, 6} |
| 3840 x 2160 | {6, ..., 12} | {6, 7, 8, 9} | {6, ..., 12} | {6, 7, 8, 9} |

ROW fails on width (`side_avail` below 144) from `H` 4 on the `k_auto` = 2 pictures and from `H` 5 on 1080p; COLUMN at 50 % fails only on 1280 x 720, 1280 x 800, 1366 x 768 at `H` 5 and above (`side_avail` 73 and `fit_v`) and on 1600 x 900 at `H` 6; COLUMN at 39 % fails on `fit_v` first (1280 x 720 at `H` 4 needs 9 + 80 = 89 against 70). **Reading the table (the choice is `/ux-design`'s, by the resulting set, with the accessibility-specialist signing off any reduction):** ROW keeps P1's upper 70 % but gives up the 133 % `H` ceiling on every `k_auto` = 2 display (720p, 1366 x 768, 1600 x 900 and the Steam Deck offer `H` 3 only, a 100 % ceiling: the largest numeral a player there can reach is 21 screen px cap height) and the floor invariant (`H_default + 1` is not offered there). COLUMN at 50 % keeps every offered set of the reference table and the floor invariant (`side_avail` is at least 102 against 74) at the cost of a left-edge tower that rises to **49.4 % of the canvas height at its worst offered cells** (1280 x 720 at `H` 4, 1080p at `H` 6, 1440p at `H` 8, 2160p at `H` 12: `9 + 80 = 89` of 180) and, while a notice shows, to 70.6 % (`9 + 80 + 38 = 127` of 180); COLUMN at 39 % is the lowest share that still offers `H_default` at 1280 x 720 and loses the same sets as ROW. The tower is 74 HUD px wide at the left edge, not centred, but the "upper 70 % HUD-free" rule it breaks is the art bible's, so the trade belongs to the art-director and the accessibility-specialist (OQ-HS-81, OQ-HS-82). **Floor invariant (restated per arrangement):** `H_default` is in `offered()` on every picture for both arrangements (the layout's fallback, asserted by AC-HS-79); `H_default + 1` is also offered on every picture for COLUMN at 50 %, with at least 28 HUD px of `side_avail` to spare (102 against 74), and for ROW only where the table shows it. **The accessibility cost of the stopgap:** under ROW, or under any arrangement that loses `H` 4 on a `k_auto` = 2 display, the ceiling there is 100 % of default against the art bible's 100-200 % (HR-12); under COLUMN at 50 % it stays 133 % (`H` 4, 28 screen px cap height); the cost is recorded for the accessibility-specialist (OQ-HS-34). The deterministic stacked layout (option (b)) is to be evaluated before Alpha. `PANEL_SWEEP_DEG` is limited to the arrangement that has actually been drawn, 180 (HS-16).

### D.9 anchor_snap (HS-9; registered, HUD-owned)

The anchor_snap formula is defined as:

`W_int = P_w div H ; H_int = P_h div H ; extra_x = P_w - H * W_int ; x_left = margin_hud_x ; x_right(w) = W_int - margin_hud_x - w ; x_centre(w) = (W_int - w) div 2 ; y_bottom(h) = H_int - margin_hud_y - h ; screen_x = H * x_hud + (extra_x div 2) for centred elements, H * x_hud otherwise ; plate_origin_x = floor(ax) - (w div 2)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `P_w`, `P_h` | int screen px | 1280 x 720 up | Pipeline picture rect | The picture the HUD canvas covers |
| `H` | int | `H_default`..`2*H_default` | Pipeline | HUD scale |
| `w`, `h` | int HUD px | element sizes | UX spec | Always whole HUD px |
| `extra_x` | int screen px | 0 to `H - 1` | derived | The sub-`H` remainder of the canvas width (the Deck's fractional canvas, 426.67 HUD px at `H` 3) |
| `ax` | real HUD px | `world_to_hud(anchor)` of the unjolted pose (Pipeline), cited | Pipeline | Prompt anchor; the plate origin is floored |

**Output Range:** every edge lands on a whole HUD px, plus at most half the remainder for centred elements (at most `(H - 1) div 2` screen px). A prompt plate swims against its anchor by at most `H / 2` screen px (plates are integer multiples of `H`); that is the cost of the rule, not a defect (HS-39). **Space:** `x_hud`, `x_centre`, `x_right` and `y_bottom` are HUD-px values relative to the HUD-root `Control` (HS-9); `screen_x` and the `extra_x div 2` offset are the conversion a test uses and the HUD-root's placement carries, not a second layout space. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Example:** the Deck, `P_w` 1280, `H` 3: `W_int = 426`, `extra_x = 2`. The strip plate (`w` 76): `x_centre = (426 - 76) div 2 = 175`, `screen_x = 3*175 + 1 = 526`, the plate centre `526 + 114 = 640` exactly. A 65-px element: `x_centre = 180`, `screen_x = 541`, centre `541 + 97.5 = 638.5`, off by 1.5 screen px (0.5 HUD px). The right-anchored 100-px rail: `x_right = 426 - 22 - 100 = 304`, right slack `3 * (426.67 - 426) = 2` screen px.

### D.10 safe_margin (HS-55; registered, HUD-owned; the 5 % is the art bible's)

The safe_margin formula is defined as:

`margin_screen = (5 * P + 99) div 100 ; margin_hud = ceil_div(margin_screen, H)` per axis, where `P` is `P_w` or `P_h`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `P` | int screen px | 720 up | Pipeline picture rect | Picture extent on the axis |
| `H` | int | `H_default`..`2*H_default` | Pipeline | HUD scale |
| `HUD_SAFE_MARGIN_PCT` | int % | 5 | art bible ab:754, cited (never a second value) | The 5 % |

**Output Range:** 5 % to 5 % plus `(H - 1)/H` HUD px; never below 5 %, so it is testable with no node.

**Example:** 1920 x 1080, `H` 3: 96 and 54 screen px, 32 x 18 HUD px (the Pipeline's own figure); 1280 x 720, `H` 3: 64 and 36 screen px, 22 x 12 HUD px; 1280 x 720, `H` 6: 64 to 11 and 36 to 6 HUD px.

### D.11 integrity_zone_ticks (HS-18; registered, HUD-owned)

The integrity_zone_ticks formula is defined as:

`tick_pm(x) = SUIT_HP_x_bp div 10 ; tick_theta_cdeg(x) = (PANEL_SWEEP_DEG * 100 * tick_pm(x)) div 1000`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SUIT_HP_x_bp` | int basis points | `SUIT_HP_OK` 7000, `SUIT_LEAK_HP` 5000, `SUIT_HP_FAIL` 2500 | Suit & Equipment (registry), cited | The suit's three integrity thresholds; **never** the Graph's `HP_OK` 7000 / `HP_FAIL` 3000 (a different symbol by registry rule) |
| `PANEL_SWEEP_DEG` | int degrees | **180** `[PH]`, range 180-300 (PF-45's `arc_deg` range) | this GDD, decided with the panel arrangement (HS-16) | Sweep of the suit-panel dials. 180 is a flat arc with the pivot on the cell's bottom interior row; larger sweeps need the 4-row column arrangement. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; the default was 240, which cannot fit the 2 x 2 arrangement)* |
| `start_deg`, `bbox_w`, `bbox_h` | int degrees; HUD px | `start_deg = 90 + PANEL_SWEEP_DEG div 2`; `bbox_w = 2L`; `bbox_h = L * (1 + sin((PANEL_SWEEP_DEG - 180) / 2))` | derived, as in D.5 | The needle's start angle and the dial's bounding box. At 180 degrees a dial of `L` 16 is 32 wide by 16 tall (it fits an 18-px cell at the limit); at 240 degrees it is 32 by 24, which an 18-px cell cannot hold |

**Output Range:** `tick_pm` in 0 to 1000 and the angle in 0 to `PANEL_SWEEP_DEG`; the dial's bounding box is part of the panel's width and height budget (D.8).

**Example:** `PANEL_SWEEP_DEG` 180 (default): `SUIT_HP_OK` gives 700 permille and 126 degrees; `SUIT_LEAK_HP` gives 500 and 90; `SUIT_HP_FAIL` gives 250 and 45 (at a 240-degree sweep, the column arrangement only: 168, 120, 60). Using the Graph's `HP_FAIL` 3000 by mistake would draw the tick at 300 permille, 9 degrees (about 2.5 px at a 16-px needle) off at 180 degrees. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.12 charge_fill_and_warn (HS-19; registered, HUD-owned)

The charge_fill_and_warn formula is defined as:

`fill_px = (displayed_pm * CELL_FILL_PX + 999) div 1000 ; warn_pm = (CELL_WARN_S * 1000) div CELL_S`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `displayed_pm` | int permille | 0-1000 | `perceived(suit.charge).displayed_pm` | The cell's charge as shown |
| `CELL_FILL_PX` | int HUD px | 8 `[PH]`, range 6-12 | this GDD (art-director: an 8-px interior) | Interior height of the 16 x 16 cell glyph. (orchestrator ruling: the ux-designer's 24-px figure assumed a wider cell that HS-19 does not define; the art-director's 8 stands) |
| `CELL_S`, `CELL_WARN_S` | int seconds | 1200 (600-2400), 20 (10-30) | Suit & Equipment (registry), cited | Cell endurance and the warning point |
| `warn_pm` | int permille | 16 at defaults | derived, a **cross-check only** | The Suit decides when the mark appears; the HUD never compares against this |

**Output Range:** `fill_px` in 0 to `CELL_FILL_PX`; it is `0` only at exactly empty (the ceiling parallels HS-17, so a remaining charge never reads empty); each step is `1000/CELL_FILL_PX` = 125 permille, positional and not a luminance event. Absent (an empty slot) when `CELL_ENABLED` is 0.

**Example:** `displayed_pm` 1000 gives 8; 500 gives 4; 126 gives 2; 125 gives 1; 16 (the warn point, 20 s of 1200 s) gives 1, so the cell is not drawn empty before the mark; 0 gives 0. A floor would draw 0 px below 125 permille (about 150 s of cell left, about 130 s before the `CELL_WARN_S` mark), which is why the ceiling is used.

### D.13 gleam_lit_length (HS-27; registered, HUD-owned; dormant at MVP)

The gleam_lit_length formula is defined as:

`n_lit = 0 if leak_amplitude_pm == 0 else min(edge_len_px, max(2, (edge_len_px * leak_amplitude_pm + 500) div 1000))`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `leak_amplitude_pm` | int permille | 0, or 250-1000 while a lie is active (floor stage-indexed 400/325/250 at stages 0-1/2/3) | `Perceived` record (Filter formula; floor `AMP_FLOOR_PM` 400 at stages 0-1, `AMP_FLOOR_S3_PM` 250 at stage 3, range 250-600) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* | The gleam's amplitude, owned by the Filter |
| `edge_len_px` | int HUD px | 5 up (the `max(2, ...)` floor matters only at 5 px) | the host edge (arc glass about 24, needle edge 16, numeral window about 17) | Length of the edge the gleam runs along; `GLEAM_EDGE_PX` is its thickness (unregistered; PF-16 1 art px, range 1-2) |

**Output Range:** 0 or 2 to `edge_len_px`; anchored at the needle tip or the arc's leading edge so it moves with the reading; colour `#B07BE6` exactly, no alpha, no tint.

**Example:** a 17-px numeral window at amplitude 400: `(6800 + 500) div 1000 = 7` px lit; at 850: `(14450 + 500) div 1000 = 14`. A 24-px arc edge at 400 gives 10; a 16-px needle edge at 400 gives 6. `max(2, ...)` never binds for edges of 6 px or more at the Filter's amplitude floor of 250 permille (`(6 * 250 + 500) div 1000` = 2); it binds only for a 5-px edge below 300 permille (`(5 * 250 + 500) div 1000` = 1, raised to 2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier "5 px or more" was off by one and the main review's "8 px" over-stated it; the creative-director accepts the systems-designer's 6)*.

### D.14 warmth_band_index (HS-18; HUD-owned; edges are the Suit's, SE-29)

The warmth_band_index formula (the name is kept for traceability; it returns a load zone, not an ambient band) is defined as:

`zone = (displayed_pm >= WARMTH_EDGE_PM[1]) + (displayed_pm >= WARMTH_EDGE_PM[2]) + (displayed_pm >= WARMTH_EDGE_PM[3]) + (displayed_pm >= WARMTH_EDGE_PM[4]) ; notch_theta_cdeg(k) = (sweep_deg * 100 * WARMTH_EDGE_PM[k]) div 1000` *(the HUD counts the Suit's four inner published edges by name; an edge value belongs to the upper zone; the HUD invents no edge)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `displayed_pm` | int permille | 0-1000, cold to hot, 500 nominal | `perceived(suit.warmth).displayed_pm`, the Suit's `warmth_pm = (500 * (thermal_load + TL_MAX)) / TL_MAX` (SE-29) | The warmth reading |
| `WARMTH_EDGE_PM[1..4]` | int permille | 333 / 466 / 533 / 666 at the defaults `[PH]` | Suit & Equipment SE-29: the images of -`TL_CRITICAL`, -`TL_STRAINED`, +`TL_STRAINED`, +`TL_CRITICAL` (the axis ends 0 and 1000 are the images of -/+`TL_MAX` and are not notches); cited, never given a HUD-side value | The four notch positions. If the Suit tunes `TL_*`, the notches move with it |
| `zone` | int | 0-4 | output | Five pattern-coded load zones, cold on the left: 0 `WZ_COLD_CRIT`, 1 `WZ_COLD_STRAIN`, 2 `WZ_COMFORT`, 3 `WZ_HOT_STRAIN`, 4 `WZ_HOT_CRIT` (same order as HS-18). Drawing ids only; the HUD never infers a rung from a zone |
| `sweep_deg` | int degrees | 180 default (`PANEL_SWEEP_DEG`) | D.11 | The panel dial's sweep |

**Output Range:** `zone` in 0 to 4; no hue (Paper White ink). The `WZ_COMFORT` zone is `[466, 533)` permille wide, 6.7 % of the axis: about 12 degrees at a 180-degree sweep, 3.4 px of arc at a 16-px needle, which is why the notch **tick** (3 px radial), not the zone width, is the information (as for the O2 dial, HS-13).

**Example:** `displayed_pm` **500** gives the sum `1 + 1 + 0 + 0` = zone 2 (500 >= 333 and 500 >= 466 are true; 500 >= 533 and 500 >= 666 are false), so **`WZ_COMFORT` is lit at the nominal reading**; 0 gives 0; 332 gives 0; 333 gives 1; 465 gives 1; 466 gives 2; 532 gives 2; 533 gives 3; 665 gives 3; 666 gives 4; 1000 gives 4. At a 180-degree sweep the notches sit at 59.9, 83.9, 95.9 and 119.9 degrees. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; the earlier example put `COMFORT` at `displayed_pm` 600)*

### D.15 needle_frame_index (HS-9, HS-29; registered, HUD-owned)

The needle_frame_index formula is defined as:

`N_frames = ceil(L * sweep_deg * pi / 180) ; frame = (displayed_pm * (N_frames - 1) + 500) div 1000 ; theta_frame_cdeg = (frame * sweep_deg * 100) div (N_frames - 1)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the frame-to-angle map is defined; the drawn tip is D.5's tip form evaluated at `theta_frame`, while D.5's `theta` from `pm` is the pure function's unquantised target angle, so a test of the drawn needle compares the frame's tip and a test of the pure function compares the target tip, never one against the other)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L` | int HUD px | strip at most 11; panel at least `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` (16), recommended 24 | art-director; `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` owned by In-World Instruments | Needle length |
| `sweep_deg` | int degrees | `STRIP_SWEEP_DEG` 180, `PANEL_SWEEP_DEG` 180 (240 in the column arrangement only) | this GDD | The dial's sweep *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `displayed_pm` | int permille | 0-1000 | `Perceived` | The needle target |
| `N_frames` | int | 35 / 51 / 76 at 180 degrees for `L` 11 / 16 / 24 (68 / 101 for `L` 16 / 24 at 240 degrees) | derived | Frames in the pre-baked strip (-> ADR candidate A decides baked strip versus snapped sprite; the observable requirement is only the quantisation and the never-rest proof). `FRAME_PM = 1000 / (N_frames - 1)` feeds D.6 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `frame` in 0 to `N_frames - 1`. The tip moves at most one quantised pixel per frame step: about 5.2 degrees per tip pixel at `L` 11 and about 2.4 degrees at `L` 24.

**Example:** strip `L` 11, 180 degrees: `N_frames = ceil(34.56) = 35`; 210 dkPa at range 300 (`pm` 700) gives `(700*34 + 500) div 1000 = 24`, whose frame angle is `theta_frame` = 127.05 degrees (`24 * 18000 div 34` = 12705 cdeg) and whose drawn tip at `L` 11 and pivot (12, 15) is (19, 6) (the pure function's target tip at 126 degrees is (18, 6), D.5); 175 dkPa (`pm` 583) gives frame 20, `theta_frame` 105.88 degrees and the same drawn tip (15, 4) as the target; the `HYPOXIA_PP` default edge (`pm` 467) gives frame 16. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Panel `L` 16, 180 degrees: `ceil(50.27) = 51`; `L` 24: `ceil(75.4) = 76` (at 240 degrees: 68 and 101). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Glide check on `L` 24: `rate_floor = 28.6/24 = 1.19` degrees/s (0.5 px/s of tip drift) plus wander 2.2 px at 0.40 to 0.45 Hz changes the tip pixel at least every 1.0 s (`NEVER_REST_MAX_S`); at `L` 11 the same drift would need 2.6 degrees/s, above `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG` 2.0, hence ineligible. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*

### D.16 cancel_drain_fill (HS-41, HS-42; HUD-owned, presentation-only, integer) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

*(Rewritten 2026-10-27, design review.)* The first form drained at `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE` times the fill speed, so the time to empty scaled with the channel (a 360-tick weld bar took about 3 s) and two overlapping channels were undefined. The drain is now a fixed duration, written in integers; for a hold-to-confirm **release** the Controller's own `b` (D.10) drains at `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE` (0.4 s from full) and the HUD draws `fill_px = (hold_pm * L) div 1000` with no drain of its own. This formula is the HUD's cosmetic drain after a **channel** `PLAYER_CANCEL` (`channel_view.ended_by`).

The cancel_drain_fill formula is defined as:

`fill(t_ms) = fill_0 - (fill_0 * min(t_ms, BAR_DRAIN_MS)) div BAR_DRAIN_MS ; a new ACK sets t_ms = BAR_DRAIN_MS for any drain in progress before the new bar starts`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fill_0` | int HUD px | 0..`L` | the bar's fill when `ended_by` becomes `PLAYER_CANCEL` | Fill at the cancel |
| `BAR_DRAIN_MS` | int ms | 400 `[PH]`, range 200-500 | this GDD (new knob) | Drain duration from any fill and any `length_class`; 400 ms is the Controller's own hold-release time (0.8 s / 2.0) |
| `L` | int HUD px | 16 / 24 / 40 | `BAR_LEN_*_PX` by `length_class` | Interior length |
| `t_ms` | int ms | 0.. | `present_time` since the cancel edge | Frozen under pause; the caller clamps `dt` |

**Output Range:** 0 to `L`; whole cells are removed right to left; monotone non-increasing; exactly 0 at `t_ms` = `BAR_DRAIN_MS`; cosmetic only (the simulation channel already ended); reduced motion snaps to 0.

**Example:** SHORT bar, `L` 16, from full: at 200 ms `16 - (16 * 200) div 400` = 8, at 400 ms 0 (the Controller's AC). LONG bar, `L` 40, from half (20 px): at 200 ms 10, at 400 ms 0 (it took 1.0 s at `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE`); a 360-tick weld from full: 40 px in 400 ms instead of about 3 s. A second press at 150 ms into a drain sets the drain to its end (fill 0) and the new bar starts clean, so drains never stack.

### Cited formulas (owned elsewhere; verbatim outputs, not redefined)

### D.17 cap_px (Pipeline D.4)

The cap_px formula is defined as:

`cap_px = 7 * H`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `H` | int | `H_default`..`2*H_default` | Pipeline PR-12 | HUD scale |
| `cap_px` | int screen px | 21, 28, 35, 42 at `H` 3, 4, 5, 6 | Pipeline D.4 | The 7-art-px numeral cap height on screen; the floor that makes the 12 % strip cap unshrinkable |

**Output Range:** at least 21 screen px. **Example:** `H` 4 gives 28 screen px, 7 HUD px.

### D.18 prompt_show (Interaction D.UI-2)

The prompt_show formula is defined as:

`show = (prompt_state != HIDDEN) AND (stable_ticks >= PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS) AND (since_last_show >= PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS) ; hide = immediate whenever prompt_state == HIDDEN or focus_ref changed`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `prompt_state` | enum | HIDDEN, ENABLED, NEEDS_TOOL | `focus_view`, Interaction | Interaction has already applied lighting and reach |
| `stable_ticks` | int ticks | 0.. | HUD counter on `focus_seq` | Ticks the same focus has held |
| `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` | int ticks | **13** (13-20; was 9, 6-20) | HUD-owned `[PH]`, adopted from ia:1083 and raised above the 12-tick corridor churn in the design review | Show debounce *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` | int ticks | 20 (20-40) | HUD-owned `[PH]` | Floor `ceil_div(TICK_RATE, FLASH_MAX_PER_S)` = 20 |
| `since_last_show` | int ticks | 0.. | HUD counter | Ticks since the last show-start |

**Output Range:** boolean; at most `TICK_RATE / PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` = 3 show-starts per second by construction, further reduced by D.2 in STRICT mode and for an unexempt plate (an exempt plate waits only for its debounce) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **Example:** a corridor changes focus every 12 ticks: A takes focus at tick 0 and is replaced at 12, before it has been stable for 13, so it never shows (at the earlier debounce of 9 it showed from tick 9 to 12, a 50 ms pop); B takes focus at 12 and is replaced at 24, and so on: no plate shows. A focus that holds: C takes focus at 0 and stays; it shows at tick 13 (if `since_last_show >= 20`, else at the tick that satisfies it) and, if the player presses at tick 5, the accepted press force-admits the plate at tick 5 (HS-37). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.19 fill_px (Interaction D.UI-1)

The fill_px formula is defined as:

`fill_px = floor(progress_pm * L / 1000)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `progress_pm` | int permille | 0-1000 | `channel_view.progress_pm`, Interaction | Channel progress |
| `L` | int HUD px | `BAR_LEN_SHORT_PX` 16 / `BAR_LEN_MEDIUM_PX` 24 / `BAR_LEN_LONG_PX` 40 `[PH]`, each 12-64, S < M < L | HUD-owned, by `length_class` | Interior length; `length_class` is Interaction's: SHORT if HOLD_CONFIRM, MEDIUM if the declared duration is below `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` 240, else LONG |

**Output Range:** 0 to `L`, positional steps (not luminance events). **Example:** a V8 cut of 240 ticks is LONG (240 is not below 240): 6 ticks per px; `progress_pm` 500 gives 20 px. Hold-to-confirm 48 ticks at `L` 16 is 3 ticks per px; a weld of 360 ticks at `L` 40 is 9; a cap of 90 ticks at `L` 24 is 3.75; a scan of 120 ticks at `L` 24 is 5; a patch of 180 ticks at `L` 24 is 7.5.

### D.20 lying_digit_tick_schedule (Perception Filter registry entry)

The lying_digit_tick_schedule formula is defined as:

`T_tick = max(LIE_TICK_MS, N_r * 334)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `LIE_TICK_MS` | int ms | 400 (range 334-1000; reduced motion may raise to 600) | Perception Filter (registry), cited | Lower bound of a lying digit period |
| `N_r` | int | 1-4 (`LIES_MAX` is 1 / 2 / 4 by stage; 4 at stage 3) | Filter | Concurrently lying numeral readouts |

**Output Range:** 400 / 668 / 1002 / 1336 ms for `N_r` 1 to 4 (verbatim from the registry; readings staggered by `T_tick / N_r` >= 334 ms, so consecutive transitions in a region are at least 334 ms apart), giving 2.5 / 3.0 / 3.0 / 3.0 transitions per second in total (rounded). **Example:** `N_r` 2 gives `max(400, 2 * 334) = 668` ms per reading, about 1.5 each, 3.0 in all, which already fills `FLASH_MAX_PER_S`; this is the Vertical Slice A arithmetic behind the D.2 overrun note. The HUD draws the substitution when `tick_phase_ms` says and never reschedules it.

## Edge Cases

Ids are `EC-HS-n`. Each case states the exact outcome, never "handle gracefully". Cases the rulings overrode in the specialists' lists are not repeated: the clamped-plate fallback (HS-39 draws no plate instead), a centre-low first-frame line and a non-deferrable notice (HS-46 and HS-47 place both at the panel's edge, the notice at the third deferral priority), and the retired alpha-ramp gleam and `NUMERAL_MIN_HOLD_TICKS` (replaced by lit-length and `HONEST_TICK_MS`).

### Scale, layout and display

- **If `H` changes while a channel is running**: the layout is recomputed on the next render frame by the pure `HudLayout` function; the plate and bar re-quantise to the new `H`; the bar does not reset, its `fill_px` is recomputed from `channel_view.progress_pm`; nothing is read from or written to the simulation. The change is a cut, never a tween (SCALE_CHANGE state). (EC-HS-1)
- **If the zoom ladder steps from `k` 3 to 1 or to `k_auto + 2` mid-channel and the anchor now projects outside the picture rect**: the plate and the bar, which rides the plate, hide on the next render frame and are never clamped to the frame edge; when the anchor returns they reappear without a debounce reset, and the bar's fill is recomputed from `progress_pm`; the simulation's channel is unaffected. This is distinct from losing `lit` (EC-HS-32), where the plate hides but the bar stays. Zoom changes plate position by re-projection only; plate and bar sizes are HUD px and do not change. (EC-HS-2)
- **If a settings value or a window size produces a `(picture, H)` pair for which `fit` is false**: `HudLayout` returns a defect flag (an assertion in debug, the counter `hud_layout_overlap` in release) and the pipeline root falls back to the layout at `H_default` (always offered; there may be no previous layout after a resize or at first mount); the settings offer only `offered(P_w, P_h)` (D.8). At MVP the offered steps are the Pipeline's PR-12 option (a), computed per panel arrangement (D.8): a 1280 x 720 window offers `H` 3 and 4 under COLUMN and `H` 3 only under ROW (`H` 4 gives 102 HUD px per side against the ROW panel's 144; `H` 5 and 6 give 73 and 53 against COLUMN's 74). The no-overlap rule is binding (HS-54). (EC-HS-3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If the display is the Steam Deck (1280 x 800, `H` 3 over a `k` 2 world) under `keep`** (the picture mode pinned for every Deck figure here; `expand` is the Pipeline's post-spike ideal and would only add canvas height, 266.67 HUD px): the canvas is 426.67 x 240 HUD px *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; every rectangle is floored to a whole HUD px, the sub-`H` remainder (`extra_x` 2 screen px at `H` 3) is split by `anchor_snap`, right and bottom slack is at most 1 HUD px, and nine-slice borders sit on whole HUD px, so no half-pixel border appears; the strip is about 15 % of the width, the accepted platform overage (HS-15). Behaviour of `gui_snap_controls_to_pixels` here is NOT SOURCEABLE until the spike (HS-9). (EC-HS-4)
- **If the picture is wider than 16:9 (an ultrawide)**: the strip stays centred on the picture rect, the panel and rail anchor to its edges inside the 5 % margin, and no element stretches or reflows, because layout is a pure function of `(picture rect, H)` (HS-51); more canvas width only adds slack to `bottom_row_fit`. The Pipeline owns the rect. (EC-HS-5)
- **If a `Control` is parented under the world `SubViewport`**: the scene-tree lint fails the build (Pipeline lint (b)); it is a build gate, not a runtime check, because in a release build the node would receive world light, glow and the Filter. (EC-HS-6)
- **If the prompt anchor is on screen but no placement satisfies the chest keep-out `PROMPT_KEEPOUT_PX` 16 and the `PROMPT_EDGE_MARGIN_PX` 4 margin after Interaction's AD-5 side order and the world-px fallback offset**: the plate is not drawn (as if off screen), never clamped over the chest; counter `UI_PLACEMENT_WITHHELD`. A plate over the chest point would mask the beam origin, which is the pointer. This runtime withholding does not apply to the `give_up` plate or the self plate (HS-39): they have no world anchor and are never withheld, and a miss there is a build-gate defect (AC-HS-147 (c), AC-HS-150) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)*. (EC-HS-7)
- **If a candidate plate position would overlap the strip, panel or rail rectangles**: the plate is moved to the next AD-5 side or withheld; it is never drawn over a reading. The three rectangles are an exclusion region. (EC-HS-8)

### Clocks, pause and time

- **If the kernel is paused (solo) while a mark is mid-pulse**: `T` stops, and every active mark is held ON solid, undimmed, from the first paused frame (HS-23; CF-HS-13 resolved by the art-director's alternative): a frozen OFF outline can no longer read as safe beside a CRITICAL reading. An inactive slot stays empty. On resume the mark follows the unchanged `T`; the pause cycle costs at most one mark leading edge (the arbiter counts it, never delays it). Layer 3 (the Chart Table) stays live on process time over the HUD. (EC-HS-9)
- **If the game resumes after a 10 s pause, a window-focus loss or a hitch**: the first post-resume time step fed to any presentation timer is clamped to `HUD_DT_CLAMP_MS` 50; no latch, debounce, settle or tick fires from the gap; a forced discrete rebuild runs (HS-7); the honest grid is re-armed. No integrator divides by the time step. (EC-HS-10)
- **If the render rate is 30 fps and a mark edge falls between render frames**: the edge is drawn on the first render frame whose `(T, tick_alpha)` is at or past the boundary (at most 33.3 ms skew against the audio edge) and counted once; there is no double edge. If a hitch skips a whole `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` period the mark shows the phase of the current `(T, alpha_pm)` with no catch-up pulses. (EC-HS-11)
- **If `T` is read before the first committed tick (boot)**: the surface state is UNMOUNTED, nothing is drawn, `view()` is not read, the mark phase is 0 and no mark exists (no rung). The HUD draws the last committed state; none means nothing. (EC-HS-12)

### Readings: zero, maximum and out of range

- **If `suit.o2` `displayed_value` exceeds `O2_DIAL_MAX_DKPA` (an enriched compartment)**: the needle clamps at the right stop (`pm` 1000), the numeral prints `min(displayed_value, 999)`, and nothing spills into the mark slot. The fixed three-column field cannot grow. (EC-HS-13)
- **If `suit.o2` reads 0 (vacuum) with `breath_src` GASP or NONE (no breathable source and no reserve engaged)**: the strip shows the ambient reading (HS-12): the numeral is `0` right-aligned, the needle rests on the left stop, the arc band and pivot cap turn Graphite lit as a hard cut (no fade), and the mark appears only when the Suit's breath rung is CRITICAL after its hold. The HUD never infers the rung from the 0. **If the same vacuum has `breath_src` RESERVE (the suit is breathing its reserve, the door-breach case)**: the strip does **not** show that 0; it shows the reserve (HS-12): the numeral is `reserve_seconds` (for example `90` falling), the arc band and pivot cap stay O2 Cyan (a breathable source exists: the reserve), the tank tick and warn notch are lit and the dkPa zone ticks are Graphite, and the ambient 0 is drawn in the panel's ambient slot. (EC-HS-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If `breath_src` changes between ROOM and RESERVE faster than the player can read it (a threshold flicker, for example a hatch cycling)**: the swap of HS-12 (3) is drawn on each frame that reads a new enum value and is one counted source-state event per change, never deferred; the Suit publishes `breath_src_change_seq` and its own rung hold, so the HUD adds no debounce of its own; if repeated swaps would exceed `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` the overrun is recorded as `HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN` and nothing else changes (it is a published source state, like a hue cut). Both honest numerals are armed on each swap, so no numeral boundary lands within the same grid cell. (EC-HS-56) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If `RESERVE_S` is tuned to 240**: the reserve numeral uses all three digit cells at every `H` (`240` at full), the panel row is authored for three digits, and `reserve_seconds` is `0` only at exactly empty. (EC-HS-15)
- **If the reserve is refilled (a top-up, MVP-live: `top_up` is an MVP verb, user ruling X5)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the numeral counts upward in whole steps at the honest grid, never tweened; a boundary at which no displayed digit changed is not a luminance event; the dial-less reserve has no needle to settle. (EC-HS-16)
- **If `perceived(SUIT_KEY(k), PlayerId)` returns no record for a key not yet registered**: that reading's plate draws with a blank numeral and the arc at rest, `HUD_READ_MISSING` is counted, and the HUD never falls back to `view()`'s reading fields. (EC-HS-17)
- **If a stub Filter returns `leak_amplitude_pm` below 0 or above 1000, or `displayed_pm` out of range**: the HUD clamps to range and counts `hud_perceived_oob`; the record is a request and the Filter's own honest fallback is the owner's. (EC-HS-18)
- **If `CELL_ENABLED` is 0** (a data edge case; the cell is on at MVP, user ruling X5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the charge cell is an empty slot, with no "disabled" art and no mark; `toggle_beam` still has its keycap only when a cell exists (the lamp switch lives on the charge row). (EC-HS-19)
- **If the Suit retunes `TL_STRAINED`, `TL_CRITICAL` or `TL_MAX`, or the published edge list is not the four inner edges of SE-29**: the notches move with the Suit's published edges (D.14); the HUD never keeps a literal edge and never invents one; a build whose edge read is missing or not strictly increasing in 0-1000 draws no notch ticks, counts `hud_perceived_oob` and fails the data-load lint. The earlier "Suit has not yet published" case is closed: SE-29 publishes the axis and the edges. (EC-HS-20) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If the O2 numeral and the reserve numeral both change at one grid boundary**: they are one luminance event, not two (a boundary is one event however many digits change); both show the latest values. (EC-HS-21)

### The warning mark

- **If several axes are CRITICAL at once (up to six marks: strip, reserve cell, warmth, integrity, charge and the ship-alarm slot; the strip and reserve marks coexist while `breath_src` is `RESERVE`, HS-22)**: all marks share one phase (no per-mark offset), the leading edge is one event in `hud_flash_budget`, and the worst-case Alarm Red area is six marks of 16 x 16 (1,536 px at 256 each), which the visual criterion measures; at rest it is zero. (EC-HS-22) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If a rung falls from CRITICAL to a lower rung mid-pulse**: the mark hides on that render frame (hide is never deferred); the slot returns to empty, not to the OFF outline. (EC-HS-23)
- **If the seat is DOWNED**: the readings keep updating, the lamp stays lit, the marks persist and pulse (or stay solid, HS-23), there is no banner and no tint, and the solo "give up and reload" action (user ruling D7) is the Controller's command, drawn on the HUD only as the DOWNED-only `give_up` confirm plate (`hud_give_up_plate`, HS-42(b)): visible from the first DOWNED frame with the bar at 0, bar = `confirm_view.hold_pm`, gone on the first frame `confirm_view.give_up_live` is false; the plate carries no text beyond its verb, no banner, no tint *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; walk-only is surfaced by the breath and warmth readings *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. If the seat is restored into DEAD, no mark is drawn. (EC-HS-24)
- **If `give_up_live` drops and rises again within `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` (20) ticks of the plate's last show-start**: the hide is immediate; the re-show waits until `since_last_show >= 20` (so at most 3 show-starts per second, `floor(60 / 20)`); the Controller still accepts a press in the gap and the plate catches up with the live `hold_pm`. Example: show at T = 100, `give_up_live` false at T = 105, true at T = 110 -> plate re-shown at T = 120. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (EC-HS-57)
- **If `reduced_motion` is on**: the needle settle becomes a one-frame step (`p = g`), the cosmetic drain snaps to 0, glide slows by `RM_GLIDE_SCALE` but keeps drift and wander, the lying tick never falls below `LIE_TICK_MS`, and the mark holds ON solid (HR-8; settled by user ruling D91 (v): reduced motion still solidifies the mark, because a pulse is also a flash effect) with the Adaptive Audio sound carrying the alarm. **If `reduce_flashing` is on** (its first-launch default, user ruling D79): the mark holds ON solid, the arbiter runs in STRICT mode (every event of HS-47 counts, no area exemption), and nothing else changes: the settle, glide and drain are untouched, and the honest grid stays on its pinned cadence. The two settings are separate by what they change; the earlier table row said `reduce_flashing` leaves "no luminance events beyond show-starts", which this case contradicted. Under reduced motion a deaf player has the solid `suit.alarm` mark as the HUD ship-alarm cue (CF-HS-4, closed by user ruling D89). (EC-HS-25) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If a Vertical Slice A lying suit reading and its rung mark disagree**: the rung mark follows the reading's class (HS-30), so it may be absent beside a lying reading; the ship-alarm mark, if drawn, never follows a lie. No Acceptance Criterion asserts it (CF-HS-19); the MVP rule stands: the Suit's rung drives the mark, the HUD never computes CRITICAL from `displayed_value`. (EC-HS-26)

### The flash arbiter

- **If a prompt show-start is waiting and the window is full** (STRICT mode, or an unexempt plate in WEIGHTED mode, D.2): with the honest grid running, the next grid boundary is skipped, the prompt is admitted in its slot, and the numeral shows the then-latest value one boundary later (lag at most `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS`, unconditionally: a boundary is never skipped twice in a row). The prompt waits at most one grid cell when at most one other counted event is in the window, otherwise at most `MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 60 ticks and is then force-admitted and counted. Hides and mark edges are never deferred. (EC-HS-27) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If the lamp is toggled ten times in one second**: the world beam is never debounced (the cone is the truth); the charge-row glyph change is a **state-display draw** (HS-47 class A): the shared limiter admits at most one draw per `GLYPH_STATE_LATCH_TICKS` 20 across the whole class (so the glyph, the tool bracket, the glowstick count and the TAP relabel share three draws a second), each draw shows the latest state (ten toggles, an even number, end on the starting state and draw at most the net change), and a pending draw is force-admitted at `MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 60; the glyph may therefore lag the beam by up to 0.333 s in the common case and 1 s at the bound, and the HUD cannot flash faster than 3/s through the class. (EC-HS-28) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If a lie starts while an honest latch is armed on the same numeral**: the Filter's record wins; the honest latch state is dropped on the first non-HONEST `motion_mode`; on RETURN to HONEST the grid restarts armed, with no immediate change allowed for one grid cell. (EC-HS-29)
- **If the sum of non-deferrable events (Vertical Slice A lying ticks plus marks) already exceeds `FLASH_MAX_PER_S`**: the HUD records `HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN`, defers every deferrable event it can, changes nothing in the Filter's schedule, and the ask to the Filter and accessibility-specialist stands (CF-HS-10). It is unreachable at MVP. (EC-HS-30)

### Prompts, the bar and input

- **If focus changes every 12 ticks along a corridor**: `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` 13 and `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` 20 together mean a plate that has not been stable for 13 ticks never shows (at the earlier 9 ticks a plate stable for 9 and then replaced at 12 popped for 50 ms, so the claim was false at the defaults), no plate shows even for one frame under the 12-tick churn, and no more than three plates start per second; hides are immediate. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* This is the guarantee Interaction's AC-IA-72 assigns to this GDD. (EC-HS-31)
- **If the subject stops being lit mid-channel**: the plate hides the same render frame `prompt_state` goes HIDDEN, but the bar stays until the simulation's abort (at most 5 dark ticks), then plays the one ABORT frame set. The HUD never queries `lit`. (EC-HS-32)
- **If a world abort and the player's release land close together**: the drain is keyed off Interaction's `channel_view.ended_by` (registry `ended_by`), not the Adapter's release read: `PLAYER_CANCEL` draws the drain, `WORLD` draws the snap-plus-notch. `ended_by` is written with the terminal state, so a world abort inside tick t's hook that lands before the Adapter's `cancel_channel` (applied as `IA_CANCEL_NOOP` at t+1) reads `WORLD` and draws the snap-plus-notch; the one-tick race does not arise. (EC-HS-33)
- **If a press is rejected immediately**: any pre-drawn ACK (keycap drop, empty track) is withdrawn within one render frame; nothing shakes, flashes, reddens or explains; the result is identical for every rejection reason; the restored keycap is held Graphite-lit for `BAR_REJECT_HOLD_TICKS` (at most one reject presentation per seat per `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS`) so a rejected press on a shown plate leaves a perceivable trace, and a reject with no plate shown draws nothing. (EC-HS-34) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If the ACK pre-play is drawn and no CONFIRMED arrives within `PREPLAY_MAX_TICKS` 2**: the pre-play is withdrawn; a late CONFIRMED redraws the track at fill 0 and continues from `progress_pm`. (EC-HS-35)
- **If the input device switches while a channel or hold is non-IDLE**: the glyph set is latched and swaps at the next prompt show after `DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS` (cited by name); the bar does not reset; the swap redraws only the keycap region. (EC-HS-36)
- **If the player rebinds `interact` to a key outside the closed token set**: the keycap is the wide `?` token, `hud_unmapped_key_glyph` is counted, and the prompt plate's width budget already reserves the wide case so nothing reflows. (EC-HS-37)
- **If a `tr()` key is missing, or a translated string exceeds the +40 % pseudo-locale budget**: a missing key renders the key and counts `UI_MISSING_KEY`; an over-long string wraps to a second line at `PROMPT_PLATE_MAX_W_PX` 72 and, if two lines still overflow, the plate grows taller and `hud_plate_overflow` is counted; the face is never shrunk or truncated (grow, not clip: orchestrator ruling, see HS-49). (EC-HS-38)
- **If a module is carried and the focused world target is not in the closed carry set**: no prompt is drawn for it; the only prompts are `G . Drop`, `verb_install` at a compatible hardpoint, the pod-console hop plate and the door face's V4 plate *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. (EC-HS-39)
- **If the player holds hold-to-confirm while the game is soloed-paused**: gameplay input is not accepted in the paused context, so `b` and `present_time` cannot diverge in solo; in a co-op OVERLAY pause the kernel runs and `present_time` runs. (EC-HS-40)
- **If the player spams the interact key to flash plates**: the plate cycle is bounded by `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` (at most 3 show-starts per second) and by the arbiter; a held focus shows once and stays. The accepted-press ACK plus force-admitted show-start is rate-gated to one per `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` per seat, is counted, never deferred, and **may overrun a full window**; the overrun is recorded as `HUD_FLASH_OVERRUN`. **Nothing the player does can raise the HUD above `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` through a deferrable or state-display event** (the shared limiter of HS-47 class A coalesces the tool bracket, the glowstick count, the lamp glyph and the TAP relabel); the acknowledgement cues of class B are the stated exception and are counted. (EC-HS-41) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

### Save, restore and life state

- **If the first `RUNNING` frame follows a restore**: readings are drawn directly at the restored values (no settle, no abort frame, no break-notch), every presentation-local counter is cleared (debounce, grid, bar fill and drain, tick phases, flash history, device latch), the mark draws ON at once if the live phase is inside its duty (no start artefact), and `restore_reason` is read as a request that never alters hallucination content from `DEATH_STUB` (it keys only the one record line of HS-46, shown for `UI7_DWELL_S`). (EC-HS-42) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If the seat is restored into DEAD, or dies**: the readings freeze at the last drawn values; there is no death screen, score, text or fade; no mark is drawn after a restore into DEAD; the last light and the cryo-bay lamp are world beats. (EC-HS-43)
- **If `save_status` flips FAILED, WRITTEN and FAILED within one frame**: the notice follows the last committed value; the failure count is Save/Load's; the notice has no animation, so it adds no luminance event beyond its show-start. (EC-HS-44)
- **If the first-frame line and the failed-write notice are shown together**: both stack at the suit panel's edge (the line above the notice slot), each is a deferrable show-start; neither enters the bottom-centre band; the line dwells `UI7_DWELL_S` 8 on `present_time` then cuts, the notice persists until `WRITTEN`. (EC-HS-45)
- **If the Chart Table has already shown the corrupt-ring line under Continue**: the HUD shows nothing for it. (EC-HS-46)
- **If an `ALL`-coalesced dirty notification arrives during a loading hitch (more than `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 keys)**: a full discrete rebuild runs; no dirty bookkeeping survives; readings are pulled fresh every frame regardless. (EC-HS-47)
- **If the pod hop (`TRANSIT_HOLD`) is in progress**: the HUD's layout is unchanged and statuses run in the pod (user ruling D60), so the readings keep updating from `perceived` (the dial settles and the numerals tick on the grid as in RUNNING), remain lie-capable, and a prompt shows only if a lit plate exists; if the seat dies in the pod the HUD enters DEAD like anywhere else and the restore follows HS-53. (EC-HS-48) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

### Hallucination interplay

- **If the scanner slot receives a lying record (Vertical Slice A) while no scanner is equipped**: the slot is not drawn and no record is requested; the gleam draws only while its host reading draws. (EC-HS-49)
- **If a `frame_override` names an atlas region the HUD's table lacks**: the reading draws its honest frame and `hud_unknown_override` is counted (the Filter's own honest-fallback rule). (EC-HS-50)
- **If the honest O2 numeral and a lying wall gauge disagree at MVP-equivalent fixtures**: the HUD draws `perceived(suit.o2).displayed_value`, which equals the compartment's `p_o2`; that disagreement is the designed second-instrument beat, not a defect, and the HUD never flags which one is wrong. (EC-HS-51)
- **If a readout is lie-capable but `READING_LIE_STAGE` is "never" (all keys at MVP) and `SCANNER_LIE_STAGE` is never**: the slots are inert; a stub Filter drives them only in tests; a build with a non-"never" value is a Vertical Slice A build and out of this GDD's MVP scope. (EC-HS-52)
- **If the player reads the reserve numeral during a seam leak**: the numeral is nominal-draw and intentionally optimistic; the true time left is shorter; the seam jet and the hiss are the falsifier. This is documented, not a defect, provided the Suit's cue-margin check holds (HS-17, OQ-HS-60); whether players read it as a "lie" is a playtest question (OQ-HS-58). (EC-HS-53)

### Co-op hooks (Vertical Slice B, reserved)

- **If a co-op OVERLAY panel covers the bottom-right hand rail**: the HUD is correct; no rule depends on the rail being visible and nothing is drawn elsewhere to compensate. (EC-HS-54)
- **If more than one seat exists**: only the local seat's `perceived`, `view`, `focus_view` and `channel_view` are drawn; addressing is by PlayerId, never `peer_id`; another seat's bar is never drawn; the `crew_strip`, `chat`, `caption` and `own_identity` anchors draw nothing at MVP. (EC-HS-55)

## Dependencies

The index's Systems Enumeration row 21 lists four dependencies: Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Suit & Equipment, Perception Filter and Player Controller & Input. All four exist and are Designed. The table below adds every other system the HUD reads or that reads the HUD, because the design-docs rule requires dependencies to be bidirectional. **Hard** = the HUD cannot be built or tested without the interface; **Soft** = the HUD degrades gracefully if the interface is late or different. "Status" is the status of the other GDD at 2026-10-26: **designed**, **in design**, **in flight (draft on disk)**, or **undesigned-provisional** (no file; the assumption is labelled and repeated in Open Questions).

### Dependency table

| System | Direction | Strength | Interface | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera | upstream | **Hard** | layer 2 in the root viewport, `H`, the picture rect, the safe margin, `world_to_hud`, `present_time`, the pause state, the flash invariants (PR-13), `cap_px` | designed |
| Suit & Equipment | upstream | **Hard** | `view(PlayerId)` non-reading fields, `tool_in_hand`, `stowed_count`, `carried_instance`, `tool_charge`, dirty key `S/<PlayerId>`; requests: `suit.alarm` (accepted at MVP, user ruling D89 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*), the four inner warmth edges as named reads (the axis is published, SE-29), the seam-leak cue-margin check (OQ-HS-60) | designed |
| Perception Filter | upstream | **Hard** | `perceived(SUIT_KEY(k), PlayerId)` x 5, `perceived(SCANNER, PlayerId)`, the `Perceived` record, the three published scalars; `perceived(suit.alarm)` as a sixth TRUTH key, **not yet defined by the Filter (OQ-HS-79; provisional)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | designed |
| Interaction System | upstream | **Hard** (prompts and bar) | `focus_view(seat)`, `channel_view(seat)` | designed |
| Player Controller & Input | upstream | **Hard** | the glyph token read, the device latch, the hold fraction and pressed action *(Controller ASKs)*; `confirm_view.give_up_live` and `glyph_view(seat, give_up)` for the `give_up` plate (HS-42(b)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | designed |
| Save/Load & Versioned Format | upstream | Soft | `save_status`, the fallback-load flag, `restore_reason` (request) | designed |
| Command & Simulation Layer (kernel) | upstream | **Hard** | `PRESENTATION` subscription, committed `T`, `tick_alpha`, notifications that carry no state, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | designed |
| Life-Support Graph | upstream | Soft | the O2 band edges (`O2_BAND_ANOXIC_PP`, `HYPOXIA_PP`, `O2_BAND_THIN_PP`, registry); no read of Graph state; the body-scope rule behind `suit.alarm` (Graph LS-13 (c), `alarm_sections()`, intersected inside the Suit, SE-29) is the Graph's and Suit's, not a HUD read *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | designed |
| Adaptive Audio & Tells | lateral | Soft | the pulse-clock contract (AAV-9), the `ui` bus, sound-request ids (dormant), captions slot (AAV-38) | designed |
| Sanity & Hallucination Rules | upstream, indirect | Soft | `READING_LIE_STAGE` (read only through the Filter); the rail-count honesty confirmation; the SN-19 wording change for the mark's truth class (HS-30, OQ-HS-61) | designed |
| Boarding & Salvage; Modular Ship Assembly; Item & Module Database; Ship Section & Layout Data Model | upstream, content | Soft | prompt-grammar tokens, the closed carry-prompt set, tool silhouettes and `name_key`, `subject_anchors` (through `anchor_o`) | designed |
| Chart Table, Web Map & Menus | lateral | Soft | shared component grammar (`design/ux/interaction-patterns.md`), the UI-7 handoff, co-op OVERLAY coverage of the rail | designed |
| Avatar Presenter *(no GDD; R9)* | upstream, indirect | Soft | the 16 pre-drawn chest-plate swivel step frames and the avatar's self-visibility with the lamp off, which carry the lamp-off aim cue (HS-52, UI Requirements item 10); the HUD draws nothing for it | **undesigned — provisional contract** (the interface assumed: 16 aim-step frames published by the Controller's aim step, drawn in the world picture, no light, no glow); repeated in OQ-HS-63 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R9)* |
| Ship Body & Interior Physics | lateral | none (push-back) | no room readout (HS-10) | designed |

### What each neighbour must repeat in its own GDD (bidirectionality)

- **Suit & Equipment:** the five readings are drawn from `perceived`, not from `view()`'s reading fields (SE-29 and its `true_value` callers list rewritten); the mark sits "beside the breath dial's reading", not beside an O2 glyph on the strip; OQ-41 is answered by HS-12 (the strip shows the reserve while RESERVE; the ambient numeral is demoted to the panel's ambient slot) and the Suit's text citing a nonexistent "HUD HS-65" is corrected to HS-22 / D90 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the mark cap is six (five suit rungs plus the accepted one-bit `suit.alarm`, TRUTH-class, never lie-capable: user ruling D89); the MVP kit has no suppressant and the rail's slot 4 is empty (X6); statuses run in the pod (D60) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; every seam leak carries a body or world cue (jet, hiss, frost) before the reserve error exceeds a stated margin (OQ-HS-60); the warmth band axis and edge values are published by the Suit; the suppressant-at-0 mark is a diagonal cut by default (dormant until the tool returns).
- **Perception Filter:** the HUD draws the gleam on its own glass from `leak_amplitude_pm` and `leak_modes`; the Filter's gleam-seam question is closed from the HUD side; the Filter supplies the mark state for a lying reading and states PF-17 for HUD dials; it states the unit and root-viewport applicability of `LEAK_MAX_CH`; the flash unit is ruled by the user (`ceil(n_transitions / 2)`, D91 (ii)): it adopts it, adopts the area weighting for its lying ticks or states that they stay strictly counted, carries `suit.alarm` as a one-bit TRUTH key with its lie stage pinned `never`, and gives the HUD's honest numerals and mark a place in its `N_r` arithmetic *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **Render Pipeline:** the pause state table keeps the HUD frozen except that an active mark is held ON (HS-23); layers 3-4 (not 2-4) run on process time; PR-8 item 6 allows reading `T` and `tick_alpha` for clock-locked phases; the "only writer of HUD anchors" is "only caller of `HudLayout`"; the strip cap is defined at the 1080p reference and the Deck overage is accepted; the letterbox hairline is a layer-1 element excluded from the HUD count.
- **Player Controller:** publishes `confirm_view` (or `input_view`, one name; with `mode` and `armed`) and the closed glyph token type (largely done, pci:953); registers `DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS` (done); the bar's world-abort visual is Interaction's snap-plus-notch; the channel bar lives on the prompt plate; there is no cursor in `gameplay`; publishes `give_up_live` in `confirm_view` and `glyph_view(seat, give_up)`; retires `input_ignored_view` and the first-use captions from its own GDD (PC-2 non-audio counterpart, AC-PCI-024); drops "draws nothing" cross-references now done here. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
- **Interaction:** the HUD-side knobs it proposed are HUD-owned; its Open Question 1 is answered *adopted*; the HUD does not read `abort_cause`.
- **Save/Load:** registers `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT`; the notice shape and placement are the HUD's; the first-frame line is the HUD's unless the Chart Table shows it.
- **Life-Support Graph:** a dependency row naming the HUD (done, lsg:282 and lsg:881); registers the O2 band edges (done: `O2_BAND_ANOXIC_PP`, `O2_BAND_THIN_PP`; `ALARM_SEV_MIN` is already registered by the Graph and cited here only).
- **Sanity & Hallucination Rules:** SN-19 (and its truth-class rows) says the ship-alarm and world-housing warning marks never lie and that a suit-rung mark inherits its beside-reading's class; rail counts are honest anchors; the scanner reading content.
- **Chart Table:** the pause panel states that the HUD behind it is frozen except for an active mark held ON, and a minimum pause re-toggle interval (OQ-HS-62); its HUD row (ct:542) already names this GDD and cites `design/ux/interaction-patterns.md` for the shared components; its settings page ships `reduce_flashing` ON at first launch (user ruling D79) and its Delete hold is 1.6 s (D81, default applied confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; no other refresh owed.
- **Ship Body:** its room-and-compartment readout row is re-pointed to "no HUD surface".
- **In-World Instruments & Inspect View:** the card's honest numeral rides the HUD's `HONEST_TICK_MS` grid and requests slots from `hud_flash_budget`; it cites `HONEST_TICK_MS` as range 400-500 with the derived floor = `hud_flash_budget`'s `honest_tick_floor_ms` (400 at `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` 120 under the leading-edge unit), already stated at iw:267, iw:638, iw:1123 **(refresh owed: the value is now pinned at 500, anchored to the committed tick as `T mod 30 == 0`, and the registry's grid entry should be restated in ticks; the pin is ratified by user ruling D91 (iii))** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the HUD repeats "no sanity surface", "the HUD may not read `node()` / `edge()`" and the at-rest-only scope of the non-Ground cap; it owns `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` (16) and its own `INSTR_SETTLE_MS` / `INSTR_SETTLE_ZETA` (the HUD uses `HUD_SETTLE_MS` alone, with a critically damped response and no damping knob, D.6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **Debug Overlay (True Values):** repeats that no HUD node hosts or parents an Overlay node and that the HUD never calls `true_value`; the Overlay consumes `hud_debug_snapshot()` as a read-only channel (do:417, do:777) carrying `H`, the picture rect, the plate rectangles, the mark phase, the last model and the nine AC-HS-122 counters, with no `k` (read `k` from the Pipeline); the HUD exposes no other overlay hooks.

## Tuning Knobs

Every HUD-owned value is a `[PH]` placeholder until playtested and lives in a data file (`assets/data/ui/hud_tuning`), never in code. A ★ marks a registry candidate: a cross-system fact another GDD reads or constrains; the rest are private to the HUD and are not registered. The HUD owns **no** gameplay balance value: no knob here changes a simulation value, a rung, a hold, a timer or a lie's content; the knobs tune how a value is drawn and how fast the screen is allowed to change. Registry entries are written at Phase 5 from this text.

### HUD-owned knobs

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` (adopted from ia:1083) | **13** (was 9) | ticks | **13-20** (was 6-20) | The prompt feels sluggish (13 ticks = 217 ms); a late prompt omits a hint but is never wrong; an accepted press force-admits the plate regardless (HS-37) | Below 13 (the 12-tick corridor churn) a plate that had been stable shows for the last few ticks before the next focus replaces it: a 50 ms pop at 9; AC-IA-72 fails | `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS`, Interaction's focus churn (12 ticks, cited) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; registry request)* |
| ★ `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` | 20 | ticks | 20-40 (floor `ceil_div(TICK_RATE, FLASH_MAX_PER_S)` = 20) | Slow re-offer after a hide | Below 20 exceeds 3 show-starts per second and breaks HR-13 | `FLASH_MAX_PER_S`, D.2, D.18 |
| `PREPLAY_MAX_TICKS` | 2 | ticks | 1-6 (at least the round trip at Vertical Slice B) | A false acknowledgement lingers after a reject | The press feels dead; the ACK is withdrawn before a slow CONFIRMED | Controller press read, Interaction result latency |
| ★ `BAR_LEN_SHORT_PX` | 16 | HUD px | 12-64, ordered S < M < L | Bar crowds the plate | Unreadable steps (3 ticks per px at `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` 0.8) | `length_class`, D.19, hold-to-confirm |
| ★ `BAR_LEN_MEDIUM_PX` | 24 | HUD px | 12-64, ordered | As above | As above | `length_class`, D.19 |
| ★ `BAR_LEN_LONG_PX` | 40 | HUD px | 12-64, ordered | The plate grows past `PROMPT_PLATE_MAX_W_PX` | Fill steps exceed 10 ticks per px and stall visibly | `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` 240, weld 360 ticks |
