| OQ-HS-57 | Does the at-rest HUD (about 1,890 of 2,304 non-Ground px at MVP, about 2,060 once the suppressant returns) compete with the cone on the Deck? *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the MVP figure is 1,890, the allocation table)* | agent (art-director) | playtest |
| OQ-HS-58 | Do players read the reserve numeral's optimism under a seam leak (it is nominal draw) as a "lie", when the jet and hiss are the falsifier? If a meaningful share do (propose: 2 or more of the first 8 testers say the reserve "lied"), the Suit should model the leak in the reserve reading (OQ-HS-60). **Added probe** `[PH]` *(re-review: nothing asked which of two falling unlabelled numerals is the player's clock)*: once the strip shows the reserve and the panel shows the ambient numeral (HS-12), ask each tester at the valve click "which number is your time left?"; pass is at least 7 of 8 pointing at the strip's numeral within 5 s with no label, and at least 7 of 8 reading the tank tick and warn notch (not the digits) when asked how they know; a failure routes to the art-director (the discriminator) rather than to the Suit. | agent (qa-lead) | playtest |
| OQ-HS-59 | In the door-breach scenario, is a valve prompt ever perceived as late? Measured latencies *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the typical and the worst case are separated)*: **typically 13 ticks (217 ms)** from stable focus; **at most 43 ticks (717 ms) in STRICT when a third counted event is in the window** (the one-event breath-source swap at the valve click, a hue cut, an earlier ACK, or the orphan hide of the previous plate; HS-45, AC-HS-144), at most 73 ticks at the `MAX_DEFER_TICKS` bound; 0 after a press on an ENABLED or NEEDS_TOOL subject. If 2 or more of the first 8 testers say a prompt was late, the levers that **leave every user ruling intact** are, in order: (1) lower `MAX_DEFER_TICKS` toward 30 (bounds the wait at 43 ticks); (2) draw the breath-source swap on the next grid boundary (at most 30 ticks later) so the swap and the boundary are one event instead of two, which removes the commonest third event (a change to HS-12 (3), not to a ruling); (3) shrink or re-weight the plates so more show-starts are exempt in WEIGHTED (`FLASH_AREA_EXEMPT_PM` is capped at 100 and the plate sizes are the UX spec's); `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` is already at its floor of 13 and `HONEST_TICK_MS` is pinned at 500, so neither is a lever, and the counting unit is ruled (D91 (ii)); the priority order is not changed. The first-launch default is STRICT with a solid mark (D79), so the figures above are the first-launch experience. | agent (qa-lead, creative-director) | playtest |
| OQ-HS-84 | **Restore comprehension (HS-53, re-review 2026-10-27; the ruling D15 / D84 is not re-opened).** P5 ("you lost the interval, not the lesson") depends on the player knowing they died. The only trace is one plain peripheral line after a hard cut, which may read as a crash. Measure it beside OQ-HS-56: after a death restore in the first playtest, ask each tester "why did the game just restart?" **Pass bar** `[PH]`: at least 7 of 8 testers can say they died and that the game restored an earlier record. A failure is **not** fixed by HUD text: any fix is owned by the world (a diegetic beat) or by Save/Load, never by a HUD death message. | agent (qa-lead, creative-director) | playtest |
| OQ-HS-86 | **Comprehension probes with numbers (re-review 2026-10-27).** HS-DT-1's validation said "most players" with no N. Give it: with sound, at least 6 of 8 testers asked "what told you the air was going?" answer with the breath or the valve click before the numeral; muted, at least 4 of 8 (the muted arm may not carry the click), judged against its own bar, and a muted failure with a sound pass is an audio-cue gap, never a HUD defect. Also probe HS-12 and HS-17 (OQ-HS-58's added probe) and the solid-mark time to notice (OQ-HS-54). | agent (qa-lead) | playtest |

### Art bible amendment text (for the art-director to apply; this GDD edits no art-bible file)

1. **HR-3 (add):** "The suit-reserve reading (whole seconds beside the reserve glyph) is part of the closed screen-space reading set, and while the suit breathes from its reserve the critical strip's breath dial shows the reserve (whole seconds, a reserve engraving lit on the dial face) in place of the ambient O2, which moves to the suit panel." *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: HS-12, answering Suit OQ-41)*
2. **HR-12 (read as):** "Every critical **O2** needle on the critical strip has a numeral beside it. Integrity and warmth are zone-band dials without a numeral, because a health number and a body-temperature number are Suit anti-requirements."
3. **7.2 (add):** "A suit reading without a loop (warmth) uses a Paper White silhouette with no hue: a flat chamfered plate with three horizontal fin hairlines. It is never the coolant collar, a thermometer or a flame." And: "The 16 x 16 glyph cell includes its 1-px clearance; the live silhouette is 14 x 14." And: "The warning mark lives beside the reading it concerns (strip or panel); the strip slot is the O2 (breath) mark, and the ship-alarm mark has its own slot on the suit panel (a second 16-px slot would take the strip past its 76-px cap, D.1)." *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
4. **7.2 (only if chosen):** a "state tick, not a glyph" exception for an 8 x 8 empty-tool mark. Default: none (the diagonal cut across the 16 x 16 glyph needs no exception).
5. **7.3 (replace "numerals tick one step per real change"):** "Numerals update on a shared hold grid (`HONEST_TICK_MS`, pinned at 500 ms and anchored to the committed tick); each update shows the current value as a cut, never a tween." (Ruled by the user, D91 (i).) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
6. **3.4 (wording note):** "Heavy" for a frame means mass (chamfer, rivets, plate area), not stroke width.
7. **Critical-strip cap:** define "12 % x 5 %" at 100 % UI scale at the 1080p reference; a higher `H` knowingly exceeds it, the player's explicit accessibility trade.
8. **7.4 opacity vs HR-2:** no HUD opacity setting at MVP; any later floor is measured over the beam core.
9. **7.2 (narrow; `suit.alarm` accepted by user ruling D89)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: "The ship-alarm warning mark is TRUTH-class: it is driven only by the Graph's `alarm_eligible` through the one-bit `suit.alarm` reading, and a lie may neither show, suppress nor tint it. A suit-rung warning mark sits beside its reading and inherits that reading's class." (HS-25, HS-30)
10. **7.3 / HR-13 (flash accounting, user ruling D91 (ii) and (iv)):** "A flash is one pair of opposing transitions, counted `ceil(n_transitions / 2)`. The ceiling of 3 per second is weighted by the area a flash covers; sub-threshold digit swaps and prompt show-starts are exempt, and the ceiling is held strictly for the Alarm Red mark and under reduce flashing." *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
